# Parachord Android — Development Guide

## Core Principle

**Always match the desktop app's approach.** The desktop Parachord app (Electron + React 18 + Tailwind) at https://github.com/Parachord/parachord is the source of truth. Before implementing any feature, check how the desktop app does it first. We spent a lot of time refining those approaches and should not reinvent anything.

**When told a feature should "work like desktop":** Do not make assumptions or do a partial implementation. Study the desktop implementation in detail first — read the relevant code in `app.js`, understand the data flow, the UI, the settings, and the edge cases. Ask follow-up questions if anything is unclear. The goal is feature parity, not a rough approximation. Half-implementations that miss settings, toggles, or data sources the desktop includes are worse than no implementation.

Desktop app structure: single `app.js` (~57,700 lines), `.axe` resolver plugins, `index.html` with CSS design tokens.

**Build every feature for both mobile platforms, as shared code.** When adding a capability or feature, implement it for Android AND iOS in the same change, never one platform only, and never two separate implementations of the same logic. Put the logic in `shared/commonMain` (models, rules, decisions, parsing, API clients) and keep each platform layer to what genuinely can't be shared: UI, the playback engine, OS integration. Where something must exist per platform (a page a WebView hosts, a bundled script), keep one source of truth and check the copies stay identical (e.g. the SoundCloud widget page, #495, guarded by a unit test). If one platform truly can't do it yet, say so in the PR and file the follow-up; don't leave the gap silent.

**Every review checks that as much as possible is shared code.** This holds for our own PRs and contributors' alike, and for plans and issues as well as code. For each piece of logic a change puts in `app/` or `iosApp/` (or `shared/iosMain` / `androidMain`), ask whether the other platform needs the same decision. That includes a rule, a threshold, a message, parsing, a state machine, or which source or engine to pick. If it does, the logic belongs in `shared/commonMain` and the platform should only call it. Flag it in the review even when the other platform hasn't been built yet: it's cheaper to move now than to write the second copy later. Also check the other side:
- When a change adds shared code, is every platform-side piece the other app will need listed in the PR or its follow-up issue? Bundled copies, settings, gating lists and icons are easy to miss.
- Does any platform code duplicate something already in `shared/`?

Example: the Subsonic review (#671 / #670) found the shared engine sound, but iOS's plan missed `active_resolvers` gating, the bundled `.axe` and the resolver order.

**When a fix or behavior change may apply to desktop too, ask whether to file a desktop ticket.** Parity runs both ways: a bug fixed here often exists in the same code path on desktop, and a behavior change here leaves the platforms apart unless desktop follows. Whenever you fix a bug or change behavior, check whether desktop has the same code or the same gap (read `app.js`, not memory). If it might, ask the user whether to write a ticket on [Parachord/parachord](https://github.com/Parachord/parachord/issues) before you finish. Don't file one without asking. A good ticket names the desktop code path, what mobile changed and why, and links the mobile PR. Examples: the N-way missingStreak gate (#469 → desktop parachord#1163), and the ListenBrainz friend Top Albums artwork, where desktop runs one MusicBrainz search per album but the stats payload already carries `caa_release_mbid`.

**When a change materially alters how a feature works, update its documentation in the same change.** A material change is one a reader of the docs would get wrong without the update: what the feature does, the rules it follows, the services it calls, its limits, or what the user sees. Before you finish, search for the feature's name and the code you changed (`grep -rn -i "<feature>" --include='*.md' .`), then fix every place that now describes old behavior. That means this file's section for the feature, `iosApp/AGENTS.md`, the README feature list, and KDoc or comments next to the changed code. Write what is true now, not a changelog. Leave dated plans and design records in `docs/plans/` and `docs/design/` as they are, since they record what was intended at the time. Example: the Spinoff similar-artists fallback (#535) updated this file's Spinoff section, the iOS guide and the README together.

## Architecture Patterns (from Desktop)

### Metadata Providers — Cascading Lookup

The desktop uses a cascading provider pattern. Each provider has a specialty:

| Provider | Priority | Specialty | Auth Required |
|----------|----------|-----------|---------------|
| MusicBrainz | 0 (first) | MBIDs, structured data, **discography, tracklists** | No |
| Last.fm | 10 | Images, bios, similar artists, tags | API key only |
| Spotify | 20 (last) | Album art, preview URLs, Spotify IDs | OAuth token |

**MusicBrainz is the primary source for discography and album tracklists.** Last.fm and Spotify supplement with images and additional metadata. Results from all providers are merged — later providers fill in gaps from earlier ones.

**One provider list, built once (#595).** `MetadataProviders` (`shared/.../metadata/MetadataWiring.kt`) builds the cascade's providers for both apps: MusicBrainz, Wikipedia, Last.fm, Discogs, Spotify, then the Apple Music catalog's artist art (25). `withoutSpotify()` is the list for iOS's background enrichment service. The cascade's `enrichAlbumArtwork` step is the shared `upgradeCoverArtArchiveArtwork`, which swaps a Cover Art Archive cover for the iTunes 600x600 art. Which providers the user switched off still comes from two different settings: Android reads `disabled_meta_providers`, iOS reads `disabled_plugins`. On both, Spotify and Apple Music are asked only while their plug-ins are on (shared `metadataProvidersOff`, #701, desktop parachord#1466): off when the plug-in is disabled, or when the active-resolver list is set and doesn't name it. A saved Spotify sign-in outlives turning Spotify off, so a token check alone kept sending searches and artist photos to Spotify and extended its rate-limit pause.

**Top lists are cached a day (#595).** History's and a friend's top tracks, albums and artists go through the shared `TopListsCache`, on both apps. A list fetched less than 24 hours ago shows without a request. A failed or empty refresh shows the last list. Recently Played isn't cached.

**Which edition an album opens (#560, desktop parachord#1290).** A release group holds every edition. The album page (when a discography or search has given the release group) and `parachord://` album links by MBID ask for up to 25 official releases with their track lists in one request (`MusicBrainzClient.albumReleaseOfGroup`) and open the one shared `pickRelease` chooses: digital-only releases first (what streaming services carry), then the most common track count (the standard edition, so not Abbey Road's 40-track deluxe), then the earliest date, undated last. Taking the first release MusicBrainz listed opened George Michael's "The Faith Tour" with 9 of 18 tracks (one CD). A port of desktop's `release-pick.js`; change it there first. Rule 3 (#833, desktop parachord#1654): digital releases come first only while one of them lists its tracks, since days after release a group can hold empty digital placeholders while a CD has the list. The release-search path (no release group known: chart albums, Critical Darlings) asks for 25 hits and opens the top hit's album as `pickRelease` would pick it among them (`orderReleaseSearchHits`; search hits carry each medium's format and track count, so no request is added), never another album: the top hit alone opened an empty placeholder edition of "Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album". **The album page's type badge is MusicBrainz's** when it has one, in every merge (`musicBrainzReleaseType`): Spotify's `album_type` calls every EP a "single", and it won whenever its result had at least as many tracks, so Phoebe Bridgers' "Anything" read "EP" on Home and "Single" on its page. Fresh Drops hands its release groups and types to the album lookup (`MetadataService.rememberReleaseGroups`, `KnownReleaseGroup`), so a card opens its own release group, as desktop does, and keeps its type even when MusicBrainz doesn't answer (`MusicBrainzProvider.knownReleaseType`). **A compilation's tracks keep their own artists on every path (#834, desktop parachord#1652).** MusicBrainz's list credits each track (`MbTrack.artistName`); a plugin catalog's list (`AlbumCatalog`, the `lookupAlbum` answer that often lands first) carries each track's `artist`, and `toAlbumDetail` keeps it only when none of its credited names is one of the album's (`ownCatalogTrackArtist`), else the album's artist, so a single-artist album's track keys don't change. Stamping "Various Artists" on every row resolved each track under a name no service credits it to, which scored below the 0.60 floor.

### Resolver Scoring — Two-Tier System

Source selection uses priority-first, confidence-second sorting:

1. **Minimum confidence filter** — sources below `MIN_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` (0.60) are discarded. `scoreConfidence()` returns 0.95 only when BOTH title and artist substring-match the target; single-axis matches (wrong artist, or wrong title) collapse to 0.50 and get filtered. This is the equivalent of desktop's `validateResolvedTrack` gate (`app.js:14257-14267`) — desktop never adds a single-axis match to `track.sources`, so it never reaches the priority sort. Without this gate, a wrong-song Apple Music result at 0.85 (title matched, artist didn't) would outrank a correct local file at 0.95 because applemusic sits above localfiles in the priority order and confidence is only a tiebreaker WITHIN the same priority tier.
   **Identity tier first (#402, desktop parachord#1056).** Before the name gate, `scoreConfidence` returns **1.0** when the target and the source carry the same ISRC (after `validateIsrc`) or the same recording MBID (case-insensitive). A missing, malformed or different id falls through to the name gate unchanged, so it only ever upgrades: a rip tagged "Various Artists / Track 1" with the right ISRC now survives the 0.60 floor. Callers pass the target's `targetIsrc` / `targetMbid` (Android `ResolverManager.resolve`/`resolveWithHints`, iOS `IosContainer.resolveSources`); sources supply theirs via `ResolvedSource.isrc` / `recordingMbid`. `selectBestMatch` has the matching picker tiers, as desktop's `findBestMatch`: exact MBID, exact ISRC, exact normalized title + artist, a name match that isn't another version (`isOtherVersion`: a live/remix/cover/karaoke/medley word the target lacks, desktop parachord#1115, or a title joining songs with a slash, `joinsSongs`, #582 / desktop parachord#1306: a Bandcamp "Her Majesty" was a nine-song medley), then any name match. In the exact tier a trailing remaster label on the result ("- 2016 Remaster", "(Remastered 2009)", `REMASTER_LABEL`) doesn't count against it unless the request names one: Spotify labels remastered studio tracks while a live album or an outtakes set carries the bare title, so the exact tier picked Ramones' live "Judy Is a Punk" from "It's Alive" over the studio "Judy Is a Punk - 2016 Remaster" ranked above it, and "Her Majesty - Takes 1-3" over "Her Majesty - Remastered 2009". Desktop's `pickTrackMatch` has the old rule ([parachord#1304](https://github.com/Parachord/parachord/issues/1304)). Native Spotify (`SpotifyClient.searchTrack`) picks among its 5 results through it too, via the `MatchTarget` the coordinator passes; desktop's Spotify plugin still takes the top result.
   **Multi-artist credits (#522, desktop parachord#1253).** Services join one credit differently, and `normalizeStr` keeps the words, so "Erykah Badu and The Alchemist" failed containment against "Erykah Badu & The Alchemist". So each axis has one narrow fallback after containment, in `scoreConfidence` and `selectBestMatch` alike (a selected candidate must score 0.95). `artistsMatch`: split on `,`, `&`, ` and `, ` x `, `feat`/`ft`/`featuring`, then every name on one side must be credited on the other, whole and in any order, so "Simon & Schuster" isn't "Simon & Garfunkel". `titlesMatch`: equal once a bracketed trailing credit comes off both sides; equality, not containment, so "Hello (feat. A)" can't reach "Hello Goodbye". `+`, `/`, `vs`, `with` and `×` are deliberately not joins: change that on desktop first. `ArtistCreditMatchTest` holds desktop's vectors, the cross-platform contract.
2. **Resolver priority** — user-configurable ordering (lower index = higher priority)
3. **Confidence score** — tiebreaker within same priority (0.0–1.0, default 0.9)

A Spotify result at 70% confidence beats a SoundCloud result at 95% when Spotify is ranked higher. But a Spotify result at 50% confidence (no match) is filtered out entirely, allowing the higher-confidence SoundCloud or local file result to win.

**Canonical resolver order (default):**
```
spotify > applemusic > bandcamp > soundcloud > localfiles > subsonic > youtube
```

### Resolver Coordinator — Shared Orchestration (#210)

Resolver *orchestration* is one `commonMain` class, `ResolverCoordinator` (`shared/.../resolver/ResolverCoordinator.kt`) — both platforms inherit a single fan-out / gating / re-score / ranking path so a resolution-logic change lives in ONE place.

**What the coordinator owns:** the parallel fan-out, the native **Spotify** branch (an injected `suspend (query) -> ResolvedSource?` lambda — the platform binding closes over the Spotify token-gate + `SpotifyClient.searchTrack`), gating (via the shared `ResolverGating` helpers), the re-score (`scoreConfidence` when both target fields are present), and ranking. It exposes three entry points:
- `resolveScored(query, targetTitle?, targetArtist?, album?, excludeResolvers)` — fan out + re-score, **unranked / unfloored**. Android's `resolve()` delegates here (the 0.60 floor + priority sort run downstream via `ResolverScoring.selectBest`).
- `resolveRanked(query, …, preferredResolver?)` — `selectRanked(resolveScored(...))`: drops sub-floor (< 0.60) sources, orders by priority. iOS's `resolveSources(artist, title, album)` delegates here.
- `resolveSingle(resolverId, query, targetTitle?, targetArtist?, album?)` — additive re-resolution of ONE resolver (used when the user enables a resolver after a track was cached). Dispatches: `spotify` → the lambda; an id in `runtime.nativeResolverIds` → `resolveNative`; otherwise, ONLY if a resolve-capable plugin with that id is loaded → `resolveAxe`, else null. Re-scored, returned only if `!noMatch && confidence >= 0.60`. Throws when the resolver couldn't answer (#566).
- `resolveScoredReport` / `resolveRankedWithHintsReport` — the same, plus which resolvers were asked and which couldn't answer (`ResolveReport`, #566; see "A resolver that couldn't answer is not a miss"). `onlyResolvers` limits a run to a retry of the failed ones.

**`ResolverRuntime` interface** (`shared/.../resolver/ResolverRuntime.kt`) — the per-platform native non-Spotify resolvers + `.axe` execution that genuinely can't be shared:
- **Android** `AndroidResolverRuntime` (`app/.../resolver/AndroidResolverRuntime.kt`): Apple Music (the shared catalog search, then the plugin, then iTunes), local files; `.axe` via `PluginManager.resolve`. `nativeResolverIds = {applemusic, localfiles}`. SoundCloud is `soundcloud.axe` on both platforms (#598).
- **iOS** `IosResolverRuntime` (`shared/iosMain/.../IosResolverRuntime.kt`): Apple Music via the shared catalog search; `.axe` via the JavaScriptCore unique-key polling workaround. `nativeResolverIds = {applemusic}`.

Android's `ResolverManager` keeps its richer `resolveWithHints` wrapper (Spotify ID verify + 429-keep-the-badge fallback + cascade-artwork merge + `excludeResolvers`) **on top of** the coordinator — it calls `coordinator.resolveScored(..., excludeResolvers)`. iOS's `IosResolverCoordinator` is now a thin delegate (just `pluginManager.ensureInitialized()` + forward to `resolveRanked` / `resolveSingle`).

**Native-gating divergence (behavior-preserving, D3).** The coordinator's native gate is `(active.isEmpty() || id in active) && id !in exclude`. Android never populates `active_resolvers` (gates natives on connection-state, so `active.isEmpty()` is always true → exclude-only), while iOS + desktop populate `active_resolvers` and remove a resolver on disable or disconnect, so `id in active` is the real gate there. Desktop seeds it with the resolvers that need no sign-in (`bandcamp`, `localfiles`); iOS seeds those plus any already connected. Spotify, SoundCloud and Apple Music join when connected (iOS #533). The unified gate preserves both. Converging Android onto `active_resolvers` (Finding 1) is a tracked follow-up — **OUT OF SCOPE** for #210 (it changes user-visible Android settings behavior).

### Per-Resolver Volume Offsets (dB)

```
spotify:     0    applemusic:  0    localfiles:  0    subsonic: 0
soundcloud:  0    bandcamp:   -3    youtube:    -6
```

**Where and how (desktop's Settings → General → Volume Normalization, after Playback Quality; both apps).** One slider per enabled plug-in in resolver order, -12 to +6 dB (`ResolverVolume`). **Whether a row works is decided by the plug-in's capabilities, never its id** (`ResolverVolume.controllability`, desktop parachord#1522): `capabilities.systemVolume` (Apple Music) is off with "System vol"; neither `stream` nor `embedPlayer` is off with "Browser". **iOS also turns off `embedPlayer` plug-ins (SoundCloud, Bandcamp) as "System vol":** their pages set an HTML media element's `volume`, which WebKit on iOS ignores; Android's WebView applies it, so Bandcamp and SoundCloud sliders work there. **A changed offset applies at once** to the playing track when it came from that resolver (`ResolverVolume.reappliesLive`; desktop's use-volume.js): ExoPlayer's gain (never the silent keep-alive loop), the embed page, Spotify's Connect volume (honoring `rateLimitRemainingMs()`), after 250 ms of quiet so a drag sends one request (Android `PlaybackController.onResolverVolumeOffsetChanged`, iOS `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.reapplyVolumeLive`). A play applies it at its start as before.

### Playback Routing

Each resolver type routes to a specific playback mechanism:

- **Spotify** → Web API (Spotify Connect, controls Spotify app on-device via HTTP)
- **SoundCloud** → SoundCloud's embeddable widget in a hidden WebView (external playback, like Apple Music); iOS the same in a `WKWebView`
- **Local files** → ExoPlayer with content:// URI
- **Bandcamp** → Bandcamp's embedded player as the top-level page of a hidden WebView (external playback); iOS the same in a `WKWebView`
- **Direct streams** → ExoPlayer with HTTP URL
- **A plugin-supplied Widevine stream** (`capabilities.widevine` + `getPlaybackManifest` + `relayLicense`: Amazon Music) → ExoPlayer playing the plugin's DASH manifest with Widevine licences relayed through the plugin (Android only; see "Plugin-supplied Widevine playback"); if its DRM stream doesn't start and the plugin implements `getStreamUrl`, its plain stream through ExoPlayer instead
- **A plugin's `getStreamUrl` stream** (`capabilities.html5Stream`: Subsonic; on iOS also a Widevine plugin's plain stream: Amazon Music's purchases) → ExoPlayer on Android, AVPlayer on iOS, with a URL the plugin builds at play time (see "Plugin settings + the html5Stream engine")

### Queue advance and repeat (shared)

One rule set moves the queue on both platforms: `QueueAdvancePolicy` (`shared/.../playback/QueueAdvancePolicy.kt`). Android's `PlaybackController.skipNextInternal(trigger)` and iOS's `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.advance(_:)` both ask `advanceAction(trigger, repeatMode, hasNext, hasCurrent, playedSinceWrap)` and do what it says; neither keeps its own repeat logic.

- **Triggers.** `TRACK_ENDED`, `NEXT_PRESSED`, `SKIPPED_UNPLAYABLE`. A spinoff pool is asked first and isn't governed by repeat.
- **Actions.** `PLAY_NEXT`, `REPLAY_CURRENT` (repeat one, on a track end only; Next always moves on), `WRAP_TO_START` (repeat all at the end of the queue, on a track end, Next, or an unplayable last track), `STOP_AND_CLEAR` (repeat off at the end: playback stops and the current track is cleared; it goes to history first, `QueueManager.pushToHistory`, so Previous goes back to it; a Play after that, from the lock screen, the car or a headset, starts nothing: shared `playStartsNothing`, #617).
- **An unplayable last track wraps only after something played.** Each platform keeps one flag, `playedSinceWrap`: false on a new queue (`setQueue` / `playQueue`, Android's single-track `playTrack`), on a restored queue and after a wrap; true once a track starts playing (Android: a play that ends with no `PlayFailure`, and only if no new queue was set while it was starting, `queueGeneration` / `playMarksQueuePlayed`; iOS: `.played`). Under repeat all, `SKIPPED_UNPLAYABLE` at the end wraps only while it's true, else stops, so a queue where nothing plays can't loop forever.
- **Wrap.** `QueueManager.wrapToStart(currentTrack)` rebuilds the queue from the lap (history, the current track and Up Next, leaving out every track that isn't in it), so tracks added since the queue started (Add to Queue, Play Next) come round too, reshuffled when shuffle is on. iOS no longer keeps an `originalQueue`.
- **Only this queue's tracks wrap.** Every track `QueueManager` holds (each history entry, each Up Next entry, the current track) records whether it belongs to the current queue, the lap. `clearQueue()` and `setQueue()` keep history for Previous but mark it out of the lap, so after a single-track play on Android (`playTrack` clears the queue: album, history and friend rows, a DJ `play`, a `parachord://play` link), a new queue on either platform, or Clear queue, repeat all wraps only the current track and what's queued since. **A play that replaces the current track puts it on history too, out of the lap (#605):** Android's `playTrack` (`clearQueue(outgoing, next)`) and `playQueue` / `restoreQueuePaused`, iOS's `setQueue` / `setQueuePaused` (`setQueue(…, outgoing)`), so Previous after a single-track play reaches the track that was playing, then the old queue's. Playing the same track again adds nothing; the Clear queue button passes none. History is capped at `QueueManager.MAX_HISTORY` (200), dropping the oldest entries from earlier queues first; the current lap is never trimmed. Desktop adds to history only when the queue moves on and keeps it unbounded for the session (Parachord/parachord#1329). Previous and Next keep every track reachable: Previous always puts the outgoing track at the front of Up Next, as before, and each track keeps its flag as it moves between history, the current track and Up Next (reorder, remove and shuffle keep it too), so an out-of-lap track can be played again but never wraps.
- **Spinoff pool tracks stay out of the lap; the shared `QueueManager` decides.** The pool plays outside the queue, so the platforms only report `poolTrackStarted(current)` when a pool track starts; every other play goes through `QueueManager`. `currentIsPoolTrack` (the current track came from the pool) is kept apart from `currentInLap`: a pool track is out of the lap, but so is a demoted track reached with Previous. When a pool track starts and the current track isn't one (the seed, a tapped track, a track reached with Previous) it goes to history with its own flag, so the seed is in the lap and Previous reaches it. Later pool tracks push nothing: only the pool track that's current when the queue resumes (`skipNext` / `playFromQueue` / `wrapToStart`, or the end-of-queue `pushToHistory`) goes to history, out of the lap. Previous from a pool track goes back to the seed, and the pool track waits at the front of Up Next, out of the lap. A radio link starts its first track as a pool track (`poolTrackStarted(null)` on Android, after `playTrack` cleared the queue), so when the station runs out under repeat all there's nothing to wrap and playback stops; on iOS a radio link keeps the user's queue suspended under it, so the queue resumes (and wraps) afterwards, with the track that was playing as the seed. Saved queues carry the flags (`playHistoryInQueue`, `upNextInLap`, `currentInLap`, `currentIsPoolTrack`); a blob without them restores every track as in the lap.
- **A rapid second Next or Previous (#602).** A platform shows a new track only once its play has started, so a second press while the first track is still starting hands `QueueManager` the track still on screen, which it already moved. `QueueManager` remembers the track it last made current and the one that replaced; while the track handed in is still that replaced one, it uses its own (`outgoingFor`), so no track goes into Up Next or history twice. Both apps, nothing platform-side.
- **Previous.** `previousAction`: restart the track when more than 3 000 ms in or when nothing is before it, else go to the previous track. On a track nothing holds (a `LoadOnPlay` slot, see "External Playback Background Survival") it measures from the slot's position (`positionForPrevious`), since the player reads 0; a restart there plays nothing: the slot goes to 0 (`restartedFromTop`), the track stays on screen, and the saved resume position follows. Both apps.
- **Repeat button.** `nextRepeatMode`: off, all, one, off. Android's Now Playing has the button beside shuffle, and the media session's repeat command (car, headphones, lock screen) maps to Parachord's repeat, not ExoPlayer's (see "External Playback Background Survival"). Under repeat all (not during Listen Along) the session's player reports a next item on the last track (`hasNextMediaItem`, `getNextMediaItemIndex` = 0), so Next stays offered where it wraps.
- **The media session's shuffle is Parachord's too (#584).** Android's forwarding player answers `getShuffleModeEnabled` from `PlaybackStateHolder` and sends `setShuffleModeEnabled` to `PlaybackController.setShuffle`, so the car and lock screen show and change the same setting as Now Playing; `publishShuffleMode()` (plus a collector on the state) tells the session when it changes, and a refused set (spinoff, Listen Along: `shuffleAllowed`) re-publishes the real value. ExoPlayer's own shuffle events aren't forwarded.
- **Listen Along owns the end of its own song.** The session's end-of-track hook (Android `onOwnTrackEnded`, iOS `onTrackFinished`) runs before `advanceAction`, so repeat never replays or wraps a Listen Along song, and repeat can't be changed while a session runs (Android `setRepeatMode` refuses it and the media session re-publishes the real value; iOS `repeatAllowed`; both Now Playing buttons are disabled). See "Spinoff + Listen Along".
- **A track with no source: one rule on both apps.** Each records `PlayFailure.NO_SOURCE` when nothing routes the track, the network is up and the resolvers answered with no match (message `PLAY_NO_SOURCE_MESSAGE`), and `START_FAILED` when offline (Android `NetworkMonitor`, iOS `IosNetworkStatus`) or when the on-the-fly resolve couldn't answer: nothing usable came back and a resolver failed (`ResolveReport.failed`, e.g. a Spotify cooldown or a plugin timeout, see #566), or the resolve threw. The shared `noSourceFailure(online, resolversCouldNotAnswer)` picks, and the shared `unplayableTrackOutcome(failure, advancing, spinoffMode, refusedMessage, carStopsSkip)` (#616) decides stop or skip and the message, for a track with no source and a refused embed alike: `failedPlayAwaitsLoad` first (`START_FAILED` stops on the track for Play with `PLAY_START_FAILED_MESSAGE`, advancing or not; `NO_SOURCE` stops on a tapped track with `PLAY_NO_SOURCE_MESSAGE`, a refused embed with its own message), and a skip (while the queue advances or a spinoff runs) asks the car's run, which can turn it into a stop with `OPEN_PARACHORD_ON_PHONE`. Cases only one platform has (Listen Along's own track, Apple Music not authorized, Spotify or an embed tapped in the car) are handled before it. iOS does this in `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.playTrack`'s `.noPlayableSource` branch (`iosApp/Parachord/ContentView.swift`), skipping through `advance(.skippedUnplayable)`. **A manual source switch that fails leaves the playback as it was, on both apps** (see "A failed source switch" below). On Android the skip (a refused embed's too) waits 600 ms (`scheduleAutoSkip`, `AUTO_SKIP_DELAY_MS`) and runs only if that track is still the current one and no other play began meanwhile (`playsBegun`, or one still starting); the previous playback is stopped first. During that gap Play and Pause do nothing (`autoSkipPendingFor`): re-playing the track would only fail again. Previous in the gap goes to the track before (the skipped track never played), and a request that plays nothing (Previous restarting the track) doesn't drop the skip, so the queue can't be stranded on it. When the queue skips on like this, an external handler's audio, polling and end-of-track detection stop but external mode, the silent loop and the foreground stay up for the next track (`failedTrackStop` → `KEEP_EXTERNAL_FOREGROUND`), and a foreground taken at play start is kept too (`keepsForegroundForAutoSkip`, see "Without the foreground, Android cuts the network"). After a local track (ExoPlayer) that ended by itself with no Parachord screen showing, the service has already kept the foreground at its end (see "A local track's end keeps the foreground", #614), and the skip carries it to the next track; when nothing holds it, the skip asks for it (`holdForegroundForAutoSkip`), which Android grants only while a start is still allowed. In the car, the third such skip in a row can stop instead (`CarUnplayableRun`, see "CarPlay / Android Auto").

### SoundCloud — the Widget (#495, desktop parachord#1209)

The SoundCloud API no longer gives a signed-in user full tracks: `/tracks/{id}/stream` 429s with the token in the query and redirects to a 30-second preview with it in a header, and the full streams (HLS only) answer 403 "An active Artist Pro subscription is required". So SoundCloud plays through SoundCloud's own widget (`w.soundcloud.com/player`), which needs no sign-in. Search and resolution still use the API and a SoundCloud sign-in (see "SoundCloud sign-in — the loopback listener").

- **Resolution is `soundcloud.axe`, on every platform (#598).** Android dropped its native SoundCloud resolver; the plugin's `resolve` picks the upload for all three apps, so a fix belongs in desktop's `plugins-src/soundcloud`. **Which upload (#582, parachord#1307):** SoundCloud is mostly fan uploads. The plugin takes only tracks Parachord can play (streamable, not `blocked` or `preview`: the widget refuses a preview), and counts a result as the artist's only when its uploader is the artist ("The" aside) or its title is an "Artist - Title" upload naming the artist; then the result carries that artist and title for the confidence check. Among those: the artist's own uploads first, the exact title, then one that isn't another version, then another version; with none, no SoundCloud source. Uploader containment isn't enough: "Ramones of the Day"'s "ROTD-Ep100 Judy Is a Punk" is a 29-minute podcast, and the first result was the preview-only "1975 Demo". **A failed search throws** (plugin 1.3.4: offline, a 401, 429 or 5xx, "SoundCloud search failed: <status>"), so it counts as unanswered and is retried (#566), not a miss; signed out is a miss. On a 401 both apps refresh the sign-in once and ask again (shared `retryAfterSoundCloudRejection`); the token the plugin gets is already refreshed before it expires (`SoundCloudAuth.pluginConfigJson`).
- **Desktop's module, unchanged.** `assets/js/soundcloud-widget-player.js` is a byte-for-byte copy of desktop's `src/renderer/lib/soundcloud-widget-player.js` (the widget's postMessage protocol, no SoundCloud `api.js`). `assets/js/soundcloud-widget.html` hosts it and relays its callbacks, tagged with a load generation, to `SoundCloudWidgetBridge` through a `@JavascriptInterface`. Update the module by copying desktop's again, not by editing it.
- **Background playback rides on the Apple Music setup**: a WebView never added to a window, `LAYER_TYPE_NONE`, `mediaPlaybackRequiresUserGesture = false`, `keepAlive()` before each track, and the controller's silent ExoPlayer loop for the foreground service and MediaSession. Chromium owns audio focus.
- **DRM tracks need two things on Android (#519, desktop parachord#1244).** Some tracks SoundCloud serves only encrypted (major-label releases such as "Shape of You"). The module's iframe delegates `allow="autoplay; encrypted-media"`, and `SoundCloudWidgetBridge` grants `RESOURCE_PROTECTED_MEDIA_ID`. The grant alone can't lift the iframe's policy: without the attribute the widget has no stream it can play and the track is refused after 10 s. No sign-in or subscription is involved.
- **Previews count as refused.** A track SoundCloud lets the widget only preview (a Go+ release: `getCurrentSound` reports `policy: "SNIP"`, `duration` 30000 against `full_duration`) still starts, fades out at 0:30 and fires `finish`, while `getDuration` reports the full length. Desktop's module asks `getCurrentSound` once the position moves and rejects such a track with reason `preview` (parachord#1211 / #1212, found by this port); the apps show desktop's "SoundCloud only lets Parachord play a preview of …" for it (`SoundCloudWidget.refusedMessage`).
- **A refused track is a failed source** (`EmbedPlaybackRefusedException`): another source plays (with a "Playing via X" toast), else the queue moves on when it was advancing or in a spinoff, else a snackbar offers "Open in SoundCloud" when the track has a soundcloud.com page. The browser never opens by itself. Both messages, the Now Playing context line ("Playing from: …") and resolver names come from the shared `PlaybackMessages` / `resolverDisplayName` (#596).
- **Link:** the source's soundcloud.com permalink, else `https://api.soundcloud.com/tracks/<id>` (the form SoundCloud's own embed code uses). Shared: `SoundCloudWidget.widgetLink` / `pageLink` (`shared/.../playback/SoundCloudWidget.kt`).
- **iOS does the same** in a hidden `WKWebView` (`iosApp/Parachord/SoundCloudWidget.swift`) with byte-identical copies of the page and module in `iosApp/Parachord/Resources/` (an Android unit test fails if they drift — edit Android's, copy). iOS has one extra trap — WebKit's own audio session can't be re-activated with the screen locked, so the page's `scHoldAudio` keeps it in use between tracks; see `iosApp/AGENTS.md` → "SoundCloud through its widget".

### SoundCloud sign-in — the loopback listener

Search and resolution need a SoundCloud sign-in. **Every sign-in, on every platform, uses desktop's redirect URI `http://127.0.0.1:8888/callback/soundcloud`.** It's the only one Parachord's built-in SoundCloud app (`SoundCloudBuiltInApp`, the client id + secret desktop ships as `FALLBACK_SOUNDCLOUD_*` in `main.js`) has, and SoundCloud matches it exactly (another port, `localhost` or `parachord://` leaves its sign-in page blank; checked in Chrome). A person's own app registers the same URI, so one app works on desktop, Android and iOS. **Never change the built-in app's redirect: desktop would stop signing in.**

- **The phones listen on 127.0.0.1:8888 during sign-in, as desktop does** (`SoundCloudLoopbackServer`, shared, on ktor-network). Loopback is shared between processes, so the browser's redirect reaches the app. The listener is only a trampoline: it answers `302` to `parachord://auth/callback/soundcloud` with the same `code` + `state`, and each platform's existing callback takes over (Android `OAuthRedirectActivity`, iOS ASWebAuthenticationSession). Only `code`, `state`, `error`, `error_description` are carried; another state gets a `400` and the listener keeps waiting; it stops after the forward, 10 min, or `stop()`. Verified on a Pixel 9a (Android 17) with a real SoundCloud account.
- **The exchange sends the redirect the authorize step used.** Android records it on the pending flow, so a flow started before this change (with `parachord://`) still exchanges correctly.
- **Credentials: own pair → build pair → built-in** (`resolveSoundCloudCredentials`), never mixed, so authorize and exchange name the same app.
- **A busy port can't fall back to another one** (exact match). The sign-in shows `SoundCloudLoopbackPortBusyException`'s message and doesn't open the browser. A squatter that sees the code can't use it without the PKCE verifier.
- **No cleartext exception is needed**: `network_security_config.xml` governs the app's own outgoing requests, and the listener only accepts. iOS ATS likewise.
- **Desktop keeps 127.0.0.1:8888 open whenever it runs**, and the iOS simulator shares the Mac's loopback, so a simulator sign-in fails as "port busy" while desktop runs. Devices are unaffected.

### Bandcamp — the Embed (#513, desktop parachord#1231)

Bandcamp plays through its own embedded player (`bandcamp.com/EmbeddedPlayer`), with no sign-in. The URL rules, state rules and id lookup are shared (`shared/.../playback/BandcampEmbed.kt`, a port of desktop's `bandcamp-embed-url.js`, `sanitizeEmbedState` and adapter); each platform has a thin web view and poll loop (Android `BandcampEmbedBridge` + `BandcampPlaybackHandler`, iOS `IosBandcampEmbed`).

- **The embed is the web view's top-level page.** It has no protocol and our page can't reach into a cross-origin iframe. Desktop's `bandcamp-embed-control.js` runs in it and drives the page's `<audio>`: `assets/js/` and `iosApp/Parachord/Resources/` hold byte-identical copies of desktop's `main-process/` file (an Android unit test fails if they differ). Update it by copying desktop's.
- **No bridge into the page.** Bandcamp's scripts run at that top level, so there's no `@JavascriptInterface` or `WKScriptMessageHandler`. The apps poll `bcState()` every 500 ms from outside and hand the answer to `BandcampEmbedTracker`, which trusts none of it. Only fixed scripts run, and a value reaches them only as a checked number.
- **Drive a page only after its load finished.** Replaying a track is the same URL, so the old page is still up for a moment; its moved position read as "started" and the new page never got its click (paused at 0:00, found on a Pixel). Android gates on `onPageFinished`, iOS on `didFinish` for the navigation it started.
- **Always the single-track URL.** `album=…/track=…` runs on into the album's next track by itself. The URL is built only from a numeric track id. `bandcamp.axe` search results carry it (`ResolvedSource.bandcampTrackId`); a track row has no column for it, so Android reads it from `TrackResolverCache`. With no id (older cached sources, library rows, `lookupUrl` results), `BandcampEmbedResolver` looks it up through the search API and then the page, but only for a track page on a `bandcamp.com` subdomain.
- **`#big_play_button`, not `.embeddedplaybutton`.** The second is a 0x0 element and loads no stream.
- **A failed source, like SoundCloud.** Started means the position advanced, within 15 s. A track that doesn't start throws `EmbedPlaybackRefusedException`: another source plays, else the queue moves on, else a snackbar offers "Open in Bandcamp". The browser never opens by itself. An error after it started counts as the track's end, so the queue can't stall.
- **`stream: false` stays in the plugin**, so app versions without this engine keep opening the browser. Both routers check for Bandcamp first, ahead of their non-streaming check. A `minAppVersion` for the phones isn't needed for that reason.
- **The controller treats both embeds the same**: `EmbedPlaybackHandler` (position, duration, `onFinished`, volume), mirrored by `startEmbedStatePolling`.
- **The real length comes from the embed.** The plugin's results hard-code `duration: 210`.

**Not checked:** whether repeated plays of one track hit a Bandcamp limit. Desktop's #1231 asked for that check and recorded no result. A limit that stops a track from starting is a refused source; one that stops it mid-track isn't detected.

### CarPlay / Android Auto / Android Automotive — shared car logic

**Keep the car experiences at feature parity, on shared code.** Android Auto, Android Automotive and CarPlay should offer the same browse tree, Now Playing buttons, messages and playback rules wherever the platforms allow. A car feature lands on all of them in the same change, with its logic in `com.parachord.shared.car` and each platform drawing only what its car framework requires. If one platform can't do it yet (an API it lacks, an entitlement not granted), say so in the PR and file the follow-up; don't leave the gap silent. Android Automotive (Parachord running on the car's own Android, not projected from the phone) reuses this shared tree and the `MediaLibrarySession` that Android Auto uses.

**Android Automotive is built — the app's own UI on the car's screen (one APK for phones and cars).** `MainActivity` detects AAOS (`CarEnvironment`, `FEATURE_AUTOMOTIVE`) and swaps its root to `CarRoot` (`app/.../ui/car/`); the app runs **always dark** on a car (Auto's template is; the player surfaces are — the parked full UI follows on the same display). The car home is a Library section first — a Collection Radio tile and the saved playlists as tiles (every play through the shared `HomeQueues`) — then the shared recommendations page — categories as rows of cards, previews only, tap plays (every play action is `RecommendationsViewModel`'s, unmodified; no See-all, no source chips, no long-press menus) — plus a **search** button and a **settings gear** in the top bar and a now-playing bar at the bottom (car-scaled `MiniPlayer` shape) that opens Now Playing. Now Playing matches Auto's layout (large art, seek, prev/play/next) plus shuffle (the shared `shuffleAllowed` rule disables it during a spinoff or Listen Along) and the shared `carNowPlayingButtons` (heart, Spinoff); a corner button opens the **queue**, which only jumps (`playFromQueue`) — no reorder, remove or clear. **Search** runs the shared `SearchSession` (the phone search's engine: the library's tracks and albums, remote tracks, albums and artists) with its results **merged into one list** of song/album/artist rows, each tagged with its kind and source; a **song tap replaces the queue with that one track** (`playTrack` under a `"search"` context), an album plays its tracks (a library album straight from the library; a remote one through `CatalogQueues.album`), an artist plays their top songs — and it opens Now Playing. **The gear opens the whole phone app parked only** (`ParachordAppContent` at its Settings route under a close bar that returns to the car home); **driving away closes the full app by itself** (the drive UI is the safe surface): `CarDrivingState` reads `android.car`'s `CarUxRestrictionsManager` through reflection (`android.car` isn't in the public SDK; `CarDrivingStateMonitor` is system-only) and **fails open** when the car service is unavailable. **While moving, the drive UI follows the driver-distraction rules**: typing is off (the search field disables, with a hint to use the car's voice assistant — which searches and plays through the shared `MediaLibrarySession`), and the queue caps its jump targets at 8 rows with a "Park to see the full queue" line. Sign-in assumes a browser exists on the car (Chrome Beta, or the OEM's link viewer): `OAuthManager.launchCustomTab` falls back to a plain `ACTION_VIEW`, and the manifest queries for browsers. Known limits (v1): while the car home is up, nav-only deep links are dropped (documented on `CarRoot`; play links still play), and the cards reuse the phone components' text sizes. Design record: `docs/plans/2026-11-24-aaos-car-ui.md`. iOS has no AAOS counterpart — CarPlay draws from the same shared models.

The car UIs show one tree and follow one set of rules, all in `shared/.../car/` (`com.parachord.shared.car`):

- **`CarBrowse`** (over a `CarLibrarySource`; `daoCarLibrarySource` is the DAO-backed one): `children(parentId)`, `item(id)`, `playRequest(id)`, `isBrowseTreeId(id)`. The root lists Playlists, Loved Songs and Collection Radio. Loved Songs are the loves only (`lovedTracks`: no local file the user never hearted); Collection Radio plays every track row, local files included. Caps: `CAR_LOVED_CAP` 100 loved songs, `CAR_PLAYLIST_CAP` 500 tracks in one opened playlist (the playlists list is uncapped).
- **`CarIds`**, the exact media ids: `parachord_root`, `browse:playlists`, `browse:loved`, `action:collection-radio`, `playlist:<id>`, `playlist-shuffle:<id>`, `playlist-track:<position>:<playlistId>` (position first so an id containing `:` parses), `loved-track:<id>`.
- **`CarItem`** (`id`, `title`, `subtitle`, `artworkUrl`, `album`, `isBrowsable`, `isPlayable`, `kind` = `FOLDER | PLAYLIST | TRACK | ACTION`) is the tile; **`CarPlayRequest`** (`tracks`, `startIndex`, `shuffle`; null shuffle keeps the current setting) is what a tap plays. Collection Radio plays the whole collection shuffled.
- **`carNowPlayingButtons(loved, spinoffMode, spinoffAvailable, spinoffLoading)`** gives the Now Playing buttons: heart (`LOVE` / `UNLOVE`), then Spinoff (`SPINOFF` / `END_SPINOFF`). None while nothing plays; Spinoff is hidden, not dimmed, when nothing similar exists. While the pool is being built (15 s or more) Spinoff already shows as `END_SPINOFF`, and a tap is ignored: the car shows no other sign that the tap took, and an unchanged button read as a tap that did nothing (#551).
- **`CarMessages`** (`OPEN_SPOTIFY_ON_PHONE`, `OPEN_PARACHORD_ON_PHONE`) and **`CarUnplayableRun`** (stop after `CAR_UNPLAYABLE_RUN_LIMIT` = 3 unplayable tracks in a row, only in the car with Parachord not in front on the phone). Both apps call `failed(carWithoutPhone, connection)` with their count of car connections, so a new connection starts the run over; a track that plays (`started`) and an explicit play (`reset`) do too.

**Per platform, only the drawing.** Android `PlaybackService` maps `CarItem` → `MediaItem` and serves it from the `MediaLibrarySession` callback (and sends `playRequest` to the controller). The car's covers follow the phone's (#551): Android's playing track is `PlaybackState.effectiveTrack`, the phone's Now Playing (the service's reported title and artist over the queued track), with `PlaybackState.effectiveArtwork` (streaming service, the track's own, then its resolved sources'); the queue `carArtworkFor`. Before #721 the car took the queued track's title and artist with the service's cover, so a queued row whose names didn't match its Apple Music id showed one song's names over another's cover. **Hearts and track menus never save `effectiveTrack`** (#721): it keeps the queued track's ids under the reported names, and a heart during a stale report from the previous song stored a hybrid row. They act on `currentTrack`, as desktop's `addTrackToCollection` does. A spinoff track from Last.fm has no cover of its own, so the car showed the previous cover, then a blank one, until the artwork lookup after the track started found one. iOS draws `CPListTemplate`s from `IosContainer.carChildren` / `carPlayRequest` (`iosApp/Parachord/CarPlay/`; details in `iosApp/AGENTS.md` → "The CarPlay screens"). Change the tree, caps, ids or button rules once, in shared.

**The phone follows what the car plays.** The car can start the app with the phone locked and no phone screen, so everything that follows playback starts with the playback session, not with a phone screen. Android: `PlaybackService.onCreate` calls `PlaybackController.connect()` (the controller, the paused queue restore, queue saving, scrobbling, the widget), and a browse tap waits for the controller (`awaitConnected`, at most 5 s) before it plays. Before this, `connect()` ran only from `MainViewModel`: a cold Auto play had no controller (a local file couldn't start), saved and scrobbled nothing, and unlocking the phone then restored the last session's queue over it, so the phone showed the old track and Auto was handed it, paused. iOS: `AppBootstrap` holds all of this, and a CarPlay tap waits for it. **The saved queue is restored only when nothing has played yet in this run** (shared `QueueRestorePolicy`, checked after the restore has read storage): a car tap, a deep link or a phone tap that started first keeps its queue. A `parachord://` play link searches before it plays, so it counts as asked for as soon as it arrives (#604: shared `DeepLinkAction.startsPlayback`; Android `PlaybackController.noteLinkPlayRequested`, iOS `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.noteLinkPlayRequested`); before, a cold-start link lost to the restore. An empty queue is never saved over the last session's until this run has had a queue (`QueueRestorePolicy.shouldSave`), so a link that finds nothing leaves the saved queue for the next launch.

**Three unplayable tracks in a row stop, on both.** A track the queue would skip (no source or a refused embed while it advances or a spinoff runs; not a track Listen Along drives) counts towards the run. In the car with Parachord not in front on the phone, the third stops on that track with `OPEN_PARACHORD_ON_PHONE` and leaves a `LoadOnPlay` slot, so the car's Play retries it, instead of the queue running silently to its end. With the phone app in front, or no car, the queue skips on. Android: `PlaybackController.carStopsAutoSkip`, decided before the track is published so the car shows one message, not the phone's and then its own; the play is marked `carStoppedPlay` and `failedPlayAwaitsLoadAfterCarStop` leaves the slot. iOS: below.

**How Android knows it's in the car** (`CarConnections`, `app/.../playback/CarConnections.kt`). `PlaybackService`'s `onConnect` / `onDisconnected` report each media-session controller; one from Android Auto's package (`com.google.android.projection.gearhead`) counts, and the connection number goes up when the first one connects (Auto's browser and controller are one connection). The service's `onDestroy` clears them; the count lives on the controller, so it keeps rising across service restarts. **Media3 1.5.1 never reports a disconnect for a legacy `MediaBrowserCompat` connection**, which Android Auto may use, so a tracked controller can outlive the car. So when the decision is made, `CarConnections.confirmed` also reads Android Auto's own car-connection provider (`content://androidx.car.app.connection`, column `CarConnectionState`: 0 no car, 1 native, 2 projection; `queryCarConnectionState`, declared in the manifest's `<queries>`). It can only veto: a 0 clears the tracked controllers; a missing or unreadable provider, or a NULL value, leaves the tracked answer, so where it's missing "in the car" can outlive the car until the playback service restarts. Checked on the Desktop Head Unit with a Pixel 9a (Nov 2026), phone locked: the provider answered 0 with no car, 2 while connected and 0 again after; the third unplayable track in a row stopped with `OPEN_PARACHORD_ON_PHONE`, the car's Play retried it and its Next moved on to a track that played; with the head unit closed the same queue skipped all three. Not checked in a real car, nor after a local track on the head unit (the media session now offers Next and Previous for a local track too, #608).

**The media session's Next and Previous always move Parachord's queue (#608).** The service's forwarding player offers Next and Previous at all times and sends them to the controller (`skipNext` / `skipPrevious`), whatever plays: an external engine, a local track ExoPlayer plays, or a stopped track waiting for Play (`needsLoadOnPlay`). ExoPlayer holds at most the one track it plays, so a local track's Next from the lock screen, a headset or the car went to ExoPlayer, which had no next item, and did nothing; its Previous restarted the track by ExoPlayer's rule rather than the shared `previousAction`, and Listen Along never heard about either.

**A play still starting shows as buffering (#677).** While an external engine starts (Spotify waking, MusicKit loading, an embed's page) or stalls, the controller's state is `isPlaying` + `isBuffering`, and the forwarding player reports `STATE_BUFFERING` (with `isPlaying` false) instead of `STATE_READY`. The service tells the session whenever either flag changes (`publishExternalPlaybackState`), since no ExoPlayer event follows them. Reported as READY, a play Android Auto started on connecting (the reopened queue's track) showed Pause over silence for the seconds it took to load. Android Auto's small home-screen media card still draws Pause while buffering (it follows what was asked for). Checked on the Desktop Head Unit with a Pixel 9a: on connecting, the session went PAUSED → BUFFERING (3 s) → PLAYING, and the car showed the buffering state before the song played.

**Car-only playback rules on iOS** (`CarPlaySession`; a phone with no car connected plays as always):
- SoundCloud and Bandcamp are refused only when a car is connected and the app has no phone window to host a web view, which happens when the car launched the app and the phone never showed it (iOS won't open a window from the background). Then Bandcamp is always refused (each track is a new page) and SoundCloud only when its page would have to be created, and the router moves to the next source. A phone window that exists plays them even with the phone locked or another app in front, as on the phone.
- Spotify never wakes the Spotify app, shows no device picker and uses only this phone's own device; if it isn't listed the source fails at once. A track only Spotify could play stops with `OPEN_SPOTIFY_ON_PHONE`.
- After 3 unplayable tracks in a row (in the car, with Parachord not in front on the phone) playback stops on the last one with `OPEN_PARACHORD_ON_PHONE`, instead of skipping to the end of the queue (the rule above; the connection number is `CarPlaySession.connectionCount`).

**Entitlement.** Apple granted the CarPlay audio entitlement (`com.apple.developer.carplay-audio`), and it's enabled on the `com.parachord.ios` App ID. It's in `Parachord.entitlements`, so simulator, device and App Store builds all carry it. A device build fails to sign if the App ID loses the capability, and a new App ID (another bundle id) needs it enabled in the developer portal first.

**Spinoff's icon is the fork** on both platforms and in both car UIs (Android `ic_auto_spinoff.xml`, iOS `SpinoffFork` / `SpinoffForkActive`); sparkles is Shuffleupagus.

### Spotify Connect — Device Wake & Playback

Spotify playback uses the **Web API** (not the App Remote SDK). The `SpotifyPlaybackHandler` manages device discovery, wake, and playback via Spotify Connect HTTP endpoints.

**Device wake strategy (two-tier):**
1. **Media button broadcast (invisible):** Sends `KEYCODE_MEDIA_PLAY` targeted to `com.spotify.music`. Wakes Spotify's `MediaBrowserService` without showing any UI. Works when Spotify is in the background but **does NOT work when Spotify is fully killed** (no process to receive the broadcast).
2. **Launch intent (fallback after 2s):** Uses `getLaunchIntentForPackage` to start Spotify's activity. This brings Spotify briefly to the foreground (jarring), so immediately after launching, we bring our own app back to front. Only used when the broadcast doesn't wake Spotify within 2 seconds of polling. It needs Spotify declared in the manifest's `<queries>`: without it Android 11+ hides Spotify from Parachord, `getLaunchIntentForPackage` returns null, and the fallback reported "Spotify is not installed" (#459 spike).

**Critical: never use the launch intent as the primary wake strategy.** It opens Spotify visually, which is terrible UX for a music player controlling another music player. Always try the invisible broadcast first.

**Device polling:** 300ms intervals (not 1s — the devices endpoint is lightweight), 8s total timeout, 500ms initial delay before first poll.

**Single-pass play flow (no compounding retries):**
1. `ensureDevice()` — fast-path check, then wake + poll if needed (0-8s)
2. `pickDevice()` — accepts inactive preferred devices (transfer will activate), auto-selects single real device
3. `startPlayback()` with `device_id` and the track's album as the context (below) — single retry on 502 after 1s
4. Quick verification on cold devices only (2 polls × 500ms)

**Play the track inside its album, never as bare `uris`.** Spotify's Android app (9.2.0, installed Nov 2026, seen on a Pixel 9a with Android 17) accepts `PUT /me/player/play` with `{"uris":["spotify:track:…"]}` (204), stops whatever it was playing and then plays nothing: `/v1/me/player` reports the phone active with no track, or 204, for as long as anyone waits, so every Spotify play fell back to the next source through #452's safety net. `uris` + `position_ms` fails the same way. The same request plays at once on Spotify's Mac app, and `{"context_uri":"spotify:album:…","offset":{"uri":"spotify:track:…"}}` plays at once on the phone, idle or already playing. So both platforms build every one-track play with the shared `SpotifyPlayRequests` (`shared/.../api/SpotifyPlayRequests.kt`): it looks up the album with a gated `GET /v1/tracks/{id}`, once per track per run (kept in memory), and falls back to bare `uris` only when that lookup fails (offline, a rate-limit cooldown). Android's `prewarm(trackUri)` fetches it ahead; the confirm and the polling retry re-send the same request. Spotify carries on into the album's next track when ours ends (`uris` with an `offset` doesn't start on the phone either, so a context can't be avoided). The near-end advance and the empty-queue stop handle that while Parachord can reach Spotify; when it can't, see "When Parachord can't reach Spotify" below. `SpPlaybackRequest` leaves null fields out of the JSON (the shared client encodes defaults, which used to send `"context_uri": null`). Desktop still sends bare `uris` ([parachord#1300](https://github.com/Parachord/parachord/issues/1300)).

**An accepted play is not a started play (#452).** Spotify can answer `startPlayback` with success and never start (its process only bound by Android Auto as a media browser), and `/v1/me/player` then answers **204** — "nothing playing", a real answer, not a failed poll (`fetchPlaybackState` returns an idle state for it). Every play, warm or cold, goes through `confirmPlaybackStarted`, which polls ~4 s for **our** track playing (the item's id or its `linked_from` id, as desktop's `matches()`; Spotify still playing its own song after ignoring the play reports `is_playing` too) (with a Parachord screen showing, it re-sends the play once and polls again; in the background it doesn't, since a re-send never fixed the bound-only case and only kept the car silent longer), pauses the device so a late start can't play over the fallback, and `play()` throws `SpotifyPlaybackNotStartedException`; `PlaybackController` falls back to the next source like `SpotifyWakeBlockedException`. Never "proceed optimistically" — the stale-position watchdog ignores stalls while polls fail, so a play that never started hung forever. The warm path used to skip the confirm on an active device with a screen showing; a play Spotify ignored then sat at 0:00 with no fallback, since the polling loop re-sends once and stops checking.

**A track resumed outside Parachord is taken back as playing (#666, desktop parachord#1357).** The user pauses a Spotify track in Parachord, then resumes it from Spotify's app or notification, a headset while Spotify holds the media buttons, or another Connect device. Before, Parachord kept showing Play, its next Play tap paused Spotify (the handler's `isPlaying` had followed Spotify), the end-of-track loop stayed stopped so the queue never moved on, and the idle timeout paused the track 5 minutes later. Now, after a user pause, the app asks `/v1/me/player` every 5 s, and when Spotify plays **our** track again (its item or `linked_from` id; another song doesn't count), it shows it as playing, cancels the idle timeout and restarts the end-of-track loop. Nothing counts in the first 5 s after our own pause, nor while a `LoadOnPlay` slot holds the track or a play is starting. The rule is the shared `SpotifyResumeWatch` (`shared/.../playback/SpotifyResumeWatch.kt`). Android: the handler's poll slows to 5 s while we've paused, keeps `isPlaying` false while Spotify plays another song (so Play still resumes ours), and calls `onResumedElsewhere`, which `PlaybackController.adoptSpotifyResumedElsewhere` answers. Checked on a Pixel 9a. iOS: `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.checkSpotifyResumedElsewhere`, in the Spotify loop. A resume in the last 5 s before the idle timeout can still be stopped by it.

**When Parachord can't reach Spotify, it pauses this phone's Spotify with a media key.** With Parachord's network cut (Android's `APP_BACKGROUND` firewall once the foreground is lost, Doze), the album context lets Spotify play the rest of the album by itself, and a Web API pause can't get through either. A targeted `KEYCODE_MEDIA_PAUSE` broadcast (`sendSpotifyMediaKey`) needs no network. Only when the track plays on this phone's own device (`playingOnThisPhone`): the key reaches the Spotify app here, not a TV. Two places:
- **At the track's end** (`shouldPauseAtTrackEnd`, run every second by its own loop, `endGuardJob`): no poll has answered for `UNREACHABLE_AFTER_MS` (3 s) and the position, extrapolated from where a poll last saw it, is within 1 s of the end. It pauses once per track. On the Pixel 9a with Parachord firewalled, Spotify paused at 91.8 s of 92.5 s, before the album's next song; once the network was back Parachord read "Track finished" and moved on.
- **When `pause()` can't reach the Web API** (no answer within 3 s, or a 5xx): the controller stops Spotify this way when it moves to another source. A 4xx is Spotify answering (403 when already paused), so no key.

**"Unreachable" is measured in time, not failed requests.** A firewalled request doesn't fail; it hangs until the 30 s socket timeout, and the failure count stays at 0. So the stale-position watchdog (`staleWatchdogFires`) also needs a poll that answered within 3 s: counting only failures, it read the hang as a stall and ended the track at 72 s of 92 while Spotify played on. iOS can't send another app a media key, so with no network an iOS play can still run on into the album.

**Prewarm before a Spotify track.** In the last 30 s of a non-Spotify song (ExoPlayer and Apple Music polling loops), and for a restored queue at startup, `PlaybackController.prewarmSpotifyFor` checks whether the next track routes to Spotify and calls `SpotifyPlaybackHandler.prewarm()`. It finds the device the play will use (`prewarmTarget`) and records it (`deviceVerified`, `lastResolvedLocalId`), so the play takes the warm path instead of spending 5–8 s waking and polling. If the phone isn't registered it wakes Spotify with **`KEYCODE_MEDIA_PAUSE`, never PLAY**: a play key resumes Spotify's own last track and takes audio focus from whatever is playing, which pauses ExoPlayer and MusicKit. Never the launch intent. A preferred remote device is only looked up. Skipped during a rate-limit cooldown.

**Cold-start routing uses stored IDs (#452, #587), on both apps.** With nothing in the resolver cache, a track plays at once from the Spotify / Apple Music IDs it carries (shared `SourceReselection.storedIdSources`, connected services only), ranked in the user's resolver order, and resolves fully in the background so its source icons appear and the next play has every source. A track's stored `resolver` is only whichever service first played or synced it; `shouldRerouteToStoredId` keeps one on a resolver the IDs don't cover (a local file, SoundCloud, Bandcamp) unless a stored ID ranks above it. Android: `reselectBestSource` → `routeByStoredIds`. iOS: `IosContainer.storedIdRoute`, in `playTrack`'s cache-miss branch; when it gives nothing, iOS waits for the resolve as before. Desktop always waits for the resolve (it has no car case). **The Now Playing source switcher** offers only sources that can play (`SourceReselection.switchableSources`: not `noMatch`, at or above the floor, one per resolver, in the user's order), and a switch plays that resolver's best such source (`sourceForSwitch`); Android used to offer and play a `noMatch` source. **The pick shows at once (both apps):** the switcher's icon and checked row show the picked source the moment it's picked, for that track, while its play starts (shared `SourceReselection.pickerShownResolver` over a `PendingSourcePick`, a display state never written onto the track), then the real source: the pick once it started, the old one if it failed (iOS #287) or another play replaced it. Meanwhile the play button (Now Playing and the mini player) shows its spinner: Android's `PlaybackState.isStartingPlay` (any play, from the request through the lock wait, lookup and route until the engine started it or it failed, since the old engine's polls keep writing `isBuffering`; the buttons read `showsLoading`), iOS's `isStarting`. Desktop shows the same spinner (`trackLoading`) but changes the icon only after the start.

**Spotify sign-in expiry (#352, desktop parachord#967).** A Spotify grant dies 6 months after the original authorization; refreshing doesn't extend it. Every successful authorization-code exchange records `spotify_authorized_at` (`SettingsStore.markSpotifyAuthorized`, Android `OAuthManager` + iOS `IosSpotifyAuth`), and Home shows a dismissible warning in the last 7 days (`SpotifyGrantExpiry` in shared; Android `SpotifyExpiryWarningBanner`, iOS `PCSpotifyExpiryBanner` via `IosContainer.spotifyExpiryWarning`), back at most once a day. No recorded time means no warning: an older connection can't know its grant's age, and gets the reactive "session expired" banner once.

**Warm-path optimization:** When `deviceVerified && preferredDeviceId != null`, fires `startPlayback` directly without any device fetch (~200ms).

**"This device" resolution:** When the user picks the synthetic local device, `resolveLocalDevice()` wakes Spotify (broadcast → fallback launch intent) and polls for this phone's Smartphone-type device (`isThisPhonesSpotifyDevice`): named exactly as the phone (`Settings.Global.DEVICE_NAME`, which Spotify uses for its Connect name), else containing `Build.MODEL` or `Build.MANUFACTURER`. A phone renamed "J9a" shows up as "J9a", which the model match alone never found (#459 spike). iOS matches `UIDevice.current.name`, which since iOS 16 is the generic "iPhone" without Apple's user-assigned-device-name entitlement, so a renamed iPhone has the same gap there. Remote devices (TVs, computers) appear in the API even when local Spotify isn't running — `deviceVerified` being true does NOT mean Spotify is running locally.

**Spotify API device visibility limitations:** `GET /v1/me/player/devices` only returns devices with active Spotify Connect sessions, not all devices on the network. It also returns stale/phantom devices (e.g., a "Bedroom TV" that's been off for days). The desktop app may see more devices via native SDK discovery. Restricted devices (Spotify Free) are filtered from the picker.

**Key files:** `SpotifyPlaybackHandler.kt`, `SpotifyApi.kt`, `SpotifyDevicePickerDialog.kt`

### Apple Music State Polling & Auto-Advance

**Apple's MusicKit SDK for Android was evaluated and isn't used (#553).** A November 2026 spike (`docs/plans/2026-11-08-musickit-android-sdk-spike.md`) found it plays with the MusicKit JS user token and needs no Apple Music app, starts tracks slightly faster (2.2 s median against 2.6 s) and survived screen-off Doze, as MusicKit JS did. It has no volume control, and Apple's license forbids committing its AARs or hosting them publicly, so CI can't build with it. Read the doc before proposing a native Apple Music engine.

Apple Music playback runs in a hidden WebView via `MusicKitWebBridge`. MusicKit can't play offline.

**A play that doesn't start throws.** `AppleMusicPlaybackHandler.play()` returns only once the track plays, so the controller never polls a track that isn't playing, and Play loads a failed one again (`LoadOnPlay`, `FAILED`). Offline, MusicKit's `play()` fails at once ("Unable to complete request for https://api.music.apple.com/…"); the handler retries 3 times (a DRM handshake can still be settling), re-authorizes and plays once more, then throws `PlaybackStartFailedException`. It also throws it when `configure()` failed (MusicKit didn't load offline, `MusicKitConfigureFailure.NOT_LOADED`, and others) or sign-in is required, and `PlaybackStartTimeoutException` when a play hung (MusicKit is reset). With `alreadyReported` (the sign-in prompt, the expired-key message) the controller adds no message of its own; otherwise it shows "Couldn't start playing. Check your connection and try again." Before this the handler returned as if it had started: polling began, Play only tried MusicKit's resume, and the track's position was lost.

**No WebView, no waiting.** `pause`, `resume`, `stop`, `seekTo` and `play` return at once when the bridge has no WebView (`awaitPlayer`): `configure()` tears it down when MusicKit didn't load, and nothing loads it again until the next `configure()`. Each call used to wait the full 20 s for MusicKit, so Play after an offline launch spent 40 s (the resume, then the router's `stop`) before its play began. The `startAppleMusicStatePolling()` loop polls at 500ms intervals on `Dispatchers.Default` for position/duration updates and uses two auto-advance detection paths:

1. **JS callback (primary):** MusicKit JS fires `playbackStateDidChange` → "ended" state → `onTrackEnded` callback → `skipNextInternal()`
2. **Polling safety net:** `isOurTrackDone()` checks `!isPlaying && position > 0 && duration - position < 1500`

An `AtomicBoolean(trackEndHandled)` prevents both paths from double-firing.

**Critical rule: never suspend the polling loop on network calls.** The `MusicKitWebBridge.evaluate()` function awaits JS async results via `CompletableDeferred`. If a slow operation runs inline in the polling loop, the entire loop freezes — no position updates, no safety-net checks, no stall recovery. Even though JS callbacks can fire independently, they may be delayed or suppressed when MusicKit is handling concurrent API requests in the same WebView context. Always use `scope.launch` (fire-and-forget) for non-essential async work within the polling loop.

**Critical rule: never call `preload()` during active playback.** The `preload()` function fires a `music.api.music()` catalog API request through the same MusicKit JS instance that's actively streaming. On Android WebView, this concurrent API call disrupts the active playback stream — observed as MusicKit flipping to `state:"unknown"/isPlaying:false` mid-song, causing the track to stop. The polling loop pre-resolves the next track's Apple Music ID (via `reselectBestSource`) but does NOT call `preload()`. The `setQueue()` call in `play()` is fast enough with a warm WebView.

**Critical rule: never run end-detection on stale poll data.** When the screen is off, `pollPlaybackState()` can time out (Main thread deferred in Doze mode), leaving `_isPlaying` and `playbackStateName` stale in `MusicKitWebBridge`. If the last cached `isPlaying` was `false` (from a transient buffering hiccup), `isOurTrackDone()` would falsely trigger, stopping the song mid-play. The polling loop tracks `pollSucceeded` and only runs end-detection when the poll returned fresh data.

**Pre-resolving next track:** The polling loop resolves the next Apple Music track's ID 30s before the current track ends. This runs as a fire-and-forget `scope.launch(Dispatchers.Main)` — NOT inline. Only the ID is resolved; no `preload()` API call is made (see rule above).

### .axe Plugin System — Full Integration

The Android app runs the **same .axe plugin system as desktop**. 20 plugins loaded via `JsBridge` (headless WebView) executing `resolver-loader.js` unchanged from the Electron app. The `PluginManager` handles loading, semver deduplication, hot-reload, and marketplace sync from the `parachord-plugins` GitHub repo.

**Architecture:**
- `JsRuntime` interface (`plugin/JsRuntime.kt`) — platform-agnostic JS execution abstraction. Android: `JsBridge` (WebView). iOS future: JavaScriptCore. KMP-ready.
- `PluginManager` — loads .axe files from `assets/plugins/` (bundled) + `filesDir/plugins/` (downloaded updates). Deduplicates by semver (higher version wins). Provides type-safe Kotlin methods for resolve/search/AI/scrobble calls.
- `NativeBridge` — polyfills `fetch` (async, non-blocking), `console`, and `storage` (backed by DataStore) for the JS runtime.
- `PluginSyncService` — fetches `manifest.json` from GitHub, downloads updated .axe files, calls `PluginManager.reloadPlugins()` for hot-reload. Runs on app start (24h debounce) + manual "Check for updates" in Settings.

**Plugin types loaded:**
- **Content resolvers** (stream: true): spotify, applemusic, localfiles — native Kotlin takes priority (faster), .axe as fallback; soundcloud — the .axe only (#598)
- **html5Stream resolvers**: subsonic — the host plays a URL the plugin's `getStreamUrl` builds at play time, on both apps (see "Plugin settings + the html5Stream engine")
- **widevine resolvers** (Android): a plugin with `capabilities.widevine` and both `getPlaybackManifest` and `relayLicense` plays through ExoPlayer with Widevine (see "Plugin-supplied Widevine playback"); `amazonmusic.axe` is the first
- **Content resolvers** (stream: false): bandcamp — plays in its embed through an explicit engine ahead of the `stream: false` check (see "Bandcamp — the Embed"), so older app versions still open the browser
- **AI providers**: chatgpt, gemini, claude — native Kotlin handles these, `AxeAiProvider` wrapper available for .axe-only providers (ollama)
- **Meta-services**: discogs, wikipedia, lastfm, listenbrainz, librefm — native Kotlin implementations (except Recommendations, below), `AxeScrobbler` wrapper ready for migration

**Recommendations come from the plugins, and the page model is shared (#606, desktop parachord#1309, #645 / #672 / #673 / #707).** A plugin that declares `capabilities.recommendations` answers `getRecommendations(config)` with `{ sections: [{ id?, title?, description?, nextCursor?, items: [{ type: artist|album|song|playlist, name, artist?, duration? (seconds), mbid?, isrc?, … }] }] }`; today `lastfm.axe`, `listenbrainz.axe` and `applemusic.axe`. Apple Music's personal picks come from the host hook `appleMusicPersonalRecommendations` (the catalog's `/v1/me/recommendations` through `AppleMusicCatalog`, needing the Music User Token), like the plugin's other four Apple Music hooks. `RecommendationsRepository` asks each provider through the resolver-loader (`PluginManager.recommendationResolvers` / `getRecommendations` / `expandRecommendationSection`, the unique-key poll, 20 s each). **Which providers:** the shared `pluginIsOn` (Android: the off-lists plus the connection check; iOS: the active-resolver list) minus the ones in `recommendations_off_resolvers`; Last.fm and ListenBrainz only with a username (`metaServicePluginConfigs` supplies it through `resolverConfigProvider`). **When a "Use recommendations from X" switch changes** (#776, desktop's provider-set effect), the repository reacts to `getRecommendationsOffResolversFlow`, so both pages and Home's "Albums you might love" follow with no platform code: a provider switched off leaves the page at once (its sections are filtered out, an answer still in flight for it stays off, and its people leave the Listeners row with its chip: `SimilarUsersStore` drops the off-list's ids from its providers, so it isn't asked and isn't a Listeners service, desktop parachord#1650), and one switched on is asked by a new load that doesn't join a running one or wait for Home's 10-minute throttle; providers with a fresh answer show it without a request. A change before any load asks nobody. **The page:** `RecommendationModel.kt`, `RecommendationCategories.kt` and `RecommendationPage.kt` port desktop's `recommendation-model.js` and page code (vectors in `RecommendationModelTest`). They build the Tracks, Albums, Artists, Playlists and Listeners rows: untitled sections merge into generic per-type buckets deduped by artist + title; titled sections become category rows; only the newest week of each weekly playlist shows; Shuffleupagus's picks are blended in; the blocklist (`block_recommendation`) is applied on every rebuild so a block takes effect at once. **Provider cache** (`recommendations_cache.json`, per provider, keyed to the account): fresh for 1 h (`FRESH_MS`), shown while it refreshes for 7 days (`STALE_MS`); a failure keeps the last answer so an outage never empties the page; `PluginNotReadyException` (a `notReady` plugin error) drops it; an empty answer replaces the stored one but is never served from the cache. **Paging:** one request per paging key at a time, at most `EXPANSION_PAGE_CAP` (10) pages; an empty page or a cursor that doesn't move ends it. **Release dates** (`ReleaseDate.kt`, `ReleaseDates`, `release_dates_cache.json`): Coming badges and years come from MusicBrainz, asked only for the albums a screen shows, cached 7 days. **Covers and photos** (`RecommendationImages`, desktop's `recommendationImages`): an album or artist card that came without an image asks for one as it comes on screen (the repository's `cardShown`, which gathers the cards shown within 300 ms and asks for the albums' release dates and every card's image together; an open row's grid composes one line at a time, so only the lines on screen ask) (`ImageEnrichmentService.lookupAlbumArt`, the artist-photo chain `MetadataService.getArtistImage`), one lookup per album or artist at a time, kept in memory for the session; a lookup that threw stores nothing and is asked again, and each rebuild applies what was found. **Listeners** (`SimilarUsersStore`, `similar_users_cache.json`): people from the plugins with `similarUsers` and `friends` (their `getSimilarUsers`) that aren't in `recommendations_off_resolvers`, cached 7 days; an empty answer is fresh, and they show at once from the cache before the refresh. **Shuffleupagus** is asked when it has no cached answer, at most once every 30 minutes on a plain load (`AI_RETRY_MS`); Try again (a forced load) asks at once. **History mixes** (Home's "Made from your listening": Daily Jams and Weekly Jams from the ListenBrainz plugin, and Apple Music's play-history mixes such as Heavy Rotation and Replay from the Apple Music plugin) come from the plugins with `capabilities.historyMixes` through the shared `HistoryMixesRepository` (`history_mixes_cache.json`, the same per-provider and per-account cache rules and 20 s bound, plus Home's 10-minute throttle, `shouldLoadHomeRecommendations`); the Recommendations page loads them alongside its own load on both apps (`loadRecommendationsPage`; Try again forces both), since it needs their ids even when Home hasn't been visited, and every mix id goes to `excludePlaylistIds`, so the Playlists row leaves them out (Weekly Exploration stays), and a mix with no cover gets a mosaic of its tracks' covers, looking up an album's cover or an artist's photo for tracks that carry none (`historyMixMosaicCovers`, retried 60 s later, 3 tries; at most `HOME_MIX_ALBUM_ART_LOOKUPS` (12) album covers per mix, so one coverless mix can't hold the MusicBrainz 1 req/s queue: desktop has no cap).
