**Last.fm has no follow API.** Last.fm deprecated `user.addFriend` / `user.deleteFriend` in 2018 (returns `Method "user.addFriend" is deprecated`). Local-only add / remove. The Last.fm-side state is whatever's already in the user's Last.fm follows; we can't change it via the API.

**Removal stickiness — `deletedFriendKeys` allowlist.** Last.fm has no unfollow API, so a removed Last.fm friend would re-appear on the next `syncFriendsFromServices` cycle from the user's still-active Last.fm follows. `SettingsStore.deletedFriendKeys` (CSV-encoded `Set<String>` in `KvStore` under `DELETED_FRIEND_KEYS`) carries the allowlist. `removeFriend` adds `"<service>:<username.lowercase>"` to the set; `addFriend` removes it; `syncFriendsFromServices` checks before re-inserting. Applied to **BOTH services** (LB too — even though LB has a working unfollow, a user-removed friend shouldn't sneak back through any periodic re-sync path).

**A hidden Last.fm profile is a state, not an error (desktop parachord#1140).** With "Hide recent listening information" on, `user.getRecentTracks` answers 403 + error 17 to everyone but the owner (signing doesn't help; top tracks/artists/loved still answer). `LastFmClient.getUserRecentTracks` throws `LastFmHiddenListeningException` for it; `FriendsRepository` records `Friend.hidesListening` (the `friends.hidesListening` column, set on 403/17, cleared by the next successful fetch, written only on change). Friend cards show "Listening hidden" instead of a stale track, and the friend's Recent tab says why instead of "No recent listening activity".

**Last.fm refresh log-spam guard.** Every 2-minute `refreshAllActivity` cycle fans out one `getUserRecentTracks` call per friend. With 60+ Last.fm friends, the rate-limit gate trips after a handful of calls and every remaining call throws `LastFmRateLimitedException` synchronously. The catch in `refreshFriendActivity` demotes that specific exception to debug, and `refreshAllActivity` tracks a per-cycle flag so subsequent Last.fm calls skip without launching coroutines that immediately throw. ListenBrainz friends are unaffected (separate API + separate gate).

**Key files:** `shared/.../repository/FriendsRepository.kt#followOnService` / `unfollowOnService` / `syncFriendsFromServices` / `refreshAllActivity`, `shared/.../settings/SettingsStore.kt` (the `DELETED_FRIEND_KEYS` family).

### Cross-Resolver Enrichment (Slow Trickle)

Local-files-only tracks never get streaming-service IDs (Spotify / Apple Music / SoundCloud) populated through the normal playback or sync paths. Without intervention they never feed Achordion's match cache either — the contribution loop is broken for the ~30% of users whose libraries are mostly local files. Mirrors desktop's "Cross-Resolver Enrichment (Eager Gate + Slow Trickle)".

**Algorithm.** A background job runs `CrossResolverEnrichmentService.runOnce()` from `shared/.../enrichment/` every 24 h: Android's `WorkManager` periodic `CrossResolverEnrichmentWorker`, iOS's `BGProcessingTask` `com.parachord.ios.enrichment` (`BackgroundSync.handleEnrichment`). Each run:

1. SQL query selects up to **20** localfiles tracks (`resolver = 'localfiles'`) that are missing at least one streaming-service ID column AND haven't been visited within the **30-day cooldown** (`crossResolverEnrichedAt IS NULL OR < cutoff`). Never-tried tracks sort first.
2. For each candidate (with a **3-second** gap between):
   - If `recordingMbid` is null → call `MbidEnrichmentService.enrichTrack(...)` synchronously and re-read.
   - Call the resolver cascade (Android `ResolverManager.resolveWithHints`, iOS `IosContainer.resolveSources`) for Spotify / Apple Music / SoundCloud matches, keeping per service its best source at ≥ 0.95 (`ResolvedSources.fromSources`, shared).
   - Persist any **new** IDs additively to the track row — never overwrite an existing populated ID. Both the Kotlin filter AND the existing `backfillResolverIds` SQL enforce the null-only-update invariant.
   - If `recordingMbid` is now non-null AND we have ≥1 high-confidence (≥0.95) streaming ID, POST to Achordion's `/api/track-links/submit` via `AchordionClient.submitTrackLinks`.
3. Stamp `crossResolverEnrichedAt = now` **regardless of outcome** so un-findable tracks aren't thrashed every cycle.

**Constraints.** Idle-priority background work — never runs on a metered connection or low battery. Android: WorkManager requires `NetworkType.UNMETERED` + `requiresBatteryNotLow = true`, `KEEP` policy on `enqueueUniquePeriodicWork` so re-enabling on app start is idempotent. iOS can't require Wi-Fi of a `BGProcessingTask` (only `requiresNetworkConnectivity`), so the handler checks and skips the run on an expensive or constrained path (cellular, hotspot, Low Data Mode), in Low Power Mode, or under 20% battery off the charger. When iOS runs it is up to iOS (usually idle and charging); the request asks for no sooner than 24 h after the last launch or run. A background launch has no window, so the handler starts `AppBootstrap` first for the `.axe` plugins.

**Shared.** `CrossResolverEnrichmentService` is `shared/commonMain` and generic over the resolver via a `suspend (title, artist) -> ResolvedSources` lambda. The Android Koin binding closes over `ResolverManager.resolveWithHints`, iOS's (`IosContainer.crossResolverEnrichmentService`) over `resolveSources`.

**Key files:** `shared/.../enrichment/CrossResolverEnrichmentService.kt`, `app/.../enrichment/CrossResolverEnrichmentWorker.kt`, `app/.../enrichment/CrossResolverEnrichmentScheduler.kt`, `iosApp/Parachord/BackgroundSync.swift`.

### In-App Announcements

Achordion + desktop already share an `announcements:json` Upstash row that drives in-app banner notifications (outage notices, launch messaging, Discord-invite pushes, etc.). Android consumes the same feed.

**Endpoints.**
- `GET https://achordion.xyz/api/announcements` — public, no auth, cached `s-maxage=60`. Returns the full validated server list.
- `POST https://achordion.xyz/api/announcements/event` — public, no auth, per-IP rate-limited (60/min). Body: `{ id, event: "view" | "dismiss" | "cta-click" }`. Best-effort telemetry; failures are swallowed at debug level.

**Client-side filtering.** The server returns everything; clients filter:

- **Surface match** — `surfaces == null || surfaces.isEmpty() || "parachord" in surfaces`. The string is `"parachord"`, not `"parachord-mobile"` — matches desktop.
- **Version range** — `appVersion >= minVersion` AND `appVersion <= maxVersion`. Semver compare via `compareSemverOrNull`. Unparseable bounds fail-open (don't crash on a malformed version string).
- **Not expired** — `expiresAt == null || Clock.System.now() < Instant.parse(expiresAt)`. Unparseable timestamps fail-open.
- **Not dismissed** — `id` not in the persisted `announcements_dismissed_ids` set (CSV in `KvStore`).

**Polling cadence.**
- **Cold start** — `ParachordApplication.onCreate` fires `repo.refreshNow()` fire-and-forget.
- **Foreground resume** — `MainActivity.onResume` calls `repo.refreshIfStale()`, which short-circuits unless `now - announcements_last_fetched_ms >= 6h`.

**Banner UI.** Top of `HomeScreen`. Severity-themed background (`error` red, `warn` amber, `success` green, `info`/unknown brand purple). Light/dark variants via `ParachordTheme.isDark`. `LaunchedEffect(id)` fires the `view` event once per session per id. CTA opens `Intent.ACTION_VIEW` (defensive try/catch + toast fallback) and fires `cta-click`. Dismiss X persists the id + fires `dismiss` + removes from the StateFlow.

**KMP-ready.** `AchordionClient.listAnnouncements` / `trackAnnouncementEvent` + `AnnouncementsRepository` live in `shared/commonMain`. iOS shell needs its own SwiftUI banner component when it lands.

**Key files:** `shared/.../api/AchordionClient.kt` (the two endpoint methods + `Announcement`/`Cta` types), `shared/.../repository/AnnouncementsRepository.kt`, `app/.../ui/components/AnnouncementBanner.kt`, `app/.../ui/screens/home/HomeScreen.kt`.

### Apple Music Catalog Throttle (`MusicKitCatalogLimiter`)

Apple Music's catalog API (`api.music.apple.com/v1/catalog/{storefront}/...`) is aggressively throttled per dev-token / IP. Once a burst trips the throttle, subsequent calls — including the `/songs/{id}` lookup that playback does internally — fail with `MusicDataRequest.Error 1`, killing the active play path too. Mirrors desktop's `nativeMusicKitLimiter`.

**One limiter app-wide** covers every catalog call: the shared `AppleMusicCatalog` (the resolver's catalog search on both platforms, and the plugin host's Apple Music hooks) and two `MusicKitWebBridge` calls that wrap their JS `evaluate()` in `catalogLimiter.runThrottled`:

- `getPlaylist(playlistId)` — used by Apple Music playlist import
- `preload(songId)` — fired per-track during background source enrichment

**Limiter knobs** (`MusicKitCatalogLimiter`, `shared/commonMain` since #241; Android's old path is a typealias):

- Concurrency cap: 3 in-flight via `Semaphore`
- Inter-request gap: 150 ms minimum, mutex-protected so simultaneous permit-grabs space themselves
- Circuit breaker: 3 consecutive rate-limit signals trip a 5-minute cooldown. In-cooldown calls short-circuit returning null without hitting the bridge. **Time-bound, NOT session-permanent** — one transient throttle shouldn't disable Apple Music for the session.
- Rate-limit detection via `looksLikeRateLimit(errorString)` — matches `"MusicDataRequest.Error 1"`, `"429"`, `"rate-limit"`, `"too many requests"`.

**iOS uses the same class (#241).** iOS has no MusicKit JS bridge; `AppleMusicCatalog.get` (shared) reads the catalog over plain HTTP, and every attempt goes through the limiter. A `429` counts as a strike and a `2xx` resets the count. During a cooldown the fetch returns null without calling Apple, so the Apple Music source is skipped instead of re-poking a throttled catalog. Unthrottled, a list resolve tripped the throttle and Spotify won over a higher-ranked Apple Music (#232). The one 600 ms retry from #232 stays, inside the limiter. The clock is injectable (`nowMs`), so `MusicKitCatalogLimiterTest` (commonTest, JVM + iOS) covers cooldown expiry.

**Key files:** `shared/.../api/MusicKitCatalogLimiter.kt`, `shared/.../api/AppleMusicCatalog.kt`, `app/.../playback/handlers/MusicKitWebBridge.kt#getPlaylist` / `preload`.

### Apple Music resolution — the shared catalog search

Both platforms resolve Apple Music through `AppleMusicCatalogResolver` (`shared/.../resolver/`): one catalog `songs` search (`/v1/catalog/{storefront}/search?types=songs&limit=10`, term "artist title") through `AppleMusicCatalog` with the developer token, then `selectBestMatch` (an exact ISRC first, #402). The source carries the catalog id, the ISRC, the song's music.apple.com page as `url`, the length and a 600 px cover. Android used to search through the MusicKit JS WebView, which is still configuring when Home resolves at launch and is the same instance that plays audio; the catalog GET needs neither MusicKit nor the user's token.

- **Four outcomes** (`AppleMusicCatalogLookup`): `Found`; `NoMatch` (the catalog answered with no songs: a real miss, iTunes isn't asked, as in the plugin host's `appleMusicSearchWithMusicKit`); `NotConnected` (no Music User Token: Apple Music isn't offered, #327, logged once); `Unavailable` (no developer token, the limiter cooling down, a failed request). Only `Unavailable` sends Android on to the plugin's `resolve` and then iTunes; iOS has no fallback.
- **The developer token is read on every call.** On Android it's `SettingsStore.getAppleMusicDeveloperToken()` (the one saved in Settings while current, else the build's), the same token MusicKit JS uses; iOS passes the build's.
- **Storefront:** the saved one when it's a two-letter code, else `us`.
- **Tests:** `AppleMusicCatalogResolverTest`, `AppleMusicCatalogTest` (commonTest, JVM + iOS).

### Spotify Rate-Limit Gate — Abuse-Ban Remediation (shared)

Spotify rate-limits the whole **account/`client_id`**, and Parachord's Android + iOS apps **share one `client_id`** — so a burst from either client penalizes both, and a 429 isn't a per-endpoint event. The shared `RateLimitGate` (`shared/.../api/RateLimitGate.kt`) fronts `SpotifyClient` (and `LastFmClient`, `MusicBrainzClient`) with: a persisted cooldown (survives process restart via `loadCooldownEpochMs`/`saveCooldownEpochMs`), a concurrency cap (2), and a 150 ms inter-request gap.

**The hard-won lesson (June 2026 incident):** Spotify's *abuse-mode* ban is server-side and **far outlasts the `Retry-After` it advertises** — observed still 429ing **12+ hours later** despite a quiet network overnight. A flat 1 h cooldown is therefore a trap: every time the local cooldown lapses, the next call (app open, background sync) re-pokes the still-banned account and Spotify **re-extends** its window — so it never clears. There is **no reliable way to read the true ban length**; `Retry-After` is a floor, not the truth, and the only ground-truth "is it clear?" test is a successful request, which itself re-pokes. The ban only decays with **sustained silence**.

**Escalating circuit breaker (`escalateOnRepeat`, opt-in).** On each CONSECUTIVE 429 the gate **doubles** the backoff (`base * 2^(strikes-1)`, capped at `maxCooldownMs`); the first non-rate-limited response resets the strike counter. **SpotifyClient opts in with a 6 h cap (1h→2h→4h→6h)**; LastFm/MusicBrainz keep the flat default (`escalateOnRepeat = false`). After a few consecutive strikes the local cooldown exceeds Spotify's window, so the app stops poking and the ban can decay. Background sync workers (`LibrarySyncWorker` hourly, etc.) inherit this for free — they short-circuit during the now-longer cooldown. The gate takes an injectable `nowMs` clock so the cooldown is unit-testable (`RateLimitGateTest`).

**Two write-path rules that earned the bans (don't regress):**
1. **Write/mutation methods MUST route through the gate** (`gatedSend`), not raw `httpClient.put/post/delete`. A 429 on a write has to arm the same persisted cooldown a read does, and a write must not fire mid-cooldown to re-arm the abuse window (#176). Only the interactive playback-control PUTs (`play`/`pause`/`seek`/`volume`/`transferPlayback`) and `getDevices` stay ungated — the user must control playback during a throttle; they honor the cooldown advisorily via `rateLimitRemainingMs()`.
2. **`ResolverManager.resolveWithHints` must not re-search Spotify when the ID is already known.** When a `spotifyId` hint exists, `verifySpotifyTrack` (a gated `getTrack`) already yields the source *with artwork*; running the full `resolve()` cascade additionally fired a Spotify **search** per track and then **discarded** it — doubling Spotify calls for every cached track and never tapering. `resolveWithHints` now excludes `"spotify"` from the cascade (`resolve(..., excludeResolvers)`) once it has an authoritative Spotify source. **And on a 429 during verify, keep the known Spotify ID as a source** (confidence 1.0) instead of dropping it — otherwise a transient rate-limit erases the Spotify badge across an entire list (the "all Spotify badges vanished on reconnect" symptom). `verifySpotifyTrack` rethrows `SpotifyRateLimitedException` so the caller can fall back to the cached ID (#182). AM/SC hints still run their cascade search for artwork — Spotify is the rate-limit offender.

**Still deferred:** visibility-scope the ~13 remaining bulk-resolving screens (the broader "don't burst" fix, #177 parity / #181). DB-backed screens already pass hints so rule 2 cuts their Spotify volume; the eager whole-list submit is most dangerous for ephemeral metadata-only lists.

**iOS parity.** `RateLimitGate` + `SpotifyClient` are shared, so iOS inherits the escalating breaker and `gatedSend`. iOS's own resolver coordinator (`IosResolverCoordinator`) and Spotify Connect flow must mirror rule 2 (skip the redundant search; keep the badge on 429) and honor `rateLimitRemainingMs()` before poking. See `iosApp/AGENTS.md`.

**Key files:** `shared/.../api/RateLimitGate.kt` (`escalateOnRepeat`, `nowMs`), `shared/.../api/SpotifyClient.kt` (gate opt-in, `gatedSend`, `gatedGet`), `app/.../resolver/ResolverManager.kt` (`resolveWithHints`, `verifySpotifyTrack`, `resolve(excludeResolvers)`), `shared/.../api/RateLimitGateTest.kt`, `app/.../resolver/ResolverManagerHintsTest.kt`.

### ListenBrainz Weekly Playlists

Weekly Jams and Weekly Exploration come from the ListenBrainz plugin's recommendation sections: an item with `weekly` (shared `WeeklyInfo`: id, `contextType` `weekly-jam` / `weekly-exploration`, label, week label, date). Mobile sends no `createdfor` request of its own.

**Weekly Jams is a history mix** (see "History mixes"): the ListenBrainz plugin sends it as one, Home shows it in "Made from your listening" and can offer it in Jump back in, and the Recommendations Playlists row leaves it out with every other mix id. Weekly Exploration is not a mix and stays in that row. Home has no Weekly carousels any more.

**The header comes from the sections (desktop's `weeklyHomeEntries`).** The shared `weeklyPlaylistHeader(sections, id)` (`shared/.../recommendations/WeeklyPlaylistHeader.kt`) finds the playlist and gives its title (the label), week label, date ("Jun 15, 2026", `weeklyDateLabel`) and blurb: `WEEKLY_DESCRIPTIONS[contextType]`, then ", curated by" and the item's own source name, else its resolver id, else "your service". It reads `RecommendationPageState.weeklySections`, the page built before the history-mix exclusion (desktop's `buildRecommendationViews`), so Weekly Jams keep a header after the page drops them. **A view opened before the page carries the playlist** (a cold "Playing from", a link) waits for it, as desktop does: both apps watch the page and fill the header in when it first appears (Android `WeeklyPlaylistViewModel.watchHeader`, iOS `IosContainer.watchWeeklyPlaylistHeader`). Until then Android shows the kind label alone (`weeklyKindLabel`: "Weekly Jam" / "Weekly Exploration") and iOS the title its route passed (empty for a `navPlaylist` link).

**Tracks** are fetched by playlist MBID through `WeeklyPlaylistsRepository.loadPlaylistTracks` (`GET /1/playlist/{id}`), on both apps, for the Recommendations cards' covers and the weekly views.

**The views.** Android: a weekly card opens `WeeklyPlaylistScreen` (an ephemeral view with Save; its header from `WeeklyPlaylistViewModel`). It supports standard track context menus (long-press) via `TrackContextMenuHost`: Play Next, Add to Queue, Add to Playlist, Go to Artist/Album, and the collection toggle. iOS: `PlaylistDetailView` (`PCRoute.playlist`), with the same header through `IosContainer.weeklyPlaylistHeader` (`IosWeeklyHeader`: `headline`, `blurb`).

Ephemeral playlists use the ID format `listenbrainz-{playlistMbid}` when saved. **Save names each week's copy apart** (shared `weeklySavedName`): "<label> · <date>", e.g. "Weekly Jams · Nov 17, 2026", or today's date when the week has none; with no header, "<kind> · <today>" ("Weekly playlist" when the kind isn't known). Sync matches playlists by name (`findCrossProviderNameMatch`, the push's name dedup, `migrateMergeCrossProviderDuplicates`), so a name shared across weeks, or with the user's own "Weekly Jams" on a service, could be merged. The relative week label ("This Week") is never used, since it repeats every week. Desktop saves under the plain label.

The shared page model (`RecommendationPage.kt`, see "Recommendations come from the plugins") puts Weekly Exploration's newest week in the Playlists row it builds, which the Recommendations page draws on both apps; Weekly Jams goes through the history-mix exclusion and stays out of that row (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-19-home-and-recommendations-design.md`).

### Playlist freshness (Home, desktop's `playlist-freshness.js`)

`playlists.tracksChangedAt` (nullable) and `playlists.lastChangeAdded` (how many tracks that change added) say when a playlist's tracks last changed. `lastModified` can't, since a rename or a sync-state stamp bumps it. Both are local only: no sync push sends them.

- **Where they are set:** inside `PlaylistTrackDao`, so every caller gets it: `insertAll`, `replaceAll` and `deleteTrack`. `PlaylistFreshness` compares the track keys before and after: artist + title folded by `slug` (not the id: hosted XSPF tracks have none, and ListenBrainz ids are positions). The DAO passes no id, so a row with neither artist nor title keys to an empty string (`trackKey` has an id fallback, unused there). Equal keys, in order, stamp nothing; a re-poll or a rename that brings the same tracks changes nothing. `lastChangeAdded` counts copies (`countAdded`).
- **`deleteByPlaylistId` never stamps.** It is the first half of a playlist delete or a rewrite, and a rewrite that brings the same tracks must not look like a change. So code that rewrote a playlist by deleting and inserting now calls `replaceAll`, which diffs once. Don't go back to delete-then-insert.
- **`PlaylistDao.insert` keeps the newer stamp** when it replaces a row, so a save that carries an older copy of the playlist can't erase one.
- **Both apps migrate the two columns at bind time** (Android `AndroidModule`, iOS `DriverFactory`'s `addColumnIfMissing`).
- **Reading them:** `playlistFreshness(p)` is `tracksChangedAt`, else `lastModified`, else `createdAt`; `freshestPlaylists` takes the newest 6; `changeBadge` gives "Δ N" for a change in the last 7 days that added tracks. These are in `shared/.../home/HomePlaylists.kt`.

### Playlist duplicates — the banner (both apps)

A playlist page shows a banner while the playlist holds later copies of a song ("3 duplicate tracks in this playlist", **Remove duplicates**); the action asks first ("Remove 3 duplicate tracks?") and keeps the first copy of each song in place. The rule is shared `PlaylistDuplicates` (`shared/.../playlist/PlaylistDuplicates.kt`): "the same song" is sync's identity, `unifyTrackKeys` over one list (the same ISRC or recording MBID, else the same artist and title with a trailing remaster tag ignored; live, remix and "feat." titles stay apart; two different ISRCs under one name stay two recordings; a blank row is never a duplicate). `offersRemoval` hides it on a hosted XSPF (its feed is the master copy). The words are `PlaylistDuplicateCopy`. `LibraryRepository.removePlaylistDuplicates` saves the cleaned list as an ordinary edit (`replaceAll`, `lastModified` now, `locallyModified` when the playlist syncs), so the next sync pushes it to the mirrors and, as a real local edit, to the pull source (#802); Apple Music can't remove tracks, so an Apple Music copy keeps its duplicates. Android: `PlaylistDetailViewModel.duplicateCount` / `removeDuplicates`, the banner under the remote-update one in `PlaylistDetailScreen`. iOS: `SavedPlaylistModel.duplicateCount` (through `IosContainer.playlistDuplicateCount` / `removePlaylistDuplicates`), the banner under the top bar in `SavedPlaylistDetailView`, hidden while editing. Desktop has no such action.

### History mixes (Home's "Made from your listening")

Plugins that declare `capabilities.historyMixes` answer `getHistoryMixes(config)` with playlists: the ListenBrainz plugin's Daily Jams and Weekly Jams, and the Apple Music plugin's play-history mixes (Heavy Rotation, Replay, Your Essentials, Favorites; from the catalog's personal recommendations, so only with a Music User Token). `HistoryMixesRepository` (`shared/.../repository/`) asks each one through the resolver-loader and caches the answers in `history_mixes_cache.json`, per provider and keyed to the account. Fresh for 1 h (`FRESH_MS`), shown while it refreshes for 7 days (`STALE_MS`), 20 s per provider (`PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS`). An empty answer is stored but never served from the cache; a failure keeps the last answer; `PluginNotReadyException` drops it. The pure rules are in `shared/.../home/HistoryMixes.kt`: `normalizeHistoryMixes` keeps only items with an id, a name and a URL; `flattenHistoryMixes` deals the providers round-robin so one service can't fill the row, keeping an id where it first appeared; a mix that came with no cover gets a 2x2 mosaic (`mixesNeedingMosaic`, `withMixMosaics`), retried 60 s later, 3 tries. The covers come from `lookupPlaylistCovers`, a port of desktop's `getPlaylistCovers`: a track's own cover, else its album's (`ImageEnrichmentService.lookupAlbumArt`, once per album), else its artist's photo (`MetadataService.getArtistImage`, once per artist), distinct by album and URL; both apps wire the two lookups into `historyMixMosaicCovers`. No covers because a lookup failed (offline, rate limited) throws, so it's retried like a failed link lookup; no covers from lookups that all answered keeps the placeholder for the session. ListenBrainz's mixes carry no covers, so Weekly Jams' mosaic comes entirely from these lookups. One difference from desktop: a cover a track already carries is used even when the track names no album.

The Recommendations page loads the mixes with its own load on both apps (`loadRecommendationsPage`), because it needs their ids even when Home hasn't been visited, and passes every id to `excludePlaylistIds` so its Playlists row leaves them out.

### Home — the shared model, drawn on both apps

Home's rules are in `shared/.../home/`, ports of desktop's `home-model.js`, `home-page.js` and their neighbours, with vectors in the matching `commonTest` files. **`HomeSession`** (`shared/.../home/HomeSession.kt`) gathers a platform's `HomeSources` into one `HomeState` through those rules and runs Home's loads; both apps draw it. The shared `RepositoryHomeSources` supplies the inputs on both (Android's Koin binding, `IosContainer.homeSources`), and the shared `HomeQueues` builds what each tap plays. **Android:** `HomeViewModel` wraps the session, and `HomeScreen.kt`, `HomeHeader.kt`, `HomeJumpBackIn.kt`, `HomeRows.kt`, `HomeLowerSections.kt` and `HomeGettingStarted.kt` only draw. **iOS:** the bridge `IosHomePage.kt` holds one app-wide `IosHomeSession` (`IosContainer.homeSession`) and flattens `HomeState` into plain DTOs; `HomeModel.swift` watches it and `HomeScreen.swift`, `HomeHeader.swift`, `HomeJumpBackIn.swift`, `HomeRows.swift`, `HomeLowerSections.swift` and `HomeGettingStarted.swift` only draw. The coordinator pushes what's playing every second through `IosContainer.updateHomePlayback`. Which sections show is the shared `homeSectionVisibility` (`HomeRows.kt`) on both: Android reads it directly, iOS gets its flags on the DTO, so neither keeps an "is this empty" test in a view. `IosContainer.historyMixesRepository` has the mosaic lookups, `IosContainer.concertArtistImages` and `askShuffleupagus` feed the concerts and Ask. See `iosApp/AGENTS.md` → "Home: drawn from the shared session". A rule changed here belongs in desktop first.

- **What Home shows, top to bottom (both apps):** the Apple Music reauth, Spotify expiry and Spotify reauth banners, then one announcement (`homeBannerItems`, its view reported once). Until `HomeState.ready` (the plugins have loaded, at most `GETTING_STARTED_DECIDE_TIMEOUT_MS`) a skeleton. With the getting-started layout on: only the layout and "Explore while you set up" (the Critical Darlings and Pop of the Tops halves). Otherwise: the header (the greeting by local hour, "Play Collection Radio", the "Finish setting up · N of M" pill when getting started was skipped), Jump back in (at most 4 tiles in a 2x2 grid; the import card for a user with nothing), "Made from your listening" (Recent Loves first under All, then the history mixes, a chip per source), "Your playlists, freshest first", "Albums you might love" (a chip per source, Shuffleupagus's picks with their reason badge), the Fresh Drops / Critical Darlings halves stacked full width (Pop of the Tops takes Fresh Drops' half while Fresh Drops has nothing; its failed load shows "Couldn’t load Pop of the Tops" with Try again; each feed starts loading, `HomeFeedLoader`, so its half shows a skeleton until the first answer), "Concerts near you" (only with a concert location and at least one show; nothing while loading, as desktop) and "Ask Shuffleupagus". Rows scroll horizontally with standard-width cards (Android `LazyRow`s of 140 dp, `RecCardWidth`), 6 per row and 3 per half; every play button over art is the shared white disc (Android `ArtPlayButton`, iOS `PCArtPlayButton`). Removed from Home (both apps): Continue Listening, Recently added, the Discover grid, the Weekly carousels, Friend activity, AI artist suggestions, collection stats, the old Recent loves list and the welcome / scan card.
- **Taps:** a Jump back in tile opens its page and its Play plays it (each under the shared `jumpTileContext`: a playlist by id, Recent Loves as a `loves` queue, an album in `CatalogQueues.album`'s form, a mix through the registry's link lookup as a `mix` queue whose id is the mix's link, so the tile leaves Jump back in while it plays and Now Playing's "Playing from" opens the mix's service-playlist view (a mix queue saved with an older, non-link id opens nothing; desktop keeps the mix id, parachord#1592); what each tap plays is the shared `HomeQueues`, which Android's `HomeViewModel` plays through; the context types are `HomeModel.kt`'s `PLAYLIST_CONTEXT`, `LOVES_CONTEXT`, `ALBUM_CONTEXT`, `MIX_CONTEXT`, and `COLLECTION_CONTEXT` for Collection Radio; Now Playing's "Playing from" on a `loves` queue opens Collection's songs sorted Recently Added, as the Recent Loves tile does, and on a `collection` queue Collection's songs in the user's sort (the shared `playbackContextDestination` → `ContextDestination.Collection(recentLoves)`, desktop's queue drawer); a mix whose lookup fails or finds no tracks shows desktop's "Could not load <mix>", and any load that throws (a playlist read, the catalog, the collection) shows "Could not load <name>" on both apps, `HomeQueues` turning it into `HomeQueue.Failed`); Recent Loves opens Collection's songs sorted Recently Added (a love dates the row) (`Routes.collection(tab, trackSort)`, desktop's `setCollectionSort({ tracks: 'recent' })`), applied once per back-stack entry on Android (`LibraryViewModel`'s `takeLinkTrackSort`, through the entry's `SavedStateHandle`), so coming Back to it or restoring the tab never overrides a sort the user picked since, and on iOS for that visit only (`CollectionModel.applyLinkTrackSort`, never saved, dropped by the person's own pick or by leaving the tab); a half's card opens its album; a concert card opens the artist's On Tour tab and its Tickets the seller's page; Ask sends its prompt straight to the chat (`HomeViewModel.ask` → `DeepLinkViewModel.handleInAppChatPrompt`, capped by `ChatPrompt.cap`, nothing for a blank field). Album and playlist cards have their long-press menus.
- **What both apps compare or build is shared, not decided in Swift:** a row's source chips (`homeSourceChips`: "All" first with no colour, then a chip per source) and an album card's key (`homeAlbumCardKey`), a banner's colour role (`bannerRole`: error, warning, success, info), the setup item that reopens getting started (`SETUP_GETTING_STARTED`) and the getting-started step ids (`GETTING_STARTED_CONNECT` / `_MUSIC` / `_LISTEN`). The iOS bridge's tile, half and half-body kinds are `const val`s in `IosHomePage.kt` (`JUMP_KIND_*`, `HALF_KIND_*`, `HALF_BODY_*`) that the Swift views compare against.
- **Covers Home looks up itself:** an album Jump back in tile with no cover (`JumpTileArtLookup`, desktop Home's `requestArt`), and, a mobile addition, a half card its feed finished without a cover (`ShownAlbumArtLookup`, keyed `halfCardArtKey`, applied by `withHalfCardArt`). Desktop's Home leaves Fresh Drops' and Critical Darlings' covers to their feeds plus `reportBroken`; on the phone such cards showed initials, so Home asks for them, but only once the feed has stopped loading (`halfCardNeedsArt(card, feedLoading)`), since the feeds look their covers up on the same MusicBrainz 1 req/s queue while they load. Each album is asked once a session; a lookup that threw is asked again. Recommended albums ask through the Recommendations repository's `cardShown`.
- **Fresh Drops holds one row per release** (`uniqueReleases`, desktop's `fresh-drops-releases.js`): a collaboration comes back once per artist on it, under different credits but one release-group id, so the merge keeps the first copy by id, else by artist + title, on every merge and on the cache load.
- **`HomeModel.kt`:** `jumpBackInTiles` (up to four tiles in the order playlist, loves, album, mix; each takes its first candidate that isn't playing or queued, `isActiveContext`), `latestLoveAlbum`, `homeSections` (which sections show; the import card is for a user with no collection and no playlists), `homeTopArtists`, `lovesMosaicAlbums`, and the Ask prompt: `promptName` cleans a name the way desktop does (JavaScript's whitespace set, not Kotlin's; unsafe characters dropped by code point range, since Kotlin/Native rejects some Unicode regex classes) and `exampleShufflePrompt` builds the example. **Source filter and album art:** `filterBySource` and `albumsNeedingArt` exist twice. The Recommendations page's versions (`shared/.../recommendations/RecommendationPage.kt`, over `RecommendationItem`) are the ones in use; `HomeModel.kt` holds the generic `<T>` implementations, and Recommendations' versions delegate to them, so a change to the rule goes in `HomeModel.kt` only. **Recommendations throttle:** a full load that finished less than 10 minutes ago keeps Home from asking again (`HOME_RECOMMENDATIONS_THROTTLE_MS`, `shouldLoadHomeRecommendations`); it counts only if some provider answered and a section resulted (`shouldStampHomeRecommendationsLoad`).
- **`HomePlaylists.kt`:** the playlist rules above, plus `whenPhrase`, `freshChangeLine` ("X picked up 3 new tracks this morning") and `playlistSubtitle` ("Refreshed 2h ago"; no "Since <year>" line on mobile). `recentLoves` now puts loves with no date last instead of first.
- **`TimeAgo`** (`shared/.../format/TimeAgo.kt`): "just now", "18m ago", "3h ago", "5d ago", "2mo ago", "1y ago", desktop's `time-ago.js`. It is separate from `RelativeTime`, which keeps its own ladder for listens.
- **Getting started and setup:** `GettingStarted.kt` (`stepOnePlugins`, `gettingStartedStatus`: connect, music, listen; `showGettingStarted`; `gettingStartedLeft` for the "Finish getting started" pill), `HomeSetup.kt` (`homeSetupItems`, the "Finish setting up" checklist, whose only item here is the skipped steps: mobile has no browser extension and no What's New) and `HomeBanners.kt` (`homeBannerItems`, `bannerViewId`, over the filtered announcements). The remembered state is `SettingsStore`'s `home_getting_started` (`GettingStartedState`: `active`, `played`, `skipped`) and `home_setup_dismissed`. With no saved state the layout is on for a profile that has no service connected and no music; desktop uses its tutorial flag there, which mobile lacks. `PluginConfigField` now carries `required`, `advanced` and `hasDefault` from the manifest, which the setup screens read. **The layout** (desktop `home-getting-started.js`): `GettingStartedLayout.kt` holds what it draws (`GETTING_STARTED_STEPS`, `openGettingStartedStep`: the current step until another is tapped; each step's Done / Next / Not yet; each tile's text and label; `exploreHalves`, Critical Darlings and Pop of the Tops, a failed Pop of the Tops keeping its half) and `HomeCopy` its words. Android draws it in `HomeGettingStarted.kt`, the steps stacked with the open one's panel under its header; it leaves out desktop's "Sound quality" (and that clause of the order note) and the phone-apps note. Every step-1 tile and each "Listening history" tile opens that plug-in's Settings sheet; "More plug-ins" and "Set the order" open Plug-Ins; "Sync your library" opens Settings on the Sync tab (`SYNC_LIBRARY_SETTINGS_TAB`, the shared `SettingsTab` names; Android `Routes.settings(pluginId, tab)`). Step 2 lists the scrobbling plug-ins (`scrobblerPlugins`, `capabilities.scrobbling`) with their accounts (`HomeSources.scrobblerAccounts`) and is done once a loaded one has an account, as desktop's `scrobblerStates` (a username saved for a plug-in that isn't loaded doesn't count), and step 3 up to three playlists, freshest first (`gettingStartedPlaylists`), each opening its page. Counts in its text go through the shared `countOf` ("1 track", "1,204 tracks"). Not built yet: desktop's skeleton for the layout (the page's skeleton covers it).
- **Concerts near you and Ask's chat origin:** the next two sections. Ask's errors use desktop's AI error wording, and the chat shares desktop's message layout (`ChatContent`).

### Album Context Menus

Every album card across the app supports long-press context menus via the shared `AlbumContextMenu` component (`ui/components/AlbumContextMenu.kt`). This is an always-dark `ModalBottomSheet` with consistent styling.

**Pattern for adding album context menus to a screen:**
1. Import `AlbumContextMenu` and `hapticCombinedClickable`
2. Change the album card's modifier from `hapticClickable` to `hapticCombinedClickable(onClick = ..., onLongClick = ...)`
3. Add `var showMenu by remember { mutableStateOf(false) }` state per item
4. Show `AlbumContextMenu` when `showMenu` is true

**AlbumContextMenu callbacks** — all optional except `onToggleCollection`:
- `onPlayAlbum` — play the album immediately (every album menu offers it, as on iOS; outside Library through the ViewModel's `playAlbumByName`, shared `CatalogQueues.album`)
- `onQueueAlbum` — add album tracks to the playback queue (`queueAlbumByName`)
- `onGoToAlbum` — navigate to the album detail screen
- `onGoToArtist` — navigate to the artist screen
- `onToggleCollection` — add/remove from collection (required)

Pass `null` for any action that isn't applicable in a given context (the menu item is hidden).

**Screens with album context menus:** LibraryScreen, HomeScreen (Albums you might love, the Fresh Drops / Critical Darlings / Pop of the Tops halves), ArtistScreen (discography), HistoryScreen (top albums), FriendDetailScreen (top albums), PopOfTheTopsScreen (chart albums).

### Artist Context Menus

Every artist card grid across the app supports long-press context menus via the shared `ArtistContextMenu` component (`ui/components/ArtistContextMenu.kt`). This is an always-dark `ModalBottomSheet` matching the album context menu styling.

**Pattern for adding artist context menus to a screen:**
1. Import `ArtistContextMenu` and `hapticCombinedClickable`
2. Change the artist card's modifier from `hapticClickable` to `hapticCombinedClickable(onClick = ..., onLongClick = ...)`
3. Add `var showMenu by remember { mutableStateOf(false) }` state per item
4. Show `ArtistContextMenu` when `showMenu` is true

**ArtistContextMenu callbacks** — all optional except `onToggleCollection`:
- `onPlayTopSongs` — fetch and play the artist's top tracks (shared `CatalogQueues.artistTopSongs`)
- `onQueueTopSongs` — fetch and queue the artist's top tracks
- `onGoToArtist` — navigate to the artist screen
- `onToggleCollection` — add/remove artist from collection (required)

Pass `null` for any action that isn't applicable in a given context (the menu item is hidden).

**Screens with artist context menus:** LibraryScreen (artist grid), ArtistScreen (related artists), HistoryScreen (top artists), FriendDetailScreen (top artists), RecommendationsScreen (recommended artists).

**ViewModel requirements:** Each ViewModel that supports artist context menus needs `playArtistTopSongs(artistName)`, `queueArtistTopSongs(artistName)`, and `toggleArtistCollection(artistName, imageUrl, isInCollection)` methods. The first two go through the shared `CatalogQueues` (see "Queue Album by Name / Artist Top Songs"), which needs `MetadataService` and `PlaybackController`. Currently implemented in: LibraryViewModel, ArtistViewModel, HistoryViewModel, FriendDetailViewModel, RecommendationsViewModel.

### Queue Album by Name / Artist Top Songs — shared `CatalogQueues` (#588)

To play or queue an album that isn't in the library (charts, friend history, discography) or an artist's top songs, use the shared `CatalogQueues` (`shared/.../playback/CatalogQueues.kt`), which both apps call:

```kotlin
private val catalogQueues = CatalogQueues(metadataService)

fun queueAlbumByName(albumTitle: String, albumArtist: String) {
    viewModelScope.launch {
        val queue = catalogQueues.album(albumTitle, albumArtist) ?: return@launch
        playbackController.addToQueue(queue.tracks)   // or playQueue(queue.tracks, 0, queue.context)
    }
}
```

The tracks are metadata-only (title, artist, album, length, cover, the provider's Spotify id and recording MBID) and resolve when they play, like every other metadata-only track; nothing is resolved up front and nothing is dropped. The context links back to the page: an album's name is its title and its id the artist (the album page's form), an artist's name and id are the artist. Top songs are `CatalogQueues.TOP_SONGS` (10). iOS's album and artist menus (`Interactions.swift`) and the DJ chat's album and artist cards (`IosContainer.chatPlayAlbum` / `chatPlayArtist`) use the same class through `IosContainer.albumQueue` / `artistTopSongsQueue`.

### Album Collection Reactive Checks

To reactively check if an album is in the user's collection (for toggle UI in context menus):

1. **DAO:** `AlbumDao.existsByTitleAndArtist(title, artist): Flow<Boolean>`
2. **Repository:** `LibraryRepository.isAlbumInCollection(title, artist): Flow<Boolean>`
3. **ViewModel:** Collect as `StateFlow` via `.stateIn(viewModelScope, ...)`

**AlbumEntity ID format:** `"album-${title.hashCode()}-${artist.hashCode()}"`

### Shared formatters and Collection sorts (#593)

Text and list rules that both apps show come from shared code, following desktop:
- **Time ago:** `RelativeTime.ago` (milliseconds) and `agoSeconds` (Last.fm and ListenBrainz timestamps) in `shared/.../format/Formatters.kt`, desktop's `formatTimeAgo`. It shows "Just now", "5m ago", "3h ago" and "2d ago", then the date: "Mar 4", or "Mar 4, 2025" in an earlier year. It's used for listens, friends' last plays and a playlist's "Last updated".
- **Play counts:** `playCountLabel` shows the full number ("1 play", "1234 plays").
- **History period:** History and a friend's profile open on `DEFAULT_HISTORY_PERIOD` (`7day`).
- **Collection sorts:** the sort orders, their labels ("A-Z", desktop's hyphen) and their defaults are in `shared/.../library/CollectionSort.kt`, together with the filter and the stable sort (`CollectionSort.tracks` / `albums` / `artists` / `friends`). Android's `ui/screens/library/CollectionSort.kt` is typealiases; iOS's `CollectionView` aliases the same enums. A sort's `name` is what's saved, so don't rename one.

The sync screen's "Last synced" text is still written per platform (#591).

### Singleton Repository Caching

`@Singleton` repositories persist across ViewModel lifecycles. If a repository caches API results (like `ChartsRepository`, which keeps each chart for 24 hours), the cache outlives individual screens and can become stale.

**Rules for singleton caches:**
- Always add a TTL or force-refresh mechanism. A `@Volatile var cacheTimestamp` with a TTL check prevents serving stale data indefinitely.
- For data that changes frequently (charts), prefer `forceRefresh = true` from the ViewModel `init` since API calls are cheap.
- For expensive data (AI recommendations), use stale-while-revalidate: show cached data immediately, refresh in background.

### External Playback Background Survival

Keeping Apple Music and Spotify playing with the screen off requires navigating several Android subsystems that actively try to kill the process. These rules are hard-won from debugging — violating any one causes intermittent playback drops.

**Foreground service — Media3 fights you.** `MediaSessionService` auto-manages foreground status based on ExoPlayer state. During external playback, ExoPlayer is idle (just metadata), so Media3 calls `stopForeground()` and `pauseAllPlayersAndStopSelf()`. Both `onUpdateNotification()` overloads and `pauseAllPlayersAndStopSelf()` must be overridden to return early when `isExternalForeground` is true.

**ExoPlayer stop triggers service demotion.** Calling `ctrl.stop()` during external→external track transitions causes `MediaSessionService` to auto-demote from foreground. Then `startForegroundService()` fails from background (Android 12+). During external→external transitions, skip `ctrl.stop()` and `ctrl.prepare()` — just update metadata via `ctrl.setMediaItems()`.

**Async stop/play race condition.** Calling `router.stopExternalPlayback()` before `playTrackInternal()` in advance paths sends an async `music.stop()` to MusicKit JS that races with the new `setQueue({startPlaying: true})`. The stop arrives after the new track starts, pausing it ~30ms later. The fix: don't call `stopExternalPlayback()` in any advance path (skipNext, skipPrevious, playFromQueue, spinoff). For same-handler transitions, `handler.play()` replaces the queue directly. For cross-handler transitions (e.g. Apple Music→Spotify), `playTrackInternalUnsafe` stops the old handler only when handlers differ.

**WebView lifecycle — don't call keepAlive() in onStop().** `WebView.onResume()`/`resumeTimers()` can block the main thread when the Chromium renderer process is suspending, causing a background ANR that kills the process. The brief WebView stutter on screen-off (~500ms) recovers on its own. Call `keepAlive()` from `MusicKitWebBridge.play()` instead (runs while app is in foreground, before each track starts).

**WebView layer type must be LAYER_TYPE_NONE.** `LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE` causes GPU context teardown when the screen turns off, disrupting the WebView's audio pipeline. The headless WebView doesn't render anything visible, and Widevine DRM works through `MediaDrm`/`MediaCodec` at the platform level, not the View's rendering layer.

**Silent ExoPlayer playback keeps the service alive.** Media3's `MediaSessionService` validates that `mediaPlayback` foreground services have an active player. During external playback, ExoPlayer plays a 1-second silent WAV (`res/raw/silence.wav`) on loop at volume 0. This makes Media3 see `isPlaying=true`, preventing the "Stopping service due to app idle" kill at ~11 minutes. Audio focus is disabled on ExoPlayer during silence (`handleAudioFocus=false`) to avoid conflicting with Chromium's `AudioFocusDelegate`. Normal settings are restored when switching back to ExoPlayer playback. The loop's repeat mode (ONE for the silence loop, OFF for local plays) is set only through a private session command, `EngineRepeatCommand`, which `onConnect` offers just to Parachord's own package. Never set `ctrl.repeatMode`: the media session's repeat command now means Parachord's repeat (off/all/one) and is mapped by `ExternalPlaybackForwardingPlayer.getRepeatMode/setRepeatMode`, so writing ExoPlayer's repeat there would change the user's repeat setting.

**startService() vs startForegroundService() from background.** On Android 12+, both can throw `ForegroundServiceStartNotAllowedException` when the app is backgrounded. `startService()` is tried first (works more reliably when the service is already running). The service should already be in foreground from initial promotion when the app was visible.

**The notification provider's artwork re-post can be refused too.** `UnifiedMediaNotificationProvider` re-posts the notification through Media3's `onNotificationChanged` callback once the artwork loads, and Media3 may start the service in the foreground from there. Media3 1.5.1 catches a refusal when it updates the notification itself, but not on this path, so a play started from a link with the phone locked crashed the app. The re-post goes through `repostMediaNotification`, which catches the refusal and skips Media3 during external playback (the service manages its own notification then).

**Without the foreground, Android cuts the network.** On Android 15+ an app that isn't on screen and has no foreground service is behind the `APP_BACKGROUND` firewall chain (`dumpsys netpolicy` shows `blocked=APP_BACKGROUND` for its uid; the process state is `TPSL` with the screen locked): MusicKit fails with "Unable to complete request for https://api.music.apple.com/…", lookups with `UnknownHostException`, and nothing plays. A started service (`SVC`) isn't blocked. A play from Android Auto, a Bluetooth button or the lock-screen controls lets the app start a foreground service for about 10 seconds. An Auto tap gets there through Media3, which promotes the service as Auto plays the silent placeholder. A play that reaches `PlaybackController` without ExoPlayer playing first (play or skip while external playback is paused, the idle-timeout re-play below) used to promote only after the lookup and routing, so `playTrackInternalUnsafe` now promotes as the play starts when a user asked for it and no Parachord screen is showing (`shouldPromoteAtPlayStart`). A skip past a track that can't play asks for it too when the track before was local and nothing holds it (`shouldHoldForegroundForAutoSkip`). After a local track that ends by itself the foreground is kept, not asked for (next paragraph). The route takes the foreground over: external playback keeps it, the ExoPlayer branches hand it back to Media3, and `playTrackInternal` releases it if nothing played, except when the queue is about to skip past that track (`keepsForegroundForAutoSkip`): the skip's own play isn't user-requested and can't promote from the background, so the foreground waits for the next track's route, and is released (`releaseForegroundHeldForPlay`) when the queue stops instead (the end of the queue, a queue given back paused, a skip that's dropped or does nothing). Work done before the play starts (a `parachord://play` link's own search) still counts against the window. While the service isn't in the foreground, `startService` intents from the controller (external mode on/off, silence pause/resume) are refused too, so the session's player can stay out of external mode after a refused start.

**A failed play keeps its notification (#619).** When a play fails and stops on its track while external playback was running (a link's track with no source after a Spotify song), the service leaves the foreground but keeps its notification (`STOP_FOREGROUND_DETACH`), showing the failed track, not playing. Without it the lock screen showed only the other app's player (Spotify's own paused "Judy Is a Punk"), as if Parachord were still on that track. The kept notification goes as soon as the current track changes or its error clears (`dropKeptNotificationIfStale`): a later play that can't start the foreground (a link with the phone locked isn't a media command) would otherwise leave it naming the failed track as playing. Checked on a Pixel 9a, locked. A play link with the phone locked isn't played at all now (next paragraph).

**A play link that arrives with the phone locked isn't played.** Android treats Parachord as a background app behind the lock screen (process state `TPSL`): it refuses the playback foreground (a link isn't a media command), cuts the network (`blocked=APP_BACKGROUND`) and ignores the audio focus request (`AudioHardening focus request … ignored`), so even a local file sat paused at 0:00 (Pixel 9a, Android 17). So `DeepLinkViewModel.handleUri` checks `LockedLinkNotifier.isLocked()` for a link that starts playback (shared `startsPlayback`) and posts "Unlock to play: <what it plays>" instead; tapping it opens the same link, which then plays as any link does. Other links (transport controls, navigation) still run. Showing Parachord over the lock screen to play at once was the alternative, and wasn't chosen.

**A local track's end keeps the foreground (#614).** Media3 1.5.1's `MediaSessionService` takes the service out of the foreground as soon as its player stops playing: at `STATE_ENDED` its notification controller calls `onUpdateNotification(session, false)`, which stops the foreground. Android 12+ then refuses any start from an app that isn't on screen, so whatever the queue played next (an external route's `promoteToForeground`, Media3's own start for the next local track, the auto-skip's hold) failed with `ForegroundServiceStartNotAllowedException`, 186 ms after the advance began; being quicker can't help. Seen on a Pixel 9a (Android 17, Nov 2026) with the app merely in the background, for local → Apple Music, local → no source → Apple Music, local → local and Apple Music → local. So the service keeps it instead of asking again:
- **At the end.** `PlaybackService` has its own `Player.Listener` on ExoPlayer (`foregroundKeeper`), registered before the session is built, so it runs ahead of the session's and the controller's listeners, while the service is still in the foreground. At `STATE_ENDED` it asks `PlaybackController.keepForegroundAtLocalTrackEnd()` (`shouldKeepForegroundAtLocalTrackEnd`: no Parachord screen showing, not external, and something follows: a next track, repeat one or all, a spinoff pool track, or a Listen Along track whose session decides). If so the service adopts Media3's foreground as its own (`isExternalForeground = true`, no new start), so Media3's demotion and later updates aren't passed on, and the controller marks it held (`foregroundHeldForPlay`). At the end of the queue nothing is kept and Media3 lets it go as before.
- **The next track takes it over.** An external route keeps it (`promoteToForeground` only updates the notification). A local track hands it back to Media3 without dropping it: with no Parachord screen showing (`localPlayKeepsHeldForeground`), whoever asked for the play, the ExoPlayer branch doesn't send `ACTION_EXTERNAL_PLAYBACK_STOP`, and the service stops holding it once ExoPlayer plays a track that isn't the silent loop (`isSilenceKeepAliveUri`); the service stays in the foreground, so Media3 only updates it, which Android allows, and lets it go at the next pause or end. "Whoever asked" matters: Listen Along's plays (`playTrack` for the friend's song, `playQueue` for the suspended queue coming back) count as user-requested though nobody pressed anything, and a person's request on the lock screen, the car or a headset gains nothing from dropping first either. This also fixes external → local in the background, which used to drop the foreground first. With a screen showing the old stop-then-Media3-starts path stays. A skip past a track with no source carries the hold (`keepsForegroundForAutoSkip`).
- **Released when nothing follows:** the end of the queue, a queue given back paused, a dropped skip (`releaseForegroundHeldForPlay`), and a watchdog (`startForegroundHoldWatchdog`, `nextForegroundHoldIdleMs`, `foregroundHoldExpired`): after 30 s (`FOREGROUND_HOLD_IDLE_LIMIT_MS`) with nothing external and no play, advance or skip under way, the service lets it go and tells the controller. While Listen Along waits for its next track after a local song (`heldForListener`) the limit is 5 minutes (`FOREGROUND_HOLD_LISTENER_LIMIT_MS`, the length of the idle timeout); the session's own end releases it sooner. After an external song there's no limit (the session's player stays in external mode after the idle timeout), so the two waits differ: once the 5 minutes pass after a local song, the session keeps polling without the foreground and the friend's next track can't start from the background. "External" for the watchdog is either the controller's state (`isExternalPlaybackActive`, so a late or refused external mode on the session's player can't demote mid-song) or the session player's external mode (which stays on after the idle timeout stops a paused external track, so its notification and Play stay); it's idle only when both say nothing external plays.
- **While a foreground is held across a local end, the state observer doesn't seek ExoPlayer.** It syncs the silent loop's position to the external track's; only while ExoPlayer plays the loop, or it would seek the next local track to where the last one ended.
- **Checked on a Pixel 9a (Android 17, Nov 2026).** With the phone locked: local → no source → Apple Music, and Apple Music → local, each started by itself and was heard, no refusal, still in the foreground at the end (on the build before the keep stopped depending on who asked for the play); then on the final build local → local → Apple Music, no refusal, in the foreground and playing 13 s into the Apple Music track. In the background with the screen on: local → Apple Music, local → local, local as the last track (released at the end), a local next track that never starts (released by the watchdog after 30 s), and a Listen Along-style `playTrack` of a local track 40 s after a local song ended (still held, then handed to Media3). Not checked on a device: Spotify as the next track, a real Listen Along session or spinoff after a local track, and the 5-minute Listen Along limit (unit test only).
- **Known limits.** The watchdog's release removes the notification (`STOP_FOREGROUND_REMOVE`), so after a local track that never plays (the queue's next one, or one asked for from the lock screen or the car) the notification stays 30 s and then goes while the stopped track stays on screen. A local next track that stays paused or buffering for more than 30 s never gets the hand-back, and the watchdog releases it. The session's pause while its player is still in external mode and the controller is already local goes to ExoPlayer without `markUserPaused`, so the 5 s spurious-resume guard doesn't apply to it.

**A long pause stops the external player; play starts the track again (#550).** Five minutes after an external track (Apple Music, Spotify, an embed) is paused, `PlaybackController.startIdleTimeout` stops its handler and leaves external playback, but the track stays on screen and in the car, and the media session stays in external mode. It records the track and its position (`LoadOnPlay`, `IDLE_STOPPED`, built by the shared `idleTimeoutLoad`; see below), unless a slot is already set for that track (`FAILED`, `RESTORED`: `idleTimeoutLoad` keeps it, so a failed track still re-chooses its source); the next play re-plays that track and seeks to where it was paused (`playThenSeek`), waiting up to 60 s for the track to start, since a slow or returning network can hold it back (it waited 10 s, after which the song played from 0:00, #567). Before this, play fell through to `ctrl.play()`, which restarted only ExoPlayer's silent keep-alive loop: the session said "playing" and nothing was heard, until Next or Previous started a new track. iOS and desktop have no idle timeout.

**Saving and restoring the queue: one shared store (#594).** `QueuePersistenceStore` (`shared/.../playback/QueuePersistenceStore.kt`) holds the rules for both apps: `saveSoon(currentTrack)` after every change takes the queue as it is at the call and writes the latest one 500 ms (`SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS`) after the last call; only with "Remember queue" on, and never an empty queue over the last session's until this run has had one (`QueueRestorePolicy.shouldSave`). `load()` reads and decodes the saved queue (the shared `PersistedQueueState` with full `Track`s; Android's older, smaller track shape still decodes), the platform checks `QueueRestorePolicy.shouldApply` and calls `apply()`. The position is saved through the same store (`positionSaveDue`, `savePosition`, `resumePositionMs`). Android's `QueuePersistence` only feeds it from the queue and playback state flows; iOS's coordinator calls it from `syncSnapshot`, `restoreQueue` and its 1 s tick (`IosContainer.makeQueuePersistence`).

**Getting the next track ready (#594).** The shared `NextTrackPreparation`: when a track starts, the next 3 queued tracks (`PRERESOLVE_AHEAD`) are resolved in the background on both apps (iOS behind the rows on screen); in the last 30 s of a track (`shouldPrepareNext`) each app readies the next track's engine: Android prewarms Spotify and resolves an Apple Music track, iOS fetches the next Apple Music song (it used 15 s before).

**A restored queue resumes its track where it was (#567).** A paused app the system no longer protects can be ended (Android frees its memory once the idle timeout lets the foreground go; iOS ends suspended apps), and the queue restored at the next launch used to start the track at 0:00. The position is now saved beside the queue, on its own key (`persisted_queue_position`, a `SavedPlaybackPosition`: track id, position, length), on a pause and every 15 s while a song plays; the queue blob isn't rewritten for it. The rules are shared (`PlaybackResumePosition` in `shared/.../playback/QueuePersistence.kt`): only the same track id resumes, and a track under 5 s in or within 10 s of its end starts over. Android saves from `QueuePersistence` and resumes through `playThenSeek` (the restored track's first play). The media session's play (car, headphones, lock screen) on a restored queue goes to `togglePlayPause` too (`PlaybackController.needsLoadOnPlay`, checked by the service's forwarding player, below): ExoPlayer holds nothing for the restored track, and playing it skipped to the empty next item and stopped; iOS saves from the coordinator's 1 s tick and seeks once the track plays (`seekOnceStarted`, 60 s). **The track starts at the saved position itself where the engine can (#657, desktop parachord#1346)**, so the song's opening isn't heard before a jump: Spotify gets `position_ms` in its play request (shared `SpotifyPlayRequests.forTrack(uri, startMs)`), Android's ExoPlayer `setMediaItems(…, startMs)`, iOS's AVPlayer a seek before play and Apple Music `prepareToPlay` then `playbackTime`. The seek after play (`playThenSeek`, `seekOnceStarted`) runs only when the engine didn't start there: Android's Apple Music (MusicKit JS) and the SoundCloud and Bandcamp embeds (each handler's `startsAt` on Android; on iOS by the engine that played). **A resumed listen that already scrobbled isn't scrobbled again (#657, desktop parachord#1346).** The saved position records whether its listen was scrobbled (`SavedPlaybackPosition.scrobbled`, from `ScrobbleManager.hasScrobbled`, or the restored slot's own flag while it hasn't played), the restored slot carries it (`LoadOnPlay.continuesScrobbled`, kept by a seek, dropped by a restart from the top), and Play at that position tells the scrobbler first (`ScrobbleManager.continueScrobbledListen`), so the threshold, passed again at once, doesn't submit a second listen. Now Playing still goes out. A restored track that starts from the top (its first 5 s or last 10 s, Previous) is a new listen. A seek or a Previous restart on a track nothing holds saves its new position at once (Android `QueuePersistence.savePositionNow`, iOS `setLoadOnPlayPosition`), since neither save above runs for a track that isn't playing. Gated on the "Remember queue" setting like the queue. Desktop follows the same rule since [parachord#1325](https://github.com/Parachord/parachord/pull/1325) (`saved_queue_position`, saved every 5 s while playing and on pause).

**Play loads a track nothing holds (`LoadOnPlay`).** The three cases above and a failed play share one slot, the shared `LoadOnPlay` (`shared/.../playback/LoadOnPlay.kt`): the track on screen, where Play starts it, whether to keep its source (`skipReselect`), why (`Why`), and the resolver picked by hand, if any (`pickedResolver`; iOS names it, Android keeps the track's source with `skipReselect`). `RESTORED` is a queue restored at launch or given back paused, `IDLE_STOPPED` the paused track the idle timeout stopped (Android only), `FAILED` a track nothing could play. Both apps keep one (Android `PlaybackController.loadOnPlay`, iOS `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.loadOnPlay`) and build it with the same functions (`failedLoad`, `idleTimeoutLoad`); it applies only while its track is the current one (`loadOnPlayTarget`). Play (Android `togglePlayPause`, and the media session's play through `needsLoadOnPlay` in the forwarding player, in or out of external mode; iOS `togglePlayPause`, the remote commands, CarPlay and the embed pages' play) then starts the track through the whole pipeline at the slot's position (Android `playThenSeek`, iOS `playTrack` + `seekOnceStarted`), re-choosing its source unless it was a manual pick, instead of resuming the player: that would play its silent loop, its failed item (an errored ExoPlayer ignores play) or nothing. A manual pick is retried on the same source, a stream that fails mid-play included (Android remembers which track's play kept its source, `manualPickTrackId`, for `onPlayerError`); iOS drops the pick when the cache no longer holds that source, so Play can't loop on a source that's gone. Before this, a play that failed offline left the track on screen and the session at `ERROR`, and Play did nothing even once the network was back. **Seek and Previous on a slot** move where Play starts, not a player: a seek sets the slot's position (clamped to the track's length), the shown position and the saved one; Previous past 3 s restarts it from the top the same way (`restartedFromTop`; see "Queue advance and repeat"). On Android the media session's seek is routed by the controller's own state (`PlaybackController.ownsSessionSeek`: an external engine plays, or a slot waits), not by the forwarding player's external flag: after a failed track (as after the idle timeout) the session's player stays in external mode while the controller has left it, and the controller's seek, sent through the session, came back and was forwarded again, forever (#607). Any play clears the slot (`playTrackInternalUnsafe`), and Play leaves it alone while a play or an advance is starting, so it can't race one. The media session answers a play on an idle player with `prepare()` and then `play()` (Media3's `Util.handlePlayButtonAction`), so the forwarding player also skips `prepare()` while the slot is set. ExoPlayer holds nothing for a restored track, an empty player ends at once, and `STATE_ENDED` made the controller advance the queue: a restored track with tracks queued after it skipped to the next one (with none queued, it only logged `skipNext: queue empty`). An Android Auto browse tap clears the slot as it arrives (`notePlayRequested`, called in `onSetMediaItems` / `onAddMediaItems`, not in the launched dispatch), so the session's play that follows the tap doesn't start the restored track alongside the tapped one. What counts as failed (`PlayFailure`, shared in `shared/.../playback/PlayFailure.kt` with `failedPlayAwaitsLoad`, `PLAY_START_FAILED_MESSAGE` and `PLAY_NO_SOURCE_MESSAGE`; on Android recorded by `playTrackInternalUnsafe`'s exits and turned into the slot by `playTrackInternal`): an embed refused the track and no other source could play it; no source (see "Queue advance and repeat"); Spotify can't start in the background and nothing else can; the handler didn't start in time (#449); the handler gave up without starting it (`PlaybackStartFailedException` → `START_FAILED`: Apple Music offline, see "Apple Music State Polling & Auto-Advance"); the play threw; the local player's item failed (Android `onPlayerError`, not the service's own `REMOTE_ERROR` message; iOS `IosAVPlayer.onPlaybackFailed`, before or after it started, with the start-failed message, booked only against the play that loaded the item: each load carries the play's number, and a replaced item's late failure is dropped). A fallback source's play replaces the refused one's outcome. A manual source switch whose pick fails records none of this (next paragraph). `failedPlayAwaitsLoad` leaves out the one case where the queue moves on by itself: a refused track (`EMBED_REFUSED`) or one with no source (`NO_SOURCE`, online) during an advance or a spinoff is skipped, so Play belongs to the next track, and the `embedRefusedTrackId` guard stays as it was. The car's third skip in a row stops instead and leaves a slot (`failedPlayAwaitsLoadAfterCarStop`, see "CarPlay / Android Auto"). Position (`failedLoad`): a resume that fails (restored, idle-stopped) keeps the position it aimed for; a stream that failed while playing starts where it stopped, under the restored-track rules (its first 5 s or last 10 s start over); a play from the top starts from the top. More on iOS's side in `iosApp/AGENTS.md` → "Play on a track no engine holds starts it".

**A failed source switch leaves the playback as it was, on both apps.** A source picked in Now Playing's switcher (Android `PlaybackController.switchSource`, iOS `switchResolver`) that can't start (no route, a refused or plan-blocked source, a manifest or licence failure, a start that failed or timed out, a throw) records no `PlayFailure`, no `LoadOnPlay` slot and no error: the shared `failedSwitchKeepsPlayback(isManualSwitch, failure, superseded)` (`shared/.../playback/SourceSwitch.kt`) says so (a superseded play is never a failure, and Play retrying a slot's picked source isn't a switch), the track keeps its old source, the switcher's pick is cleared, the play button stops spinning and the shared `PlaybackMessages.couldNotSwitchTo` says "Couldn’t play from Amazon Music. Still playing from Apple Music." No other source is tried in its place. On Android the switch's play routes with `PlaybackRouter.route(track, stopActive = false)`, so the playing external engine isn't stopped while the pick is routed (a plugin DRM manifest is fetched there), and `giveBackAfterFailedSwitch` applies the shared `failedSwitchRecovery`: an engine never touched keeps playing (`UNTOUCHED`); for another external engine the old one is paused, not stopped, and resumed in place if the pick fails (`RESUME_IN_PLACE`), stopped only once the pick plays; a pick the local player plays (a local file, a stream, a plugin DRM source) has to stop the old engine first, so a later failure (a refused licence, the DRM start wait) plays the old track again from its own source at its position (`REPLAY_AT_POSITION`, through `playThenSeek`), or, if it was paused, leaves an `IDLE_STOPPED` slot there for Play (`HOLD_FOR_PLAY`). A local player's item that fails after it was handed over (ExoPlayer's `onPlayerError`) isn't a start failure and follows the ordinary rule. iOS's router doesn't stop the old engine before a source starts, so its `.noPlayableSource` branch only shows the message (`QueuePlaybackCoordinator.playTrack`, `deckSwitch`); a pick to a local file that AVPlayer refuses as it loads still stops on the track, as any AVPlayer load failure does.

### Concerts — which artists are searched (#589)

The Concerts page looks up the concerts of up to 40 artists, picked by the shared `ConcertArtistSeed` (`shared/.../repository/ConcertArtistSeed.kt`, a port of desktop's `gatherConcertsArtists`). Both apps use it: Android `ConcertsViewModel`, iOS `IosContainer.concertsFlow` / `loadConcerts`, through `ConcertArtistSeedSource`. There are three groups:
- **Collection:** the collection's artists.
- **Library:** the artists of the collection's tracks, then of its albums.
- **History:** the top 40 artists over the last 6 months, from Last.fm (`6month`) then ListenBrainz (`half_yearly`), for whichever has a username.

Each artist counts once, in the first group that has it (names compared trimmed and lowercase). The groups are then dealt round-robin. A source that fails counts as empty. iOS used to search only history, across every period, because it predates the collection database there. With no artists, the page shows no concerts on every platform (Android used to fall back to events near the saved location).

### Concert cards — the artist's photo and the On Tour tab (shared)

A concert card shows the artist's photo: on Home's Concerts near you and the Concerts page, on both apps. iOS Home's concert cards use the shared pieces (`HomeState.concertArtistImages`, `concertPhotoUrl`, `concertTourArtist`, `IosContainer.concertArtistImages`). The shared `ConcertArtistImages` (`shared/.../home/ConcertArtistImages.kt`, a port of desktop's Concerts page loop) looks the photos up through the artist-photo chain (`MetadataService.getArtistImage`, which keeps its own persistent cache), five at a time, merging each answer as it arrives into `images`, keyed `concertArtistKey` (trimmed, lowercase). Each artist is asked once a session once a lookup answers: one found, or answered with no photo, is skipped. A lookup that threw isn't counted as tried, so the next `load` asks again (never within the same load, and a load waiting on it doesn't retry it either). A lookup in flight belongs to the load that started it; another load wanting the same artist waits on it, and if the owning load is cancelled the artist goes back to unasked and the waiting load takes the lookup over, so cancelling one screen's load never strands another's. One instance serves the whole app (Android a Koin single shared by `RepositoryHomeSources` and `ConcertsViewModel`; iOS `IosContainer.concertArtistImages`). Home asks for its shown cards only (`HomeSession`, a new list cancelling the last; `HomeState.concertArtistImages`); the Concerts page asks for every loaded show, as desktop does (Android `ConcertsViewModel`, cancelled by a new list; iOS `ConcertsModel` through `IosContainer.loadConcertArtistImages`, a cancellable Kotlin job). A card is about its event's artist (`concertTourArtist`); an event that names none gets no photo lookup and no tap, as desktop keys cards by artist. A card draws the shared `concertPhotoUrl(event, images)`: the artist's photo, else the event's own image (a mobile choice: desktop has no fallback), else the placeholder, each https only (`concertPhotoHttpsUrl`: both apps block cleartext, so an `http://` photo counts as none rather than being upgraded), cropped a quarter of the way down (`CONCERT_PHOTO_FOCUS_Y`, desktop's `center 25%`; Android `ConcertPhoto`, iOS `.concertPhotoFocus`). Tapping a card opens the artist's On Tour tab (`ARTIST_ON_TOUR_TAB`, all their shows; desktop's card isn't clickable; iOS asserts at launch that `ArtistTab.onTour` matches it); Tickets stays its own target and opens the seller's page. Screen readers read the card's text (artist, date, venue, place) with the open named "<artist> concerts" (`HomeCopy.concertsOf`); on iOS, Tickets is a named VoiceOver action.

### Home's Concerts near you and Ask's chat origin (shared)

**Concerts near you** is the shared `concertsNearYou(events, location, today)` (`shared/.../home/ConcertsNearYou.kt`, desktop's `homeConcerts`): the Concerts page's location (`SettingsStore.ConcertLocation`), upcoming shows only (`date.take(10) >= today`), within the radius by `haversineMiles` (desktop's formula, 3958.8 mi) when the location and the venue both have coordinates, else the location's city (its first comma part, lowercased) containing the venue's city or state; soonest first, at most 4. No location gives nothing. `ConcertEvent.venueLatitude` / `venueLongitude` come from Ticketmaster's and SeatGeek's venue location, and a merged duplicate keeps the first pair either provider gave (`withFirstVenueCoordinates`).

**Ask Shuffleupagus sends without the link confirmation.** The shared `ChatPrompt` (`shared/.../deeplink/ChatPrompt.kt`, desktop's `protocol-url.js`) caps a prompt at 500 UTF-16 units (`cap`, dropping a split surrogate half), builds `parachord://chat?prompt=` as `encodeURIComponent` does (`url`), and says when a queued prompt is sent (`shouldSend`: chat open, a provider, no reply in flight) or dropped (`shouldDrop`: the chat closed before it went out). An in-app prompt is queued with `AiChatService.setPendingChatPrompt(prompt, fromApp = true)`, only by the direct calls: Android `DeepLinkViewModel.handleInAppChatPrompt` (queues, opens chat), iOS `IosContainer.askShuffleupagus` through RootView's `pcAskShuffleupagus` environment action. **When it goes out:** only once the provider's saved messages have loaded, so the load can't replace the shown prompt, only to a provider that can send (configured; otherwise it stays queued), and re-checked whenever the chat opens, a provider loads or a reply finishes, as desktop's pending-prompt effect does (`AiChatService.takeSendableChatPrompt`; iOS through `IosContainer.takeSendableInAppChatPrompt`, and a send that finishes after its chat closed takes nothing). Android's `ChatViewModel` combines the pending prompt, whether its screen is started (`setChatOpen`, from `ChatScreen`'s `LifecycleStartEffect`, so a chat left in the back stack doesn't take a prompt meant for a new one), the loaded provider and loading; iOS's `ChatScreen` checks after `start()` (which awaits the load) and after each reply. **Dropping a leftover:** Android's `onCleared` drops only the prompt that screen saw open (`PendingChatPrompt.generation`, `dropPendingChatPrompt`), so a chat screen cleared late can't drop one queued for the next; iOS's `closeChat` drops it. **History is loaded once per provider (`AiChatService.historyFor`, under a `Mutex`):** two concurrent first loads used to each build the list, and the second replaced the one the prompt and its reply had been added to, so the chat showed the old conversation without the prompt. A `parachord://chat` link never sets the origin: Android still confirms it ("An external link wants to send this message to chat"), and iOS still pre-fills the input for the user to send.

### On Tour Indicator — Location-Filtered

The "On Tour" teal dot (`#10C9B4`) appears next to the artist name in the mini player and Now Playing screen when the currently playing artist has upcoming concerts near the user's configured concert area.

**Desktop behavior (source of truth):**
1. Check if the artist matches any event (primary artist or in lineup).
2. If the user has set a concert location (`concertsLocationCoords`), filter events by haversine distance ≤ `concertsLocationRadius` (default 50mi). Falls back to city-name text matching when venue has no coordinates.
3. If **no location is configured**, any upcoming event counts — the dot shows for any touring artist (desktop: `if (!concertsLocationCoords) return true`).
4. The dot is clickable — navigates to the artist page with the "On Tour" tab selected.

**Android implementation:**
- `ConcertsRepository.checkOnTour(artistName, lat, lon, radiusMiles)` queries Ticketmaster + SeatGeek with location params. When `lat`/`lon` are null (no location configured), queries without location filtering (any event counts).
- `MainViewModel` and `NowPlayingViewModel` read `settingsStore.getConcertLocation()` and pass it to `checkOnTour()`.
- The **ArtistScreen's On Tour tab** is intentionally unfiltered by location — it shows the full tour schedule for browsing all dates.

**Key files:** `ConcertsRepository.kt`, `MainViewModel.kt`, `NowPlayingViewModel.kt`, `MiniPlayer.kt`, `NowPlayingScreen.kt`

## Design System & Theming

**The full design system is [docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md](docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md)**: tokens, page and service colours, scales, the shared components (section titles, filter chips, source icons, cards, states), Home, copy and accessibility. It translates desktop's rules (desktop `CLAUDE.md` "Design system: don't let it drift", #1380) to Compose and SwiftUI; when desktop changes a rule, change it there in the same piece of work. Where the apps stand against it: [docs/plans/2026-11-15-mobile-design-audit.md](docs/plans/2026-11-15-mobile-design-audit.md). The rules most often broken:
- **White text never sits on the theme accent** (`#a78bfa` in dark).
