## KMP Shared Module

The `:shared` module (`shared/`) contains Kotlin Multiplatform code shared between Android and a future iOS app. The `:app` module depends on `:shared`.

**Status (April 29, 2026): KMP migration is feature-complete.** All business logic lives in `shared/commonMain/`. Both `iosArm64` and `iosSimulatorArm64` compile green. iOS just needs a Swift app shell — repositories, AI, sync, resolvers, etc. are all callable from `import shared`.

**What lives in shared/commonMain:**
- Platform abstractions: `Log`, `randomUUID()`, `currentTimeMillis()` (expect/actual in androidMain/iosMain)
- Models: `Track`, `Album`, `Artist`, `Playlist`, `PlaylistTrack`, `Friend`, `ChatMessage`, `SearchHistory`, `SyncSource` + charts/history/recommendation models, `AiAlbumSuggestion`/`AiArtistSuggestion`, `RecommendedTrack`/`RecommendedArtist`
- API clients (Ktor): Spotify, Last.fm, MusicBrainz, AppleMusic, Ticketmaster, SeatGeek, ListenBrainz, Discogs, Wikipedia, SmartLinks. Single shared `HttpClient` with 60s `requestTimeoutMillis`, `OAuthRefreshPlugin` for 401-driven token refresh, `User-Agent` injection. Retrofit fully dropped from `:app`.
- Database: SQLDelight `.sq` files matching the old Room v12 schema (9 tables). SQLDelight is the live database; Room was removed. DAOs (`TrackDao`, `AlbumDao`, etc.) are concrete shared classes wrapping `*Queries`.
- DI: Koin `sharedModule` with Ktor client + DAO + repository bindings
- Plugin system: `JsRuntime` interface, `PluginManager`, `PluginSyncService`, `PluginFileAccess` (expect/actual)
- All 9 repositories: `ChartsRepository`, `ConcertsRepository`, `CriticalDarlingsRepository`, `FreshDropsRepository`, `FriendsRepository`, `HistoryRepository`, `LibraryRepository`, `RecommendationsRepository`, `WeeklyPlaylistsRepository`. App-side files are typealias shims.
- Sync engine: `SyncEngine`, `SyncProvider` interface, `SpotifySyncProvider`, `AppleMusicSyncProvider`, multi-provider iteration, three-layer dedup, four-piece mirror propagation rules.
- Metadata enrichment: `MbidEnrichmentService` (Mapper cache via `cacheRead`/`cacheWrite` lambdas), `ImageEnrichmentService` (mosaic composite via platform `composeMosaic` lambda).
- AI orchestration: `AiChatService`, `AiRecommendationService`, `ChatContextProvider`, `ChatCardEnricher`. System prompts and tool-call protocol live in shared.
- AI providers (Ktor): `ChatGptProvider`, `ClaudeProvider`, `GeminiProvider`. Identical wire formats and prompt handling on both platforms.
- AI tool surface: `DjToolDefinitions` (schemas), `DjToolExecutor` interface (single-method dispatch).
- Settings: `SettingsStore` backed by `KvStore` (multiplatform-settings) + `SecureTokenStore` (encrypted tokens).
- Business logic: `ResolverScoring`, `ResolverModels`, `MetadataService`, `MetadataProvider` interface, `QueueManager`, `DeepLinkAction`, `PlaybackEngine` interface, `AppConfig`.

**iOS actuals (`shared/iosMain/`):**
- `Platform.ios.kt` — `Log` (NSLog), `randomUUID` (NSUUID), `currentTimeMillis` (NSDate)
- `DriverFactory.ios.kt` — SQLDelight Native driver
- `HttpClientFactory.ios.kt` — Ktor Darwin engine
- `PluginFileAccess.ios.kt` — `NSBundle` (bundled) + `NSApplicationSupportDirectory/plugins/` (cached)
- `KvStoreFactory.kt` — `NSUserDefaults` suite (`parachord_kmp_prefs`)
- `IosSecureTokenStore.kt` — Keychain via multiplatform-settings `KeychainSettings` (service `com.parachord.tokens`)

**What stays in :app (genuinely Android-only):**
- All Compose UI (~82 files), Android `ViewModel`s + `viewModelScope`
- Media3 stack: `PlaybackService`/`MediaSessionService`, `PlaybackController`, `PlaybackRouter`
- `SpotifyPlaybackHandler`, `AppleMusicPlaybackHandler` (`MusicKitWebBridge`), `SoundCloudPlaybackHandler`
- `JsBridge` (WebView), `NativeBridge`, `MediaScanner` (ContentResolver), `NetworkMonitor` (ConnectivityManager)
- `OAuthManager`, `OAuthRedirectActivity` (Custom Tabs), `CurrentActivityHolder`
- `PluginSyncService` (WorkManager scheduling — the underlying sync runs in shared)
- The Android `DjToolExecutor` concrete class (dispatches into `PlaybackController`); the interface lives in shared
- Entity files → typealiases to shared models (`TrackEntity = Track`, etc.)
- The `composeMosaicAndroid` function (Coil + Bitmap) forwarded into the shared `ImageEnrichmentService`

**Bridge pattern:** App-module files that were moved to shared become thin typealiases (e.g., `typealias TrackEntity = com.parachord.shared.model.Track`). This preserves all existing imports across the app without mass-renaming. The typealiases live in the original packages: `data/db/entity/`, `data/repository/`, `data/metadata/`, `ai/`, `ai/providers/`, `ai/tools/`, `plugin/`, `deeplink/`.

**Lambda forwarding pattern:** When a shared class needs platform-specific resources (file I/O, image decoding, MBID lookups), the shared signature takes a `suspend (...) -> X` lambda and the platform-specific Koin binding wires the closure. Examples:
- `cacheRead`/`cacheWrite: suspend () -> String?` for disk caches (`CriticalDarlingsRepository`, `RecommendationsRepository`, `FreshDropsRepository`, `ChartsRepository`, `LibraryRepository`, `MbidEnrichmentService`, `AiRecommendationService`). Every Discover card's repository saves its last answer this way, so a failed request shows the last list rather than an empty card (`ChartsRepository` joined them for #570: Apple's chart feed timing out at launch left Home's Pop of the Tops card empty).
- `composeMosaic: suspend (playlistId, urls) -> String?` for the Coil-based 2x2 mosaic in `ImageEnrichmentService`.
- `getPlaybackSnapshot: suspend () -> ChatPlaybackSnapshot` for `ChatContextProvider` reading from `PlaybackStateHolder`.
- `mbidEnrichTrack`/`mbidEnrichBatch` in `LibraryRepository` (kept for symmetry; `MbidEnrichmentService` is now itself shared).

## OAuth Architecture

OAuth flows use **PKCE** with a **state parameter** for CSRF protection.

- **OAuthManager** (`auth/OAuthManager.kt`) — launches Chrome Custom Tabs for Spotify, SoundCloud, Last.fm auth flows. Stores per-flow `PendingOAuthFlow(service, codeVerifier, createdAt)` keyed by a 192-bit random `state` parameter. Validates state on callback. 10-minute pruning of abandoned flows.
- **OAuthRedirectActivity** (`auth/OAuthRedirectActivity.kt`) — dedicated `noHistory="true"` / `Theme.NoDisplay` activity that receives `parachord://auth/callback/*` redirects. Handles the token exchange directly via Koin-injected `OAuthManager`, then brings MainActivity to front and finishes. This causes Chrome to close the Custom Tab automatically.
- **CurrentActivityHolder** (`app/CurrentActivityHolder.kt`) — tracks the currently-resumed Activity via `ActivityLifecycleCallbacks`. OAuthManager uses it to launch Custom Tabs from an Activity context (not Application context) so Chrome runs in the same task as MainActivity — enabling Android to pop Chrome off the back stack when the redirect fires.
- **Manifest intent filters** — `parachord://auth` goes ONLY to `OAuthRedirectActivity` (priority 999). All other `parachord://` hosts are enumerated explicitly on MainActivity to avoid disambiguation chooser overlap. Spotify Auth SDK and App Remote SDK were removed (they injected their own `RedirectUriReceiverActivity` that conflicted).

## Security Posture

A full security review was completed April 2026. The review plan is at `.claude/plans/logical-munching-waffle.md`. GitHub issues #101–#108 track remaining work.

**Completed:**
- C5: `allowBackup="false"` + `data_extraction_rules.xml` (no cloud backup of tokens)
- H2: Path traversal prevention in `PluginFileAccess.writeCachedPlugin`
- H5: OAuth state parameter + CSRF protection + per-flow verifier isolation
- H8: Release build fails if `CI_KEYSTORE_PATH` unset (no debug-keystore fallback)
- H9: `network_security_config.xml` denying cleartext traffic
- H10: XSPF SSRF block (HTTPS-only, private-IP rejection, every redirect hop re-checked, bodies capped while read: `fetchPublicPlaylistText`, GHSA-gfvj-rh43-vmp9) + XXE prevention
- M8: Keystore password from env var (not hardcoded)
- M12: Explicit `usesCleartextTraffic="false"`

**All findings closed** except the deferred plugin sandbox workstream:
- #107 Plugin sandbox workstream (C1 signing, C2 fetch allowlist, H1/H7/M1/M2) — requires breaking `.axe` SDK changes, deferred to separate design pass

**Key security rules:**
- **No credential reaches a log line.** The shared Ktor client's logging hides `SecretRedaction.SECRET_HEADERS` (`Authorization`, `x-api-key`, `x-goog-api-key`, `api-key`, `Music-User-Token`), passes every line through `SecretRedaction.redactSecrets` (query `key`, `api_key`, `apikey`, `access_token`, `refresh_token`, `token`, `t`, `s`, `sk`, `code`, `state`, `client_secret`, `code_verifier`; Bearer tokens; `sk-…` / `AIza…` literals) and logs nothing in release (`LogLevel.NONE`; iOS's default logger prints to stdout). Release is `AppConfig.isDebug` false: Android's `BuildConfig.DEBUG`, iOS's `Platform.isDebugBinary` (Xcode's Release configuration builds a release Shared framework); nothing else reads that flag. Android's OkHttp client, which `.axe` plugin fetches go through, does the same (`redactingLoggingInterceptor`: BASIC in debug, NONE in release; OkHttp 4.12 has no `redactQueryParams`). The plugin hosts' own lines are redacted too: `NativeBridge`'s fetch errors (`fetchErrorLine`), its `console.*` passthrough and failed-callback messages (a header JSON that won't parse is logged by size only), iOS's `JsPolyfills` console log and the SoundCloud widget's console. Add a new secret header or query name to `SecretRedaction`, once. Tests: `SecretRedactionTest`, `RedactingLoggingInterceptorTest`, `NativeBridgeLogLineTest`.
- **Never add secrets to BuildConfig expecting confidentiality.** The project is open source; BuildConfig values are trivially extractable. Use per-user BYO credentials (like AI providers do) for anything that needs confidentiality.
- **Plugin signing is defense-in-depth, not critical.** HTTPS transport + GitHub repo access controls are the primary defense against supply chain attacks. Sandboxing (C3 namespaced storage, C2 fetch allowlist) matters more — a signed plugin with unrestricted NativeBridge access is just as dangerous as an unsigned one.
- **MusicKit v3 `music.volume` has no effect on Android WebView.** DRM audio goes through MediaDrm/MediaCodec, bypassing the JS audio pipeline. Volume normalization between sources can't be solved app-side.
- **Global OkHttpClient User-Agent is required, and the contact URL must RESOLVE.** MetaBrainz's [2026-08 bot mitigation](https://blog.metabrainz.org/2026/08/24/blocked-listenbrainz-services-user-agent-strings/) blocks clients that identify themselves poorly, and okhttp's default `okhttp/4.x` is exactly the targeted pattern (Android is more exposed than desktop, whose Electron default at least embeds an app name). Every client — Android's Ktor `AppConfig.userAgent` and OkHttp interceptor, and iOS's `AppConfig` — builds its UA from the single shared `parachordUserAgent(version, platform)` (`shared/.../config/UserAgent.kt`; Android's `app/.../net/UserAgent.kt` is a thin wrapper), shaped `Parachord/<version> ( Android|iOS; https://parachord.com )` per MB's [rate-limiting doc](https://musicbrainz.org/doc/MusicBrainz_API/Rate_Limiting). iOS hard-coded `Parachord/0.1` until it moved onto the shared helper. **This pointed at `https://parachord.app` until #375 — a domain that does not resolve at all**, so the header looked compliant while giving MetaBrainz nowhere to reach us. If you change the contact URL, verify it actually returns 200. Don't create per-API OkHttp clients without inheriting the interceptor (`client.newBuilder()`).

- **MusicBrainz is throttled to 1 req/sec, serialized — don't undo it.** `MusicBrainzClient`'s gate is `Semaphore(1)` + a 1100ms gap (#375), honoring MB's published limit. It previously ran on the permissive default (`Semaphore(2)` + 150ms ≈ 6–13 req/sec) on the theory that a unique UA bought tolerance for bursts; the 2026-08 mitigation ended that. Concurrency 1 also serializes the three `/ws/2/` queries a global search fires — parallelism against a 1 req/sec server buys nothing but throttling. The gap counts from when the previous request FINISHED (`RateLimitGate.awaitRequestGap`, #475): a request after an idle spell starts at once, and a caller cancelled before its request is sent doesn't stamp the gap, so a superseded search can't delay the next one. Search cancels superseded queries (the shared `SearchSession` cancels the running search on the next keystroke; on iOS the search runs in Kotlin's scope, because cancelling a Swift `Task` doesn't cancel the Kotlin coroutine behind a suspend call) and shows each category as its providers answer (`MetadataService.search*Progressively`). A 429/503 is retried up to twice honoring `Retry-After`, and if it outlasts that the client sets `MusicBrainzClient.rateLimited` so the UI can say "rate-limited" instead of rendering an empty result as "No results" (a throttle has NOT established that nothing matched).

- **Never enable Ktor `HttpRequestRetry`'s status-based retry on the shared client.** Its DEFAULT policy retries 5xx; `installSharedPlugins` explicitly sets `retryIf { _, _ -> false }` and retries transport failures only. A 503 is how MusicBrainz says "you are over 1 req/sec", so retrying it triples requests against an already-throttled service — the exact re-poke `RateLimitGate` exists to prevent. This shipped briefly and was caught only because a 503 is 5xx while the retry tests originally covered just 429; `HttpRetryWiringTest` now pins both.
- **Apple Music developer token (JWT) — generated by the build; THREE copies.** The `AppConfig.appleMusicDeveloperToken` JWT is an ES256 token Apple caps at **180 days**. It gates far more than artist images: **an expired token makes MusicKit refuse to `configure()`, which takes down Apple Music playback, catalog search and resolution entirely.** It lives in three independent places:
  - **Android:** `local.properties:APPLE_MUSIC_DEVELOPER_TOKEN` (→ BuildConfig → AppConfig)
  - **iOS:** `iosApp/Parachord/Secrets.xcconfig:APPLE_MUSIC_DEVELOPER_TOKEN` (→ Info.plist `AppleMusicDeveloperToken` → `IosContainer.plist`)
  - **CI:** the GitHub Actions secret `APPLE_MUSIC_DEVELOPER_TOKEN` (`.github/workflows/build.yml`) — **release builds read this, not `local.properties`**

  **`scripts/apple-music-token.py` is the source of truth for minting it** (stdlib + `openssl`, no pip). `--check` reports every copy's expiry, `--write` mints and updates both files at once, `--ensure` rotates only inside the 30-day renewal window. The Android build calls `--ensure` at configure time, so a dev machine with `APPLE_MUSIC_AUTHKEY_P8` set in `local.properties` **auto-rotates before expiry and can never ship a dead token**. It degrades quietly where it can't help: no `.p8` (CI, fresh clone) → uses the supplied value; expired and unrotatable → loud gradle warning, build still succeeds. **The CI secret is the one copy the script cannot reach — rotate it by hand** (`gh secret set APPLE_MUSIC_DEVELOPER_TOKEN`). Because nothing renews it while dev machines silently do, its expiry would present as "works locally, broken in release" — so `.github/workflows/apple-music-token-expiry.yml` runs weekly, fails 30 days out (`--check-env`), and **files an issue**, since a red cron nobody looks at is not a signal.

  **Expiry is NOT a sign-in problem — never report it as one.** When this token lapsed in Sept 2026, `MusicKitWebBridge.configure()` emitted `signInRequired`, so the app told users to "Sign in to Apple Music" and bounced them to Settings — where tapping **Connect** re-entered `configure()`, re-fired the same toast, and discarded `authorize()`'s `false` return, giving zero feedback. An unwinnable loop: the user's Apple ID was never the problem. The bridge now emits a separate `developerTokenExpired` flow with its own honest message, and `connectAppleMusic()` surfaces authorization failures. Expiry parsing is shared + tested in `AppleMusicTokenExpiry` (`shared/.../api/`, `AppleMusicTokenExpiryTest`) — note it treats an *unparseable* token as NOT expired, since blocking a token we merely failed to read would lock users out of a working service.

  This is a separate token from the on-device *playback* token (the App Service token used by `ApplicationMusicPlayer` on iOS — see `iosApp/AGENTS.md`), and separate again from the **Music-User-Token** (a stale MUT breaks the *library* API only, while catalog + streaming keep working). Build-time generation (#186) now ships on BOTH platforms: Android via the Gradle `--ensure` call above, iOS via the **`Embed Apple Music developer token`** build phase (`iosApp/scripts/embed-apple-music-token.sh`), which runs the same script and stamps the result into the BUILT `Info.plist`. It has to stamp the built plist rather than feed an xcconfig — xcconfig values resolve before any build phase runs, so a phase physically cannot supply one; stamping after "Process Info.plist" and before code signing is also why a token rotated during a build reaches that same build. Both platforms are **fail-soft**: no `.p8` configured (an outside contributor, CI without the key) warns and keeps the existing value rather than breaking the build.

## Plugin Marketplace & `.axe` Repo Sync

`.axe` plugins are the **cross-platform source of truth** for resolvers, AI providers, meta-services, AND their behavior (model lists, filters, scrobble payloads). **Do NOT natively reimplement what an `.axe` already does** — see the rule in Common Mistakes below.

**Sync topology — the hub is the desktop monorepo, NOT the mobile repo:**
- **Authoring source:** `parachord-desktop/plugins-src/<id>/` (GitHub `Parachord/parachord`): `manifest.json` (the plugin's manifest, settings and everything else but the code) plus `implementation.js` (a real module, `module.exports = { search, resolve, … }`). Plugins are edited here. **The desktop repo holds no `.axe` files** (parachord#1099): `node scripts/build-axes.js` generates `plugins/<id>.axe` at build time, along with the bundled fallback list and the plugin's `marketplace-manifest.json` version; `--check` byte-compares and fails on drift.
- **Forward sync** (`parachord-desktop/.github/workflows/sync-repos.yml`): a push to monorepo `main` touching `plugins-src/**` or `marketplace-manifest.json` runs `build-axes.js`, then an unconditional `cp` of the generated `.axe` files to `Parachord/parachord-plugins` (the marketplace) + manifest merge. **NOT version-aware** — a stale monorepo plugin DOWNGRADES the marketplace (the #754 clobber class).
- **Reverse sync** (`reverse-sync.yml`, daily 6 AM UTC + dispatch): marketplace → monorepo, **version-aware (strictly-greater only, never downgrade)**. It adopts a marketplace `.axe` by importing it into `plugins-src/<id>/` (`build-axes.js --import --plugin <id>`) and opens a PR with that `plugins-src` diff. Pulls community contributions back.
- **Apps consume the marketplace at runtime** via `PluginSyncService` (fetches `manifest.json` from `raw.githubusercontent.com/Parachord/parachord-plugins/main/`, downloads newer `.axe`, semver-dedups vs the bundled copy — higher wins).

**Mobile (`parachord-android`) is OUTSIDE the desktop sync.** Its bundled copies — `app/src/main/assets/plugins/*.axe` (Android) AND `iosApp/Parachord/Resources/axe-plugins/*.axe` (iOS — a **separate** copy; `axe-plugins`, not `plugins`, because iOS reserves `PlugIns/`) — are an offline fallback; runtime sync delivers updates. A **one-direction** (marketplace → mobile) pull keeps the bundle fresh: `.github/workflows/sync-plugins.yml` + `.github/scripts/sync-bundled-plugins.js` (version-aware; updates only already-bundled plugins; never downgrades/adds/removes; PR). **No mobile → marketplace forward sync exists** — mobile never authors plugins, so one would only leak in-repo bundled edits upstream.

**Landing a plugin change — two paths:**
1. **Monorepo-first (safest):** edit `parachord-desktop/plugins-src/<x>/implementation.js` (or its `manifest.json`), bump `manifest.version` in `plugins-src/<x>/manifest.json`, run `node scripts/build-axes.js` (it syncs the `marketplace-manifest.json` entry's version), push → forward-sync builds and ships it. No clobber window. Never hand-edit a generated `.axe`.
2. **Direct-to-`parachord-plugins` (faster):** edit the marketplace `.axe` + **bump the version** (in the `.axe` AND `manifest.json`) → the reverse-sync imports it into `plugins-src` (strictly-greater) and PRs that back. Without a version bump the no-downgrade guard ignores it. **Clobber window:** until that reverse PR merges into the monorepo, any monorepo `plugins-src/**` push rebuilds and `cp`s the older monorepo version over your change — merge the reverse PR promptly.

**Verify against the live remote, not the local checkout.** A local `parachord-plugins` clone may sit on a stale feature branch (it was once 50 commits behind `main`, which made the marketplace *look* drifted when it wasn't). Always check `origin/main` / the `raw.githubusercontent.com/.../main/` URL.

## DJ Chat / Shuffleupagus — shared, both platforms (#223 / #202)

- **Reuse the shared stack.** `AiChatService` (the tool loop), the 3 providers, `DjToolDefinitions`, `ChatContextProvider`, `ChatCardEnricher`, `ChatMessageDao` (per-provider history) all live in `commonMain`. A platform needs only: a `DjToolExecutor` impl (8-tool dispatch), container wiring, and the UI. Don't re-derive any of this.
- **System prompt = desktop's full `AI_CHAT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`** (`app.js` ~L5286), ported verbatim into `ChatContextProvider.buildSystemPrompt()` (scoped to the 8 mobile tools), plus one mobile line desktop lacks: a mix, playlist or "some songs" with no number gets 10 tracks, a number the user names is used exactly, and an album gets all its tracks. Don't author an abridged prompt — the personality, the "you MUST call the tool (saying ≠ doing)" guardrail, the album-vs-track queueing rules (never `play`+`queue_add` together), strict personalization, and the card-syntax / common-mistakes blocks all change behavior.
- **Per-provider history — STAMP the timestamp.** `ChatMessage.toEntity()` MUST set `timestamp = currentTimeMillis()`. `ChatMessageRecord` defaults to `0L`, and `AiChatService.historyFor()` prunes `timestamp < now-30d` on every load — so a 0 timestamp means history is deleted on the next load / app restart (never remembered across sessions). Was a silent bug on BOTH platforms.
- **Cards trigger the player via DIRECT calls, not `parachord://`** (desktop 20508+ / Android `onPlayTrack`). Syntax: `{{track|t|a|al}}` / `{{album|t|a}}` / `{{artist|n}}` parsed from the assistant text.
- **The chat shows the user's messages and each turn's final reply, nothing in between** (`AiChatService.getDisplayMessages`, both apps), as desktop's panel does (`handleAiChatSend` appends only `response.content`). The tool loop's interim assistant messages (they carry tool calls: a preamble, often blank, or the model reacting to a search before it retries) stay in the history the model sees; while the turn runs, the progress text ("Searching…") fills the gap. A turn's error ("I encountered an error while processing. Please try again.") is a final reply and shows. A turn never ends blank: a reply with no text after tools (or the 5-round cap reached mid-loop) says what the tools did (shared `ToolSummary`, a port of desktop's `tool-summary.js`: "Added 3 tracks to queue. Shuffle enabled."), and a blank reply with no tools shows "Done." (`ToolSummary.DONE`). Tests: `ToolSummaryTest`.
- **A message is laid out by the shared `ChatContent`** (`shared/.../ai/ChatContent.kt`, desktop's `renderChatContent`); both chat screens only draw its `ChatBlock`s (header, card line, numbered item, blank, line of `ChatInline`s: text, card, link, bold, italic, code). Punctuation around cards goes, as on desktop: text after a card drops its leading `[\s.,!?;:*]+`, text before a card drops a trailing `*`-run, and a line after a card line drops its leading `[\s.,!?;:]+`; a numbered item that is a card centres its number on it, 4 px apart. One difference: a line of two cards is two cards (desktop's greedy whole-line pattern reads it as one mangled card). `ChatContent.convertPlainTextToCards` is desktop's fallback for names written in prose; `AiChatService` applies it before storing a reply (as desktop does) and the screens apply it to older saved messages. A playlist card (`{{playlist|name|id|count}}`) opens the saved playlist it names, by id then by name (shared `ChatPlaylistCards`, desktop's playlist card); one the model never saved offers to save the tracks the message lists (`ChatContent.messageTracks`) and opens the saved copy (desktop opens them as a temporary playlist with Save), and with no tracks says "Shuffleupagus didn’t make this playlist…"; a tap or save that fails says "Couldn’t open the playlist. Try again." / "Couldn’t save the playlist. Try again." (`ChatPlaylistCards.OPEN_FAILED_MESSAGE` / `SAVE_FAILED_MESSAGE`). **Only safe links are links** (`ChatContent.isOpenableLink`): an http(s) URL, or a `parachord://` link whose command is one desktop's chat handles in the app (`IN_APP_LINK_COMMANDS`: artist, album, play, queue, playlist, search, from `parseInlineMarkdown`'s link branch); any other link (`intent:`, `tel:`, `file:`, `javascript:`, another `parachord://` command) shows as its text. Desktop hands every other link to `shell.openExternal`. A kept `parachord://` link goes through the app's own link handling (Android `ChatScreen`'s `UriHandler` → `DeepLinkViewModel.handleUri`; iOS `ChatScreen`'s `openURL` action → `RootView.handleDeepLink`, closing the chat first for a page link, `inAppLinkOpensPage`), and an http(s) one opens in the browser (Android catches `ActivityNotFoundException`). Tests: `ChatContentTest`, `ChatPlaylistCardsTest`.
- **Card covers follow desktop's `ChatCard`** (`ChatCardEnricher`): an artist's photo from the artist-photo chain (`MetadataService.getArtistImage`), an album's from the album-art pipeline (`ImageEnrichmentService.lookupAlbumArt`), a track's from its album; a track card with no album first searches for the song (`selectBestMatch`, never another song's cover) to find its album, else uses the matched song's art, then the store catalogs' song search (`CatalogTrackArtwork`: iTunes, then Deezer, a port of desktop's `album-art-catalog.js` track half, `getTrackArt`). A cover found is kept for the session, a miss for 30 minutes (`MISS_TTL_MS`), and a lookup that threw isn't kept; cards asking at once share one lookup. Tests: `ChatCardEnricherTest`, `CatalogTrackArtworkTest`.
- **The tools search the person's streaming services, as desktop's `searchResolvers` does.** `search`, `play`, `queue_add` and `create_playlist` all search through the shared `ResolverSearch` (`shared/.../resolver/ResolverSearch.kt`, built by `streamingResolverSearch` on both apps), not the metadata search: native Spotify search (through `SpotifyClient`'s rate-limit gate, only with a Spotify sign-in), the Apple Music catalog (only once connected), the local files, and every other plugin declaring `capabilities.search` through `PluginManager.search` (the unique-key poll; a plugin that isn't configured isn't asked). The natives pass the coordinator's native gate (active list empty, as on Android, or naming them), the plugins its `.axe` gate (`ResolverGating.axeResolverIds(…, capability = "search")`: active, not switched off). All are asked at once; each service's results stay in the order it gave them, and the lists are joined in the person's resolver order, unranked, each row tagged with its resolver in `provider`. A service that throws (offline, a cooldown, a plugin timeout) is skipped. The `search` tool answers desktop's shape (`artist`, `title`, `album`, `source`, the first 10 by default, `total`, "No results found for …"; `searchToolResult`); `play` also reports the `source`. The metadata search it replaced (MusicBrainz first) answered "IDLES" with ten recordings titled "Idles" by other artists, and the model gave up and showed artist cards. Desktop asks the services one after the other; mobile asks them in parallel and joins in order.
- **A tool never takes the first search result (#520, desktop parachord#1241).** `play`, `queue_add` and `create_playlist` look tracks up through `DjTrackRequests.kt` (`selectBestMatch` over every result of the resolver search; desktop's `play` and `queue_add` stop at the first service, in the person's order, with an exact artist + title match (`earlyReturn`), while mobile asks every service at once and the exact tier picks the highest-ranked exact match, which is usually the same track) and report a miss to the AI: `play` returns `success: false` and changes nothing; `queue_add` and `create_playlist` leave the track out and list it in `notFound`. If `queue_add`'s first requested track is the missing one, it starts with the first track found, a deliberate difference from desktop, which starts nothing. With every track missing, nothing changes and no playlist is made. Nothing downstream can catch a wrong result, since it resolves against its own names, and a wrong song saved to a playlist can sync out to other services.
- **The tools' candidates include local files (#529).** The catalogs don't know a track that exists only as a file, and nothing answers with no network, so `findRequestedTrack` adds the local files whose title holds the requested title after the search's rows (the resolver search also asks local files, by "artist title"), and `selectBestMatch` still decides. The catalog's row wins when both have the exact track, since its ids spare the resolver a search. The resolver then finds the file by the row's names, and the source order decides what plays.
- **Model lists come from the `.axe`, never native.** Read the plugin's model setting: static `select` → its curated `options`; `dynamic-select` → `listModels` (live) falling back to the plugin's curated `fallbackOptions` when empty/keyless; seed the picker with the plugin's `default` (desktop: `metaServiceConfigs[id].model || modelSetting.default`, `app.js:59532`). On iOS the `.axe` async (`listModels`) needs the JSC unique-key poll — see `iosApp/AGENTS.md`.

## Spinoff + Listen Along — queue model (#231 / #235 / #246)

Both are iOS ports of Android's `PlaybackController`; **read desktop + Android FIRST.** A by-eye first cut of Spinoff got the model wrong (pool-as-queue + immediate kick-start) and had to be rebuilt against Android.

- **Spinoff = a SEPARATE hidden pool, NOT a replaced queue.** On start the user's `QueueManager` queue is NEVER modified — only the playback `context` is saved (`preSpinoffContext`); the current song KEEPS PLAYING (`kickStartFirstTrack=false`); `skipNext`/auto-advance pull from the pool; on exhaustion `exitSpinoff` restores the context and the untouched queue resumes. The first pool track puts the seed (the queue track playing when the spinoff started) in history inside the repeat-all lap, so it comes round when the queue wraps; pool tracks stay out of the lap (`QueueManager.poolTrackStarted`, see "Queue advance and repeat"). The Spinoff button (the fork icon on every platform; sparkles is Shuffleupagus) toggles (purple when active). Mutually exclusive with Listen Along (each exits the other; leaving a spinoff while its similar tracks are still being fetched cancels the fetch, so they can't start it later and end a session started meanwhile: shared `spinoffExit`, #612): starting Listen Along exits a running spinoff first, and a spinoff ends a session silently once its pool exists (both apps report `UserPlaybackAction.SPINOFF_STARTED` as the pool is set), so with no similar tracks the session carries on. A spinoff started from a session never saves the Listen Along context to come back to.
- **The pool comes from the shared `buildSpinoffPool` (#534, desktop parachord#1281, a port of `spinoff-pool.js`).** Last.fm `track.getsimilar&limit=20`, shuffled. Last.fm has no similar tracks for some of the most played songs ("Shape of You", "Perfect"), so below `MIN_SIMILAR_TRACKS` (5) it adds `artist.getsimilar&limit=8` → `artist.gettoptracks&limit=3` each, dealt round-robin (`dealRoundRobin`: no two in a row by one artist), after the similar tracks; seed and duplicates dropped by `normalizeStr` key. **A failed request is not an empty answer:** only an empty list or Last.fm error 6 falls back (`LastFmSpinoffSource` reads `error` off the response); any other failure stops with "Failed to fetch similar tracks" and asks nothing more. The Spinoff availability check is `checkSpinoffAvailable`: `track.getsimilar&limit=1`, then `artist.getsimilar&limit=1` only when that's empty; a failure answers false. `buildSpinoffPool` hands back metadata-only tracks (`spinoffTrack`, one id scheme, `spinoff:<title>|<artist>`, on both apps) in the pool's order: don't shuffle them again, or the round-robin is lost.
- **Pool tracks resolve when they play, on both apps (#584).** Nothing in the pool is resolved when it's built, so a spinoff starts without a burst of lookups. The next `SPINOFF_PRERESOLVE_AHEAD` (3) tracks (`spinoffTracksToPreresolve`) are resolved at start and after each advance, which fills their source badges and starts them quickly. That holds for a radio link's pool and for each refill too: only the next 3 are resolved, never the whole batch. Android skips a pool track that has no source (`PlayFailure.NO_SOURCE`).
- **Shared session rules (`shared/.../playback/SpinoffSession.kt`).** `spinoffNeedsRefill` (fewer than 3 are left once the next track is taken; both apps check after removing it), `shuffleAllowed(spinoffMode, playbackContextType)`, `LISTEN_ALONG_CONTEXT`, `spinoffTrack` and `firstPlayableIndex`. The refill is the shared `PoolRefiller` (`shared/.../playback/PoolRefiller.kt`, moved from Android): at most one refill every 5 s, stops after 3 empty or failed refills, drops duplicates by recording MBID, then by artist and title. A refill that lands after the spinoff ended, or after the refill URL changed, is dropped (Android checks the URL, iOS a generation counter). iOS gets its instance from `IosContainer.makeSpinoffRefiller()`.
- **Shuffle is off during a spinoff or Listen Along.** Every path goes through `shuffleAllowed`: Android's `toggleShuffle` / `setShuffle` (the Now Playing button is disabled, and the radio link, the DJ tool and the media session are refused), iOS's `setShuffle` (the radio link acks `SHUFFLE_UNAVAILABLE`). The words for a refused shuffle and for the other spinoff outcomes are the shared `PlaybackMessages` (`noSimilarTracks`, `spinningOff`, `spinoffFrom`, `SPINOFF_FETCH_FAILED`, `noPlayableSimilarTracks`, `SHUFFLE_UNAVAILABLE`), so the phones, the DJ chat and the cars say the same thing. The media session's shuffle is Parachord's own, not ExoPlayer's (see "Queue advance and repeat").
- **A radio link's artist seed must have something that plays.** Before it replaces the current playback, `firstPlayableIndex` checks the first `RADIO_PLAYABLE_PROBE` (5) pool tracks (Android `PlaybackController.isPlayableNow`, which reads the resolver cache first through `TrackResolverCache.resolveTrack`; iOS `resolveRadio`). If none plays it refuses with `noPlayableSimilarTracks` and the current playback keeps going, and it starts at the first one that does.
- **UP NEXT shows the station SEED** ("Spinoff from `<track>` by `<artist>`"), NOT the next pool track. The open queue shows the user's queue dimmed as **"YOUR QUEUE"** with **"··"** track numbers (suspended state, also used by Listen Along).
- **Queue count badge turns GRAY (`#6B7280`)** while spun off / listening along, purple otherwise (desktop `app.js:55180`).
- **Listen Along runs on one shared session and one shared host (#585).** `ListenAlongSession` (`shared/.../playback/ListenAlongSession.kt`) holds the rules: it's told what happened (`start`, `onPoll`, `onSongEnded`, `onTrackUnplayable`, `onUserPaused` / `onUserResumed`, `onCatchUp`, `onUserSkipped`, `onUserPlayedSomethingElse`, `onUserStopped`) and returns `ListenAlongEffect`s, in order: `Play`, `Message`, `End(restoreQueue, startPlayback)`. `ListenAlongHost` (`shared/.../playback/ListenAlongHost.kt`) does the rest for both apps: it suspends the user's queue, works out whether Listen Along's own song is playing, maps the player's hooks and `UserPlaybackAction`s (shared enum: skip, play something else, pause, resume, spinoff started) to the session, and does its effects through a `ListenAlongPlatform` (play, the current queue, exit spinoff, restore the queue playing or paused, clear, stop and clear, a message, release a held track, ended, state changed). Each platform keeps only the 15 s poll loop (`LISTEN_ALONG_POLL_MS`), the UI state and the glue: Android's `ListenAlongManager` (`app/.../playback/ListenAlongManager.kt`, a singleton the playback service creates, so a session outlives the app's screen and runs with none, #611; it also holds a partial wake lock while a session runs, and `MainViewModel` only observes it and shows its messages), iOS's `ListenAlongController`. Don't add host logic on either side. The player knows nothing about friends; it reports through hooks: Android `PlaybackController.onOwnTrackEnded`, `onTrackUnplayable`, `onUserAction`, `drivesTrack`, `onTrackStarting`; iOS `QueuePlaybackCoordinator.onTrackFinished`, `onTrackUnplayable`, `onUserAction`, `onTrackStarting`. Call `PlaybackController` on the main thread: its entry points log an error off it and don't re-post themselves.
- **Start** exits a running spinoff, shows "Listening along with X" and plays what the friend is playing, or waits for the first poll when nothing is known. The user's queue is suspended (shown dimmed as "YOUR QUEUE") and kept when switching to another friend. A `parachord://listen-along` link does the same on both apps: it only exits a spinoff first and doesn't clear the queue, so the whole queue comes back when the session ends (#613; desktop's link path still clears it, [parachord#1343](https://github.com/Parachord/parachord/issues/1343)). The friend's track is metadata-only (`listenAlongTrack`, id `listen-along:<title>|<artist>`) and resolves when it plays, like any other; don't resolve it first (Android once did and dropped it under the 0.60 floor).
- **The friend's next track waits for ours.** A poll with a new song while ours plays is held, and plays when ours ends, so each song scrobbles; it's held while the user has paused too, and plays when they press Play. The `isPlaying` the platform passes to `onPoll` is for Listen Along's own song only, and is true while that song is starting or loading too (Android `PlaybackController.isPlayStarting`, iOS `isStarting`).
- **Our song ends and the friend hasn't moved on** (the last poll showed the same song): "X hasn't moved on - resuming your queue", and the suspended queue plays from its first track. **When the friend's position is unknown** (the last poll failed, or none has answered since the song started), the session waits for the next poll with the track kept on screen, not playing (`holdEndedTrack` on both). **Play does nothing during that wait, on both**, and the same after an unplayable friend track the session took (`ListenAlongHost` answers a resume while it holds the track; the players also keep `heldForListener`, Android's covering the media session's play on an ended local player too), until the next poll plays a track or the session ends (`releaseListenerHold`; Play then does what it does on any stopped track). A queue restored at launch never keeps a Listen Along context (`QueueManager.restoreState`), since no session survives a restart.
- **The friend stops.** A not-listening poll ends the session after the current song, or at once if nothing is playing, with "X stopped listening". If the user had paused, their queue comes back **paused** (`End(startPlayback = false)`: its first track on screen, Play starts it; Android `restoreQueuePaused`, iOS `setQueuePaused`), not playing out loud. A failed poll is not "stopped": `LISTEN_ALONG_FAILED_POLLS_LIMIT` (4) failed polls in a row, about a minute, count as it. `FriendsRepository.pollListenAlong` tells the three apart (`ListenAlongPoll.Listening` / `NotListening` / `Failed`, in a `ListenAlongPollResult` with the refreshed friend for the UI): its lookups throw on a failed request (`ListenBrainzClient.getPlayingNowOrThrow` / `getRecentListensOrThrow`), and `listenAlongPollOf` reads the friend (a hidden Last.fm profile counts as not listening). `refreshFriendActivity` and `fetchTransientFriendNowPlaying` still read a failure as "nothing playing", so don't poll with them.
- **The user takes control.** Now Playing's Next catches up to the friend (`onCatchUp`, enabled only while the friend is on a song we haven't played: `friendIsAhead`). Next or Previous anywhere else (the mini player, the lock screen, the car, headphones) ends the session with "Stopped listening along with X" and resumes the user's queue; with nothing suspended it stops and clears the track instead, and the skip isn't acted on again (no advance, so repeat all can't replay the friend's track). Playing something else ends it with the same message and keeps what the user chose, dropping the suspended queue; a `parachord://` play link (track, album, playlist) counts as that on both apps (Android ends the session in `ProtocolPlayTeardown.prepareForNewPlayback(endListenAlongSilently = false)`, before the link's first track resolves, so a poll can't start the friend's song in between). A spinoff and a radio link end it silently, dropping the suspended queue.
- **An unplayable friend track** (one that can't start, or a local file or stream that fails partway through, #618) shows no error or snackbar; the session plays a held track if there is one, else waits for the friend's next one. Android keeps external mode and the foreground service for it (`failedTrackStop(..., followed)` → `KEEP_EXTERNAL_FOREGROUND`, then `holdForListener`), and iOS keeps its silent keepalive, so the next track can start with the screen off. A session that ends at a song's end with nothing to resume stops and clears the track, as the end of the queue does (`stopAndClear`).
- **Known limits.** A pause made outside Parachord (in Spotify's own app, an iOS route change) isn't seen as the user's pause. On iOS, a pause sent as a remote command (AirPods ear detection) counts as the user's pause. A song held on iOS's AVPlayer engine (a local file or direct stream) has no background keep-alive while it waits. A friend followed through a link (not saved) gets the same poll answer as a saved one for the same account (#610): ListenBrainz's playing-now first, else their latest listen, counted for 10 minutes (`Friend.isOnAir`), and Last.fm's now-playing flag only when a scrobble from the last 30 minutes backs it, else the latest scrobble (`lastFmActivityTimestamp`). Reading only playing-now had ended a session when our song finished in a radio station's gap between songs (seen with KUTX, Nov 2026). After a queue is given back paused, the lock screen can still name the friend's track until Play.
- **Tests:** `ListenAlongSessionTest`, `ListenAlongHostTest` and `ListenAlongPollMappingTest` (commonTest, JVM + iOS), `PlaybackControllerListenerHooksTest` (a handled track end, skip or resume isn't acted on again; the paused restore), `FriendsListenAlongPollTest`, `ForwardingPlayerSessionPlayPauseTest`, `ForwardingPlayerRepeatTest` and `LoadOnPlayTest` (app).
- **Scrobbling metadata-only tracks (both features):** their Track entities have `duration: 0`. The scrobble THRESHOLD reads the live ENGINE duration (so it fires), but the LB submit must OMIT `duration_ms <= 0` or LB 400s the whole listen. Shared fix in `ListenBrainzClient.submitListens` + `ListenBrainzScrobbler` (guarded by `ListenBrainzSubmitListensTest`); applies to ANY metadata-only track (weekly playlists, recommendations, DJ chat).

## Common Mistakes to Avoid

### Both Platforms

1. **Don't assume provider responsibilities.** MusicBrainz handles discography/tracklists. Last.fm handles bios/images. Check the desktop first.
2. **Don't use `sources.firstOrNull()` / `sources.first`.** Always use `ResolverScoring.selectBest()` (or its iOS equivalent) for source selection.
3. **Don't skip the resolver pipeline.** Even if you have a direct URL, route through `ResolverManager` → `ResolverScoring` → `PlaybackRouter` to maintain consistent behavior.
4. **Don't use blue as the accent color.** The brand accent is purple (`#7c3aed` light / `#a78bfa` dark).
5. **Don't return or select sources below the confidence floor.** `ResolverScoring.selectBest()` filters out sources with confidence < `MIN_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` (0.60). `scoreConfidence()` returns 0.95 ONLY when BOTH title AND artist substring-match — single-axis matches (wrong artist, or wrong title) collapse to 0.50 so the floor filters them. This mirrors desktop's `validateResolvedTrack` two-stage gate exactly: validation pass + priority sort. A title-only match (e.g., a different artist's "Mariana") must NOT win against a correct local file just because applemusic has higher priority. If porting to iOS, replicate this gate.
6. **Don't natively reimplement `.axe` plugin functionality — ASK FIRST.** The `.axe`/marketplace plugin is the cross-platform source of truth: portability, marketplace updates, "one place for everyone." If a plugin already provides something — `resolve`, `listModels`, the model `options`/`fallbackOptions`/`default`, scrobble, AI chat — **call the plugin, don't write a native Kotlin/Swift equivalent.** A native reimplementation forks behavior and goes stale (we shipped a native per-provider `listModels` when `chatgpt.axe`/`gemini.axe` already had one — #223; it was reverted). **Before writing any native reimplementation of `.axe` logic, STOP and check with the user, with a concrete reason** (e.g. the `.axe` genuinely can't do it on this platform). The ONE legitimate native layer is the host GLUE that *runs* the `.axe` (e.g. iOS's JavaScriptCore unique-key polling for async `.axe` calls, the `NativeBridge` fetch polyfill) — never a reimplementation of the plugin's own logic. **Corollary (dispatch side): don't hardwire per-service hosts/logic into a feature** (`when (host) { "open.spotify.com" -> … }`) — route through the resolver registry (`findResolverForUrl` + `lookupPlaylist`/`resolve`/…) so new service plugins drop in with zero feature code. See "Extensibility principle — abstract over the plugin registry" under the .axe Plugin System section.

### KMP / shared/commonMain rules

When writing or editing code in `shared/src/commonMain/`, the JVM stdlib is NOT available — only Kotlin stdlib + Kotlin/Native bridges + the dependencies declared in `shared/build.gradle.kts`. These JVM-isms compile fine on Android then break the iOS build:

1. **Don't use `System.currentTimeMillis()`.** Use the shared `com.parachord.shared.platform.currentTimeMillis()` expect (which delegates to `System.currentTimeMillis()` on Android and `NSDate().timeIntervalSince1970 * 1000` on iOS).
2. **Don't use `java.util.UUID.randomUUID().toString()`.** Use the shared `com.parachord.shared.platform.randomUUID()` expect.
3. **Don't use `@Volatile` without an import.** The default-resolved `kotlin.jvm.Volatile` is JVM-only. Add `import kotlin.concurrent.Volatile` (the KMP version).
4. **Don't use `Map<K,V>.toSortedMap()`.** It returns `java.util.TreeMap` and doesn't exist on Native. Use `.entries.sortedBy { it.key }` instead.
5. **Don't use `java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap` / `newKeySet()`.** Replace with `kotlinx.coroutines.sync.Mutex` + a plain `MutableMap` / `MutableSet` (suspend lock around access). The 5 enrichment + repository moves all follow this pattern.
6. **Don't use `Dispatchers.IO`.** commonMain only has `Default` and `Main`. The shared DAOs already wrap their queries in `withContext(Dispatchers.Default)`, and Ktor is async by default — most call sites can just drop the `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` wrapper.
7. **Don't use `SecurityException`, `IOException`, `IllegalThreadStateException`, etc.** Stick to Kotlin's `IllegalArgumentException` / `IllegalStateException` / generic `Exception`. The exception type rarely matters — callers usually catch `Exception` anyway.
8. **Don't use `java.io.File` / `java.text.SimpleDateFormat` / `java.util.Date` / `java.util.Locale`.** For dates, use `kotlinx-datetime` (`Clock.System.todayIn`, `LocalDate`, `DateTimeUnit`). For file I/O, see the lambda forwarding pattern below.
9. **Lambda forwarding for platform-specific resources.** When a shared class needs file I/O, image decoding, MBID lookups, or anything else that's platform-tied, the shared signature takes a `suspend (...) -> X` lambda and the platform-specific Koin binding wires the closure. See the "Lambda forwarding pattern" section under "KMP Shared Module" for examples (`cacheRead`/`cacheWrite`, `composeMosaic`, `getPlaybackSnapshot`, etc.).
10. **Use `internal` sparingly across modules.** `internal` in `shared/commonMain` means visible within the `:shared` module. Code in `:app` (different Gradle module) can't see internals. If a helper needs to be tested from `:app` tests, make it `public` (or move the test into shared).
11. **Don't name a Swift-facing model property `description`** (or `hash`, `debugDescription` — any `NSObject` member). On the iOS side a Kotlin `description` property is shadowed by ObjC's `NSObject.description`, so Swift reads back the data-class `toString()` instead of the field value — a silent mis-render, not a compile error. Use `summary` / `blurb` / `text`. This applies to any `data class` in `shared/` (commonMain or iosMain) that Swift consumes. Shipped + fixed once on the iOS Discover screen.
12. **Don't call a `crossinline` builder lambda bare inside a Ktor request block — bind its receiver with `apply()`.** `LastFmClient.guardedGet` built each request as `httpClient.get(BASE_URL) { build(); parameter("format","json") }` where `build: crossinline HttpRequestBuilder.() -> Unit`. A bare `build()` does **not** bind its receiver to the `get` builder, so it silently no-ops — every Last.fm request went out with only `format=json` and **no** `method`/`artist`/`api_key`, Last.fm returned 400 error 6, and every Last.fm call (artist top tracks/info, charts, friends, recent tracks) came back **empty**. Fails identically on JVM (Android) and Native (iOS); was latent + invisible on Android because Spotify supplied the same metadata. Fix: `httpClient.get(BASE_URL) { apply(build); … }`. Regression guard: `LastFmClientParamsTest`. (June 2026, #183.) General lesson: when a request-builder lambda's params silently vanish, suspect an unbound receiver.
13. **Kotlin/Native's `Regex` rejects `\p{N}` — and a throwing top-level val poisons the whole FILE.** The JVM accepts every Unicode class; Native accepts `\p{L}`, `\p{M}`, `\p{Nd}`, `\p{Lu}`, `\p{IsL}` but throws `PatternSyntaxException: No such character class` on `\p{N}` and `\p{IsAlphabetic}`. Use the shared `LETTER_OR_NUMBER_CLASS` (`\p{L}\p{Nd}\p{Nl}\p{No}`, `ResolverModels.kt`) — N is exactly Nd ∪ Nl ∪ No. The failure mode is the dangerous part: a top-level `val X = Regex(...)` that throws during Native file initialization makes EVERY function in that file throw `FileFailedToInitializeException` (a Kotlin `Error`) — in #404 that would have taken down `scoreConfidence`/`validateIsrc`/`selectBestMatch` on iOS while all 1,300+ JVM tests stayed green. **Any change touching regex classes, Unicode, or string normalization in `commonMain` must run `./gradlew :shared:iosSimulatorArm64Test`** — the JVM suite cannot see this class of bug. CI now runs it too (`.github/workflows/ios-tests.yml`, on every PR/push touching `shared/**` or build config) — but run it locally before pushing; CI is the backstop, not the first look.
14. **No `,` (or `. ; : / < > [ ] ( ) \`) inside backtick test names in `commonTest`.** Legal on the JVM, a compile error on Kotlin/Native — and one bad name fails the ENTIRE `compileTestKotlinIosSimulatorArm64`, silently disabling every shared test on iOS. Five such names (#370, #387) kept the iOS suite dark for ~5 weeks until #404. Use ` — ` instead of `, `.

> **iOS app guide:** everything iOS-specific — Xcode `project.pbxproj` mechanics, the full set of KMP→Swift bridging gotchas (primitive `Double` bridges directly with no `.doubleValue`; big initializers overwhelm Swift's type-checker in closures; flatten nested response types to DTOs; `FlowWatcher` for collecting Flows), JavaScriptCore polyfill split (Kotlin owns lifecycle, Swift attaches callables), CoreGraphics/UIKit (`UIImageJPEGRepresentation`, `format.scale = 1.0`), and the simulator build/screenshot workflow — lives in **`iosApp/AGENTS.md`**. Read it before touching `iosApp/` or `shared/src/iosMain/`.

### Android-Specific

5. **Don't double URL-decode.** Jetpack Navigation already decodes URI path segments. Use `Uri.encode()` for encoding (not `URLEncoder.encode()` which uses `+` for spaces).
6. **Don't play unresolved tracks without on-the-fly resolution.** Ephemeral tracks (weekly playlists, recommendations, DJ chat results) have no `resolver`, `sourceUrl`, or streaming IDs when first created — they're just metadata (title/artist/album). `PlaybackController.playTrackInternal` will resolve them on-the-fly if needed, but for best UX call `TrackResolverCache.resolveInBackground()` when tracks are loaded so resolver badges appear and playback starts faster.
7. **Don't use grids for horizontally-scrollable content on mobile.** Desktop's multi-column grids don't work on narrow screens — use `LazyRow` carousels with fixed-width cards instead. Home's rows and halves use this pattern (140 dp cards, `RecCardWidth`).
8. **Ephemeral playlists need their own screen and ViewModel.** Weekly playlists from ListenBrainz are not stored in Room — they use `WeeklyPlaylistScreen`/`WeeklyPlaylistViewModel` with a "Save" button (matching desktop's ephemeral playlist pattern). Don't try to reuse `PlaylistDetailScreen` which expects a Room-backed `PlaylistEntity`.
9. **Don't use `isSystemInDarkTheme()` in composables.** The app has a manual dark/light/system toggle. `isSystemInDarkTheme()` only checks the Android system setting, not the app preference. Use `ParachordTheme.isDark` instead, which reads from `LocalIsDarkTheme` provided by `ParachordTheme`. See the Dark Mode section above.
10. **Don't block the Apple Music polling loop.** Never call `withContext` on a suspend function that awaits a network response (like `MusicKitWebBridge.evaluate()` / `preload()`) inline in `startAppleMusicStatePolling()`. This freezes position updates and the safety-net auto-advance check. Use `scope.launch` (fire-and-forget) for any async work that doesn't need its result in the same loop iteration.
11. **Don't use inconsistent null defaults in filter/count pairs.** When counting items by a nullable field and filtering by it, both operations must normalize nulls the same way. E.g., `groupBy { it.type?.lowercase() ?: "album" }` counts null as "album", but `filter { it.type?.lowercase() == "album" }` excludes null. Use `((it.type?.lowercase()) ?: "album")` in both places.
12. **Don't call `router.stopExternalPlayback()` before `playTrackInternal()` in advance paths.** The async `stop()` races with the new `play()` — MusicKit's deferred stop arrives after the new track starts, pausing it. `handler.play()` replaces the queue directly for same-handler transitions. See "External Playback Background Survival" section.
13. **Don't call `ctrl.stop()` during external→external transitions.** Stopping ExoPlayer triggers `MediaSessionService` auto-demotion from foreground. Skip `stop()`/`prepare()` when `wasAlreadyExternal` is true — just update metadata with `setMediaItems()`.
14. **Don't call `WebView.onResume()`/`resumeTimers()` from Activity lifecycle callbacks.** These can block the main thread when the Chromium renderer is suspending, causing a background ANR. Call `keepAlive()` from `MusicKitWebBridge.play()` instead (runs in foreground before each track).
15. **Don't use `LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE` on the MusicKit WebView.** The headless WebView doesn't render visually; hardware acceleration causes GPU context teardown on screen-off that disrupts audio. Use `LAYER_TYPE_NONE`.
16. **Don't call `MusicKitWebBridge.preload()` during active playback.** The catalog API request through the same MusicKit JS instance disrupts the active audio stream. Pre-resolve the Apple Music ID only; skip the actual `preload()` call.
17. **Don't run end-detection on stale Apple Music poll data.** When `pollPlaybackState()` times out (Main thread deferred in Doze), cached `isPlaying` may be stale. Only check `isOurTrackDone()` when the poll succeeded.
18. **Don't fire Spotify's stale-position watchdog during poll failures.** If API polls are failing (Doze network delays), position won't update — but that doesn't mean the track stopped. Only trigger the 15s stale-position watchdog when polls answer: no failures AND a poll answered within 3 s (`staleWatchdogFires`). A request on a cut network hangs 30 s without counting as a failure.
19. **Don't use the Spotify launch intent as the primary wake strategy.** It opens Spotify's activity, which is jarring UX. Use the media button broadcast (invisible) first; only fall back to the launch intent after 2s if the broadcast fails (Spotify fully killed). Do NOT immediately bring our app back to front after launching — this causes Android to deprioritize Spotify before it can initialize and register as a Connect device.
20. **Don't assume `deviceVerified` means Spotify is running locally.** Remote devices (TVs, computers) appear in the Spotify API without local Spotify running. `resolveLocalDevice()` must always call `ensureSpotifyRunning()` when the user picks "This device", regardless of `deviceVerified`.
23. **Don't save the resolved Spotify device ID as the preferred device when the user picks "This device".** The real device ID changes between Spotify sessions (process restarts). Save `LOCAL_DEVICE_ID` instead so the preference survives cold starts. The `resolveLocalDevice()` function resolves the real ID at play time.
24. **Don't use a short timeout after launching Spotify from a killed state.** Cold-launching Spotify takes ~5-6s to register as a Connect device (process start + auth + server registration). Extend the polling deadline to 12s when the launch intent fallback fires.
21. **Don't use compounding retry layers for Spotify playback.** The old `playWithRetry(3) × attemptPlay 502 retries(3) × verification(5 polls)` compounded to 27.5s worst case. Use a single-pass flow: one `attemptPlay()` with a single 502 retry and 2 quick verification polls.
22. **Don't require preferred Spotify devices to be active.** An inactive but present device just needs a transfer. Only show the device picker when the preferred device is completely absent from the API response (device removed/renamed).
25. **Don't use synchronous `NativeBridge.fetch()` for .axe plugin HTTP requests.** Synchronous fetch blocks the WebView JS thread, preventing concurrent plugin execution (e.g., Bandcamp search blocks while YouTube fetch hangs). Use `NativeBridge.fetchAsync()` with a callback pattern instead — HTTP runs on `Dispatchers.IO`, result injected back via `evaluateJavascript()`.
26. **Don't use JS template literals (backticks) to pass .axe JSON to `evaluateJavascript`.** Backticks and `$` in the embedded JS code break the template string. Use Base64-encoding: `atob('${base64}')` in JS to decode.
27. **Don't use async IIFE with `evaluateJavascript`.** It returns a `Promise` object (`{}`) not the resolved value. Use a callback pattern: store result in `window.__lastPluginResult`, then read it in a second `evaluate()` call.
28. **Don't manage audio focus explicitly during external playback.** Chromium's WebView has its own `AudioFocusDelegate`. Our explicit `requestAudioFocus()` competed with it, causing `AUDIOFOCUS_LOSS` → we paused MusicKit → stopped playback mid-song. Let Chromium handle it for WebView audio; Spotify handles its own.
29. **Don't use `catch (e: Exception)` in coroutine loops that should be cancellable.** `CancellationException` extends `Exception` in Kotlin. A generic catch swallows it, letting the loop continue through every iteration after cancellation (20 phantom "failed" logs in microseconds). Always `catch (e: CancellationException) { throw e }` before the generic catch, or use `catch (e: Exception) { if (e is CancellationException) throw e; ... }`.
30. **Don't call `transferPlayback()` before `startPlayback()` for Spotify.** The separate transfer activates the device, which causes Spotify to auto-resume its last track for ~270ms before our `startPlayback` replaces it. `startPlayback` with `device_id` activates inactive devices automatically — the transfer is redundant and causes an audible blip.
31. **Don't fire `startPlayback` with a cached Spotify device ID without verifying it first.** Spotify device IDs change when Spotify's process restarts. Call `getDevices()` first, check the cached ID is still in the list. If stale, clear `lastResolvedLocalId` and `deviceVerified` and fall through to the normal wake flow. Without this, stale IDs return 404 and the full flow (including the visible launch intent) fires needlessly.
32. **Don't create per-API OkHttpClients without the User-Agent interceptor.** The shared `OkHttpClient` in `AndroidModule.kt` has a global `User-Agent: Parachord/<version>` interceptor. MusicBrainz specifically blocks the default `okhttp/4.x.y` UA with HTTP 403. If you need a per-API client (e.g., different timeout), use `client.newBuilder().addInterceptor(...)` to inherit the UA interceptor.
33. **Don't use JS `if` statements inside `MusicKitWebBridge.evaluate()`.** The `evaluate()` wrapper wraps the script as `var p = $script;` — an `if` statement is not a valid JS expression. Use IIFE: `(function() { if (...) ...; return true; })()` or the Base64 approach from PluginManager.
34. **Don't use nested `hapticClickable` / `clickable` modifiers in LazyColumn items.** When the list updates progressively (e.g., Fresh Drops emitting new results every 5 artists), closures in LazyColumn items can fire with stale-captured data, navigating to the wrong item. Use a single click target per row.
35. **Don't call `MutableList.removeFirst()` / `removeLast()`.** JDK 21 added native `List.removeFirst()` methods, and Kotlin 2.0+ compiles the call to the JVM method instead of the old stdlib extension. Android's runtime (API ≤ 35) has no such method, so the call throws `NoSuchMethodError` at runtime. Use `removeAt(0)` / `removeAt(lastIndex)` instead. Unit tests catch this on JDK 17 too — the same bytecode resolves through the JDK interface.
36. **Unit tests need `testOptions.unitTests.isReturnDefaultValues = true`.** Otherwise any production code that calls `Log.d`, `Uri.parse`, or any other `android.*` method from under test throws `RuntimeException: Method ... not mocked`. This is set in `app/build.gradle.kts` — don't remove it. If you legitimately need an Android stub to return something other than the default, mock the specific call in that test.
37. **Don't bind a class with a defaulted or lambda constructor parameter with `singleOf(::X)`.** `singleOf` resolves every constructor parameter from the graph and ignores Kotlin defaults, so a missing type (a `() -> Long` clock, an optional cache) crashes the app at launch (bc47c23, #305). Use an explicit `single { X(get(), …) }` so the default applies. `KoinModuleVerifyTest` builds the real graph and resolves every definition, so it fails on this; keep it passing. It also fails on a **dependency cycle** (A needs B needs … needs A, which Koin resolves until the stack overflows): #500 made `OAuthManager` → `SoundCloudAuth` → `HttpClient` → `OAuthTokenRefresher` → `OAuthManager`, and every Android launch crashed (#503). Break a cycle with a `Lazy<X>` constructor parameter (`lazy { get<X>() }` in the binding) on the side that only needs X after startup.
38. **Don't rely on `MediaSession.Callback.onDisconnected` to know the car left.** Media3 1.5.1 never calls it for a legacy `MediaBrowserCompat` connection, which Android Auto may use, so a controller tracked from `onConnect` can outlive the car. Check Android Auto's own car-connection provider at the moment it matters (`CarConnections.confirmed`), and let it only veto.
39. **Don't drop a pending auto-skip on a request that plays nothing.** The 600 ms skip past an unplayable track (`scheduleAutoSkip`) is dropped only when another play began (`playsBegun`) or is starting, or the track changed. Dropping it on a request that started nothing (Previous restarting the track) leaves the queue stopped on a track that can't play, with the play-start foreground still held.
40. **Don't let Media3 demote between two tracks, and don't drop a held foreground before a local play from the background.** Media3 takes the service out of the foreground at a local track's `STATE_ENDED`; nothing started afterwards by the queue can take it back with the app not visible (#614). Keep it at the end (`foregroundKeeper` → `keepForegroundAtLocalTrackEnd`), and hand it to Media3 once ExoPlayer plays (no `ACTION_EXTERNAL_PLAYBACK_STOP` first). See "A local track's end keeps the foreground".
41. **Route the session's play and pause by the controller's state too.** `ExternalPlaybackForwardingPlayer.externalMode` arrives by an intent and lags the controller. Routing play/pause to `togglePlayPause` on `externalMode` alone sent the controller's own `ctrl.play()` (a local track after an external one) back to `togglePlayPause`, whose local branch called `ctrl.play()` again, synchronously on the main thread: an ANR on a Pixel 9a (Nov 2026). `sessionPlayPauseGoesToController(externalMode, controller.isExternalPlaybackActive)`, plus a re-entrancy backstop, as `ownsSessionSeek` does for seeks (#607).

### iOS-Specific

6. **Don't use SwiftUI `.tint()` or `.accentColor()` globally.** These override with system blue. Set explicit purple on each interactive element.
7. **Don't forget `AVAudioSession` configuration.** Call `setCategory(.playback)` before any `AVPlayer.play()` — otherwise audio won't play when the app is backgrounded or the device is silenced.
8. **Don't use `NavigationView`.** It's deprecated. Use `NavigationStack` (iOS 16+).
9. **Don't percent-encode URLs manually.** Use `URL(string:)` which handles encoding. For query parameters, use `URLComponents` with `URLQueryItem`.
10. **Don't block the main actor.** Network calls and metadata lookups must run in a `Task` or detached context. Use `@MainActor` only for UI state updates.
11. **Don't use `Timer` for playback progress.** Use `AVPlayer.addPeriodicTimeObserver(forInterval:queue:using:)` instead — it's synchronized with the audio clock.
12. **Don't forget to handle Spotify SDK auth flow.** The iOS SDK requires handling the redirect URL in `SceneDelegate.scene(_:openURLContexts:)` or via `.onOpenURL` in SwiftUI.

## Deep Link Handling

### Spotify Branch.io Referrer Format

Spotify's link sharing wraps real URLs in a Branch.io referrer: `spotify://open?_branch_referrer=<gzip+base64 data>`. The compressed payload contains `$full_url=https://open.spotify.com/artist/xxx`. `DeepLinkHandler.parseSpotifyUri()` detects this format, decompresses via `GZIPInputStream(Base64.decode(...))`, extracts the `$full_url` parameter, and routes it through `parseSpotifyUrl()`.

### Spotify URL Resolution

Spotify URLs contain opaque IDs (`/artist/4Z8W4fKeB5YxbusRwVAqVK`) with no human-readable name. `ExternalLinkResolver.resolveSpotifyArtist()` calls `GET /v1/artists/{id}` to get the name, then navigates to the artist page. Requires a valid Spotify access token — if missing, shows a helpful error directing the user to connect Spotify in Settings.

## AI Provider Integration Learnings

### Timeouts & Reliability

- **AI generation needs long timeouts.** LLM responses routinely take 30–60 seconds. Create a separate `OkHttpClient` (or `URLSession` config) with 60s read timeout for AI calls. The default 10–15s timeout will fail constantly.
- **Always surface AI errors to the user.** Don't silently swallow failures — show a toast/snackbar so users know something went wrong rather than staring at an empty screen.
- **The chat's error wording is desktop's (`AiProviderErrors.describe`, a port of `ai-provider-errors.js`'s `describeProviderError`).** Connection, then quota ("quota" / "exceeded") before a plain 429, then a bad key, a missing model, a content-policy block, else the provider's message cut to 100 characters. The order matters: OpenAI answers a key with no credit with a 429 `insufficient_quota` ("You exceeded your current quota…"), which the old mapping showed as "Rate limit exceeded. Please wait…". A Ktor timeout ("timeout has expired") reads as the connection message. Each failure is logged at warn level (provider, HTTP status from `AiProviderHttpException`, the provider's message), and every message is run through `AiProviderErrors.redactSecrets` (the shared `SecretRedaction`) first, since a timeout's message carries the URL. The three providers send their key in a header (Gemini's `x-goog-api-key`, not `?key=`), their POSTs are never retried after a connection failure (`retry { noRetry() }`, a billable request), a tool that throws answers the model with an error result, and a failed tool round is shown and kept in history, as on desktop. Desktop's plug-in `describeError` hook isn't wired on mobile yet (`describe`'s `pluginDescription` is there for it).

### JSON Parsing from AI Providers

- **AI models wrap JSON in preamble text.** Even when asked for JSON, models often add "Here's the JSON:" or markdown fences before the actual JSON. Use a brace-depth-tracking extractor (`extractJsonObject()`) to find the JSON within the response — don't assume the entire response is valid JSON.
- **Use API-level JSON mode when available.** ChatGPT supports `response_format: { "type": "json_object" }`. Gemini supports `responseMimeType: "application/json"`. These dramatically improve reliability.
- **Claude has no native JSON mode.** Use the prefill trick: set the first assistant character to `{` so Claude continues the JSON object directly. Then reconstruct: `"{" + response.content`.
- **Always use `ignoreUnknownKeys = true`** when parsing AI-generated JSON. Models may add extra fields.

### Tool Message Ordering (DJ Chat)

- **ChatGPT requires strict tool message ordering:** `[ASSISTANT(toolCalls), TOOL(result1), TOOL(result2), ...]`. Tool result messages must immediately follow the assistant message that requested them, paired by `tool_call_id`.
- **History pruning can orphan tool messages.** When trimming old messages, never split an ASSISTANT+toolCalls message from its TOOL result messages. Walk backward to keep these groups intact. Add a `sanitizeHistory()` pass to remove any orphaned TOOL messages.
- **Claude expects tool results as user messages** with `tool_result` content blocks and `tool_use_id` fields.

### AI Suggestions Caching (Stale-While-Revalidate)

- **AI suggestions must load stale cache first, then refresh.** Like the desktop, `AiRecommendationService` persists recommendations to `ai_suggestions_cache.json` so cold starts show previous results immediately instead of a 30-60s shimmer wait. The disk cache is lazy-loaded on first access to `cachedRecommendations`, and saved after each successful AI fetch.
- **No TTL — always refresh.** Unlike Fresh Drops (6h TTL) or Critical Darlings (4h TTL), AI suggestions always fetch fresh results but display stale cache during the wait. This matches desktop's pattern where cached albums/artists stay visible while `loading: true`.
- **`AiAlbumSuggestion` and `AiArtistSuggestion` are `@Serializable`.** Required for disk cache persistence.

### Prompt Engineering for Music Recommendations

- **AI models recommend genre names as track titles** if not explicitly told otherwise. Prompts must say "recommend real, specific songs by real artists — not genre names or descriptions."
- **Always use the user's configured AI provider** (from settings), not a hardcoded default. Check `settingsStore.selectedChatPlugin`.
- **Including listening history context** (recently played tracks, top artists) significantly improves recommendation quality. Make this opt-in with a user toggle for privacy.

### Key AI Files

The full AI surface is in `shared/.../ai/` (April 2026). App-side files in `app/.../ai/` are typealias shims.

| Area | Shared file | App shim |
